&+\underbrace{(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm})\cdot\nabla z^{\mp}_{\ell}}_{\text{Gr{\"{o}}nwall}}+\underbrace{\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell}z^{\mp}_{\ell}+(z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z_{\ell}^{\pm})\cdot\nabla z^{\mp}_{\ell})}_{\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{2}\delta_{q}^{3/2}+\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}((\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{1/2})^{2}\lambda_{q}^{2}\delta_{q}^{1/2}}-\underbrace{(\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{\pm})\cdot\nabla z^{\mp}_{\ell}}_{\ell^{-2\alpha}\lambda_{q}^{r}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{1/2}(\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2})^{2}}\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}T_{3}&=\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left[\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell}R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}+(z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z^{\pm}_{\ell})\cdot\nabla R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}\right]}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}+\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{2m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}}\\
&-\underbrace{(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\mp})\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}}-\underbrace{\Delta^{\mp}\cdot\nabla(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell}z^{\pm}_{\ell}+(z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z_{\ell}^{\pm})\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell})}_{\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{2}\delta_{q}^{3/2}+\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}((\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{1/2})^{2}\lambda_{q}^{2}\delta_{q}^{1/2}}+\underbrace{(\Delta^{\mp}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{\pm})\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell}}_{\ell^{-2\alpha}\lambda_{q}^{r}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{1/2}(\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2})^{2}}\end{split}

and from the bounds in (
2.18
), Lemma (
2.1
) and the definition of
\tau^{c}
(
1.21
), we conclude:

\begin{split}||\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}\ T_{2}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{\alpha}\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-m_{0}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\\
&+\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-m_{0}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q},\\
||\mathbb{P}T_{3}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-m_{0}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}\end{split}

where, in addition to the observations above, we used that
m_{0}>1
. We are now ready to do a second transport
\&
Grönwall argument. Gathering the bounds, we deduce:

(2.19)

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||T_{1}||_{r+\alpha}+||\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}\ T_{2}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathbb{P}T_{3}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{\alpha}\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-m_{0}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\\
&+\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-m_{0}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q},\\
\end{split}

we now apply Proposition
B.4
, with

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}|_{t=t_{j}}&=\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}|_{t=t_{j}}+2B_{\ell,j}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{\pm}|_{t=t_{j}}\\
&=\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}|_{t=t_{j}}+2B_{\ell,j}(\cdot,t_{j})\cdot\nabla[\underbrace{(z^{\pm}_{\ell}(\cdot,t_{j})-z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}(\cdot,t_{j})}_{\equiv 0}]\\
&=\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}|_{t=t_{j}}\end{split}

and deduce from (
2.19
) the bounds:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{\alpha}&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{\alpha}+\int_{t_{j}}^{t}[\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}(\cdot,s)]_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}+\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\int_{t_{j}}^{t}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{\alpha}(\cdot,s)\mathrm{d}s,\\
||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}+|t-t_{j}|[z^{\mp}_{\ell,j}]_{r+\alpha}[\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}]_{1}\\
&+\int_{t_{j}}^{t}[\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}(\cdot,s)]_{r+\alpha}+(s-t_{j})[z^{\mp}_{\ell,j}]_{r+\alpha}[\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}(\cdot,s)]_{1}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-m_{0}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}\\
&+\int_{t_{j}}^{t}(||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}(\cdot,s)||_{\alpha}\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-m_{0}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}(\cdot,s)||_{r+\alpha}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2})\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\int_{t_{j}}^{t}(||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}(\cdot,s)||_{\alpha}\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}(\cdot,s)||_{1+\alpha}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2})\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

where we used
M-m_{0}-2\geq 0
. Now we conclude as before, we use Grönwall Lemma on the first estimate, plug the estimate in the second one for
r=1
, plug the result in the same estimate but for
r>1
and we conclude that:

(2.20)

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(M-m_{0}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}\end{split}

where the implicit constant depends on
r,\ \alpha,\ C_{0}
. The stated estimates then follow from unwinding the definitions, namely

v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}=\frac{\Delta^{+}+\Delta^{-}}{2}\ \text{ and }\ B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j}=\frac{\Delta^{+}-\Delta^{-}}{2}.

Pressure estimates.
To prove bounds on
\nabla\Delta^{p}
we use that
\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}
is a CZ-type operator together with the result in Proposition
B.7
and the bounds we just proved. Indeed from (
2.16
) and the bounds in Lemma
2.1
and (
2.18
) we have:

\begin{split}||\nabla\Delta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}||_{r+1+\alpha}+||z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}||_{r+1+\alpha}||\Delta^{\pm}||_{0}+||z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}||_{1}||\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}+||z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{r+1+\alpha}||\Delta^{\mp}||_{0}+||z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{1}||\Delta^{\mp}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(M-m_{0}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}\end{split}

and with the help of the commutator estimates in Proposition
B.7
, the bounds in Lemma
2.1
and (
2.18
), (
2.20
), we deduce:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\nabla\Delta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||[z_{\ell,j},\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}][\mathrm{div}[\ R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}]-(\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}+\Delta^{\mp}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell}^{\pm})]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}[\mathrm{div}[\ R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}]-(\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}+\Delta^{\mp}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell}^{\pm})]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\underbrace{||z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}||_{r+1+\alpha}(||\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}||_{\alpha}+||\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell}^{\mp}||_{\alpha}+||\mathrm{div}\ R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}||_{\alpha})}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-2\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}}\\
&+||z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}||_{1+\alpha}(||\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}||_{r+\alpha}+||\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla z_{\ell}^{\mp}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathrm{div}\ R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}||_{r+\alpha})\\
&+\underbrace{||(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm})\cdot\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-2\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}}+\underbrace{||\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}}+\underbrace{||(\Delta^{\pm}\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{\mp}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-2\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}}\\
&+||(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\mp})\cdot\nabla z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}+||\Delta^{\mp}\cdot\nabla[(z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z^{\pm}_{\ell})\cdot\nabla z_{\ell}^{\pm}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell}^{\pm}z_{\ell}^{\pm}]||_{r+\alpha}+||(\Delta^{\mp}\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla z_{\ell}^{\mp}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+\underbrace{||\mathrm{div}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell}R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}||_{r+\alpha}+||[z^{\pm}_{\ell}\cdot\nabla,\mathrm{div}]R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}}+\underbrace{||(z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z^{\pm}_{\ell})\cdot\nabla\mathrm{div}\ R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{2m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(M-m_{0}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}\end{split}

and we conclude:

||\nabla\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\nabla\Delta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}+||(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}\nabla\Delta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(M-m_{0}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}

where the implicit constant depends on
r,\ \alpha,\ C_{0}
.

Pure time derivatives.
We write the first time derivative as:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\Delta^{\pm}=\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\Delta^{\pm}-z^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{\pm}\end{split}

and we deduce from the bounds shown above that:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(M-m_{0}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{c}\delta_{q}.\\
\end{split}

We read the second-order pure time derivative bound simply by applying
\partial_{t}
to equation (
2.15
):

\begin{split}\partial_{t}^{2}\Delta^{\pm}=-\mathbb{P}\left[(\partial_{t}z^{\mp}_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla\Delta^{\pm}+z^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\cdot\nabla(\partial_{t}\Delta^{\pm})+(\partial_{t}\Delta^{\mp})\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell}+\Delta^{\mp}\cdot\nabla(\partial_{t}z^{\pm}_{\ell})\right]+\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}\ [\partial_{t}R_{\ell}^{c,\pm}]\end{split}

and the first-order bound above, the ones in (
2.18
) and Lemma
2.1
, then give:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{2}\Delta^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+3}\lambda_{q}^{[r+2-(M-m_{0}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{c}\delta_{q}.\end{split}

The implicit constants in the above statements depend on
r,\ \alpha,\ C_{0}
.
∎

Remark 3

(A Useful Identity)
.

Let

\Delta^{v}=v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j},\ \ \ \ \Delta^{B}=B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j},\ \ \ \ \Delta^{p}=p_{\ell}-p_{\ell,j},

we now rewrite

\begin{split}\mathrm{div}\ R_{\ell}^{c}&=\partial_{t}\Delta^{v}+\mathrm{div}\left[\left[v_{\ell}\otimes v_{\ell}-B_{\ell}\otimes B_{\ell}\right]-\left[v_{\ell,j}\otimes v_{\ell,j}-B_{\ell,j}\otimes B_{\ell,j}\right]\right]+\nabla\Delta^{p}\\
&=\partial_{t}\Delta^{v}+\mathrm{div}\left[\left[v_{\ell}\otimes\Delta^{v}+\Delta^{v}\otimes v_{\ell,j}\right]-\left[B_{\ell}\otimes\Delta^{B}+\Delta^{B}\otimes B_{\ell,j}\right]\right]+\nabla\Delta^{p}\\
&=\partial_{t}\Delta^{v}+\mathrm{div}\left[\Delta^{v}\otimes\Delta^{v}-\Delta^{B}\otimes\Delta^{B}+\left[\Delta^{v}\otimes v_{\ell,j}\right]^{sym}-\left[\Delta^{B}\otimes B_{\ell,j}\right]^{sym}\right]\\
&+\nabla\Delta^{p}\\
\end{split}

and we conclude that:

(2.21)

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Delta^{v}-\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Delta^{B}&=\mathrm{div}\ R_{\ell}^{c}-2\left(\Delta^{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}-\Delta^{B}\cdot\nabla B_{\ell,j}\right)\\
&-\mathrm{div}\left[\Delta^{v}\otimes\Delta^{v}-\Delta^{B}\otimes\Delta^{B}\right]-\nabla\Delta^{p}\\
\end{split}

This will be useful later when computing the transport part of the new Reynolds stress.

We now collect the work done in this Subsection.

Lemma 2.4

(Local Correction)
.

Let
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
r\geq 0
an integer and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-1
. The following bounds hold:

(2.22a)

\displaystyle||v_{\ell,j}||_{0},\ ||B_{\ell,j}||_{0}\leq C_{0}\ \text{ and }|B_{\ell,j}|\geq c_{0}\ \text{ everywhere on}\ \mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R},

(2.22b)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}v_{\ell,j}||_{r},\ ||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}B_{\ell,j}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ \sigma=0,1,2\ \&\ (\sigma,r)\neq(0,0),

(2.22c)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}v_{\ell,j}||_{r},\ ||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}B_{\ell,j}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q},

(2.22d)

\displaystyle||p_{\ell,j}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1,

(2.22e)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}p_{\ell,j}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1.

The implicit constants depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ \alpha
.

The bounds follow immediately from writing:

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}^{\sigma}v_{\ell,j}=\begin{cases}(v_{\ell,j}-v_{\ell})+(v_{\ell}-v_{q})+v_{q}&\text{ for }r=\sigma=0,\\
\partial_{t}^{\sigma}(v_{\ell,j}-v_{\ell})+\partial_{t}^{\sigma}v_{\ell}&\text{ else }\\
\end{cases}\\
&\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}v_{\ell,j}=\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}(v_{\ell,j}-v_{\ell})+\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell}v_{\ell}+(v_{\ell,j}-v_{\ell})\cdot\nabla v_{\ell},\end{split}

together with the estimates in Lemmas
2.1
,
2.3
, and the fact that
(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-2\alpha}\leq 1
, see (
1.24
). Similar decompositions hold for
B_{\ell,j}
and
p_{\ell,j}
.

2.2.
Geometric Constructions

With the help of Proposition
A.1
and the explicit construction afterwards, see the definitions and notation there, we build a minimal covering
\mathcal{C}=\{U_{j^{\prime}}\}_{j^{\prime}}
of
\mathbb{T}^{3}
with disjoint families
\{\mathcal{F}_{l}\}
for
l=1,\dots,4
and an associated partition of unity
\{\theta_{j^{\prime}}\}_{j^{\prime}}
with diameter
4/3\tau^{c}
and granularity
1/3\tau^{c}
. Namely, for each
U_{j^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{C}
we have
\theta_{j^{\prime}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(U_{j^{\prime}},[0,1])
satisfying:

•

\sum_{j^{\prime}}\theta_{j^{\prime}}^{2}\equiv 1
and for each point in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
at most four
\theta_{j^{\prime}}
are non-zero.

•

For each
j^{\prime}
there exists
x_{j^{\prime}}
such that
U_{j^{\prime}}\subset B_{2/3\tau^{c}}(x_{j^{\prime}})
and

U_{j^{\prime}_{1}},U_{j^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathcal{F}_{l}\Longrightarrow\text{dist}(U_{j^{\prime}_{1}},\ U_{j^{\prime}_{2}})>1/3\tau^{c}.

•

||\theta_{j^{\prime}}||_{r}\lesssim(\tau^{c})^{-r}
where the implicit constant depends on
r
and the specific choice of the partition of unity.

Now set
J=(j,j^{\prime})
and define:

(2.23)

\chi_{J}=\chi_{j,j^{\prime}}=\eta_{j}(t)\theta_{j^{\prime}}(x)\ \text{ and }\ Q_{J}=B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{j^{\prime}})\times B_{\tau^{c}}(t_{j}),

where
\{\eta_{j}\}_{j}
was constructed in Subsection
2.1
, note that
\{\chi_{J}^{2}\}_{J}
is now a space-time partition of unity, in the sense that:

\sum_{J}\chi_{J}^{2}\equiv 1\ \ \ \text{ everywhere on }\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}

and additionally satisfies:

(2.24)

\text{supp}_{x,t}\chi_{J}\subset B_{2/3\tau^{c}}(x_{j})\times B_{2/3\tau^{c}}(t_{j}),\ \ \ ||\partial_{t}^{r^{\prime}}\chi_{J}||_{r}\lesssim(\tau^{c})^{-(r+r^{\prime})}.

We proceed by decomposing matrices close to the identity into simple tensors satisfying an orthogonality constraint, which will play a key role in the Nash stage when the fast coefficients are added (see the introductory Subsection
1.3
).

Lemma 2.5

(Geometric Decomposition with Constraint)
.

Any matrix
M\in B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})
can be written as:

(2.25)

M=\sum_{\zeta\in\Lambda}\gamma_{\zeta}^{2}(M)\zeta\otimes\zeta

where
\Lambda
is a set of 6 unit vectors and

\gamma_{\zeta}:B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})\to\mathbb{R}

are smooth functions. Moreover, each
\zeta\in\Lambda
is associated with an oriented orthonormal basis
(k,\ \nu,\ \zeta)
, where
k
satisfies
k\cdot e_{3}=0
.

This decomposition is a standard tool of convex integration; see, for example
[
6
, Appendix B]
, for a proof and an account of its development. This gives the claim for a collection
\Lambda
of six unit vectors
\zeta
. The fact that we can extend these to ordered orthonormal bases
(k,\ \nu,\ \zeta)
with
k\cdot e_{3}=0
follows from the fact that the intersection of planes
e_{3}^{\perp}\cap\zeta^{\perp}
contains at least a line spanned by a unit vector
k
. We can finally set
\nu=\zeta\times k
.

Note that the commutation:

\partial_{t}B_{\ell,j}+\mathrm{curl}\left[B_{\ell,j}\times v_{\ell,j}\right]=0\leadsto\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}=\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}

coming from (
2.6
) (see the explicit computation in (
6.9
)), together with the bounds in Lemma
2.4
, ensures that the assumptions of Lemma
7.1
are satisfied. This Lemma constructs for us charts
\Psi_{J}:Q_{J}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
adapted to
(v_{\ell,j},\ B_{\ell,j})
. We summarise their properties in the following Lemma, which is just a rephrasing of
7.1
under the above notation. We refer the reader to Appendix
A
, where we introduce and motivate the notational conventions for the differential-geometric objects.

Lemma 2.6

(Adapted Charts)
.

Let
J=(j,j^{\prime})
a chart label as in (
2.23
) and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-2
. For any fixed
t\in B_{\tau^{c}}(t_{j})
the map:

\Psi_{J}(\cdot,t):B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{j^{\prime}})\to\mathbb{R}^{3}

is a diffeomorphism onto its image, with estimates:

\begin{split}&||\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0},||(\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J})^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0},||\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}]-1||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},\\
&||\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}||_{r},||(\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J})^{-1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ \text{ for }r\geq 1\end{split}

where the Hölder norms are understood to be taken on
Q_{J}
and the implicit constants depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
.

Moreover, let
\zeta
be a fixed unit vector, which we think of as a 2-form. We have:

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta=0\ \text{ and }\ \mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta=0.

Decomposition of the Reynolds stress.
With the space-time charts and the geometric decomposition of matrices close to the identity, we are ready to rewrite the mollified Reynolds stress
R_{\ell}
. Using the matrix decomposition (
2.25
) we compute:

\begin{split}(\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J})^{-1}M(\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J})^{-T}=\sum_{\zeta\in\Lambda}\gamma_{\zeta}^{2}(M)(\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J})^{-1}\zeta\otimes(\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J})^{-1}\zeta=\sum_{\zeta\in\Lambda}\gamma_{\zeta}^{2}(M)\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}]^{-2}\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta\otimes\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta\\
\end{split}

and we deduce:

M=\sum_{\zeta\in\Lambda}\gamma_{\zeta}^{2}\left(\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}M\mathrm{D}\Psi^{T}_{J}\right)\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta\otimes\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta

as soon as the composition with
\gamma_{\zeta}
is well defined. This is indeed the case. Computations as in the proof of Lemma
3.7
show that for
M=\mathrm{Id}-\frac{R_{\ell}}{\delta_{q+1}}
we have:

||\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}M\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}^{T}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{-\alpha}

and in particular, for any given
\alpha,\ b
we can choose
a
large enough so that:

\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}M\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}^{T}\in B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})

and Lemma
2.5
can be applied. With this and the partition of unity in (
2.23
) at hand, we can write:

(2.26)

\begin{split}\delta_{q+1}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\frac{R_{\ell}}{\delta_{q+1}}\right)&=\sum_{J}\delta_{q+1}\chi_{J}^{2}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\frac{R_{\ell}}{\delta_{q+1}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{J}\sum_{\zeta\in\Lambda}\delta_{q+1}\chi_{J}^{2}\gamma_{\zeta}^{2}\left(\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi]^{2}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}\left(\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}\right)\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}^{T}\right)\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta\otimes\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta\\
&=\sum_{J}\sum_{\zeta\in\Lambda}a_{I}^{2}\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta\otimes\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta\\
&=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}}A_{I}\end{split}

where we defined

I=(\zeta,J)=(I_{\zeta},I_{t},I_{x})=(\zeta,j,j^{\prime})

and
\mathcal{I}
to be the finite set containing all the possible labels and
A_{I}
the tensor associated with the index
I
having coefficients
a_{I}
, namely

(2.27)

\begin{split}a_{I}&=\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\chi_{J}\gamma_{\zeta}\left(\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}\left(\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}\right)\mathrm{D}\Psi_{J}^{T}\right),\\
A_{I}&=A_{\zeta,J}=a_{I}^{2}\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta\otimes\Psi_{J}^{2*}\zeta.\\
\end{split}

Note that
\Psi_{j}^{2*}\zeta
is exactly the type of divergence-free vector field we constructed in Lemma
2.6
. We now provide estimates on the decomposition.

Lemma 2.7

(Estimates on the Decomposition)
.

Let
r\geq 0
an integer and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-2
. For any
I=(\zeta,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathcal{I}
we have:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}a_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[r+j-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,\\
&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}a_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}.\end{split}

Analogously, the tensor decomposition satisfies:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}A_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,\\
&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}A_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}.\end{split}

The implicit constants in both statements depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
.

Finally, we have the following support localisation property:

\text{supp}_{x,t}a_{I},\ \text{supp}_{x,t}A_{I}\Subset B_{2/3\tau^{c}}(x_{j^{\prime}})\times B_{2/3\tau^{c}}(t_{j})\cap\text{supp}_{x,t}R_{\ell}.

The proof of this Lemma is similar to the proof of Lemma
3.7
, and we omit it. Here, in contrast with
3.7
, we do not claim bounds for higher-order transport and pure time derivatives; this is why a preliminary mollification along the Alfvén directions, see Subsection
3.2
, is not necessary. This suffices, as we will use these objects only as the right-hand side of (
2.29
), and the transport properties of the solution will be guaranteed by the equation itself.

2.3.
Galbrun LDF

With the geometric constructions at hand, we proceed to the construction of the LDF. To better identify objects, we define the type map:

(2.28)

p:I\mapsto p(I)\in\Lambda\times\{e,o\}\times\{0,1,2,3\}

distinguishing if the index is associated with an even or odd time interval, the family in the space partition, and the direction in the matrix decomposition. This is important because we will assign disjoint time-oscillating profiles to different types. Note that if two indices have the same type, then the associated cut-offs are disjoint in space by construction of the minimal covering, even if they share the same time interval and direction.

Lemma 2.8

(Time Profiles)
.

There exists a collection of functions
\{g_{p}\}_{p}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(0,1)
indexed by

p\in\Lambda\times\{e,o\}\times\{0,1,2,3\}

such that:

\int_{0}^{1}g_{p}=0,\ \int_{0}^{1}g^{2}_{p}=1,\ \text{supp}\ g_{p}\cap\text{supp}\ g_{p^{\prime}}=\emptyset\text{ for }p\neq p^{\prime}.

We think of each
g_{p}
as a periodic function
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and define:

\begin{cases}\alpha_{p}(t)=g^{[1]}_{p}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}g_{p}(s)\mathrm{d}s,\\
f_{p}=(1-g_{p}^{2}).\end{cases}

Additionally, the collection
\{g_{p}\}
can be chosen so that the resulting
\{\alpha_{p}\}_{p}
satisfy for
p\neq p^{\prime}
:

\text{supp}\ \alpha_{p}\cap\text{supp}\ \alpha_{p^{\prime}}=\emptyset,\ \text{supp}\ \alpha_{p}\cap\text{supp}\ g_{p^{\prime}}=\emptyset.

The proof of the Lemma is a quick adaptation of
[
24
, Lemma 3.3]
, and we omit it. Without renaming, we rescale each profile by the parameter
\tau^{a}
in (
1.22
), namely

g_{p}=g_{p}(t/\tau^{a}),\ \ \ f_{p}=f_{p}(t/\tau^{a}),\ \ \ \alpha_{p}(t)=\alpha_{p}(t/\tau^{a}).

As planned, we now engineer the LDF
\xi^{g}
so that the leading order of the associated perturbation is a solution of Galbrun’s equation with right-hand side

\mathrm{div}\sum_{I}f_{p(I)}\mathbb{T}A_{I},

we construct the potential of
\xi^{g}
and work locally in time, that is, on
(t_{j}-\tau^{c},t_{j}+\tau^{c})
. We invite the reader to visit Section
6
before proceeding.

According to the results in Subsection
6.1
, this corresponds to solving on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{j}-\tau^{c},t_{j}+\tau^{c})
the problem:

(2.29)

\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g},\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]=\sum_{I:I_{t}=j}f_{p(I)}\mathbb{T}A_{I},\\
\Theta_{j}^{g}|_{t=t_{j}}=0,\\
\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}|_{t=t_{j}}=\tau^{a}\sum_{I:I_{t}=j}f_{p(I)}^{[1]}\mathbb{T}A_{I}|_{t=t_{j}}\\
\end{cases}

where the initial data is chosen to simplify the a priori estimates from Subsection
6.3
, but plays no role here. Since
\text{supp}\ \tilde{\eta}_{j}\subset(t_{j}-\tau^{c},t_{j}+\tau^{c})
, we can cut off the construction in time and define:

\xi_{j}^{g}=\mathrm{curl}[\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]=\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathrm{curl}[\Theta_{j}^{g}].

We now compute the cut-off error, since

\tilde{\eta}_{j}\equiv 1\ \text{ on }\ (t_{j}-2/3\tau^{c},t_{j}+2/3\tau^{c})\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \text{supp}_{t}A_{I}\subset\text{supp}\ \eta_{j}\subset(t_{j}-2/3\tau^{c},t_{j}+2/3\tau^{c}),

see (
2.27
), (
2.1
), we get from (
2.29
):

\begin{split}\sum_{I:I_{t}=j}f_{p(I)}\mathbb{T}A_{I}&=\tilde{\eta}_{j}\sum_{I:I_{t}=j}f_{p(I)}\mathbb{T}A_{I}\\
&=\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]+\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g},\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]\\
&=\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}(\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g})+\mathbb{H}_{1}[\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}(\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}),\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}(\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g})]\\
&-(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g})-(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}-\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathbb{H}_{2}[\Theta_{j}^{g},\Theta_{j}^{g}].\\
\end{split}

We now set for
\mathcal{R}
as in (
B.22
):

(2.30)

R^{cut}_{j}=\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g})+(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathbb{H}_{2}[\Theta_{j}^{g},\Theta_{j}^{g}]\right]\\

and define, according to (
6.5
), the principal part of the Galbrun perturbation to be:

w_{j}^{g,p}=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\xi_{j}^{g},\ \ \ \ b_{j}^{g,p}=\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\xi_{j}^{g},

where
p
stands for principal.

We now unwind the rewriting done in Subsection
6.1
. We take
\mathrm{curl}
of (
2.29
) and solve for the associated Poisson equation to recover
\pi^{g}_{j}
and remove the Leray projectors. We obtain that
(w_{j}^{g,p},\ b_{j}^{g,p},\ \pi^{g}_{j})
is a solution of:

\partial_{t}w^{g,p}_{j}+v_{\ell,j}\cdot\nabla w^{g,p}-B_{\ell,j}\cdot\nabla b^{g,p}_{j}+w^{g,p}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}-b^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla B_{\ell,j}+\nabla\pi^{gr}_{j}=\mathrm{div}\left[\sum_{I:I_{t}=j}f_{p(I)}A_{I}+R_{j}^{cut}\right],\\

with associated Poisson problem:

(2.31)

\begin{cases}\Delta\pi^{gr}_{j}=-2\mathrm{div}\left[w^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}-b^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla B_{\ell,j}\right]+\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div}\left[\sum_{I:I_{t}=j}f_{p(I)}\mathrm{div}A_{I}\right],\\
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\pi^{g}_{j}=0.\end{cases}

and we finally collect all the constructed LDFs, pressure terms and cut-off errors:

\xi^{g}=\sum_{j}\xi^{g}_{j},\ (w^{g,p},\ b^{g,p},\ \pi^{g})=\sum_{j}(w^{g,p}_{j},\ b^{g,p}_{j},\ \pi_{j}^{g}),\ R^{cut}=\sum_{j}R_{j}^{cut},

note that the pressure is also localised in time, as the rhs of (
2.31
) is and abusing notation we also redefine:

\Theta^{g}_{j}:=\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta^{g}_{j}\leadsto\xi^{g}_{j}=\mathrm{curl}[\Theta^{g}_{j}].

The work done in Subsection
6.3
allows us to conclude that the following estimates hold.

Lemma 2.9

(Galbrun LDF)
.

Let
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
r\geq 0
be an integer and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-4
. We have:

(2.32a)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ \sigma=0,1,2,

(2.32b)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ \sigma=0,1,2,

(2.32c)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{\mp}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{2}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}.

In particular,

(2.33a)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}w_{j}^{g,p}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}b_{j}^{g,p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ \sigma=0,1,2,

(2.33b)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}w_{j}^{g,p}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}b_{j}^{g,p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1},

(2.33c)

\displaystyle||\pi^{gr}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1,

(2.33d)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\pi^{gr}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1.

The implicit constants depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
.

Remark 4

(Good Derivatives Loss)
.

The equation loses two derivatives on the ones we have on the data, namely
M-m_{0}-2
, see Lemma
2.7
, and there is an additional loss in passing to the vector fields because we have to move first from
\Theta_{j}^{g}
to
\xi_{j}^{g}
, and this loses one derivative.

Proof of Lemma
2.9
.

We want to apply Lemma
6.4
to get estimates on the solution
\Theta_{j}^{g}
of (
2.29
). To do so, we need to verify that the Standing Assumptions
1
are met. We notice immediately that the right-hand side of (
2.29
) is:

\sum_{I:I_{t}=j}f_{p(I)}\mathbb{T}A_{I},

which, strictly speaking, is not of the form
f\mathbb{T}F
; however, at each space-time point, by construction, at most one of the
f_{p(I)}\mathbb{T}A_{I}
is non-zero, and the proof of Lemma
6.4
can be adapted to this case. Indeed, the integration by parts in time, type of arguments in the proof of
6.4
would, in this case, involve finitely many time profiles, the number of which is fixed independently of
q
, namely
48
. The bounds on
(v_{\ell,j},\ B_{\ell,j},\ p_{\ell,j},\ R_{\ell})
can be found in Lemmas
2.1
and
2.4
. They solve the relaxed MHD equation by construction, see (
2.6
). The bounds on the right-hand side then follow from Lemma
2.7
. We set the parameter
\underline{r}
in the Standing Assumptions
1
to be
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-2
. The fact that we actually redefined
\Theta_{j}^{g}
to be
\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}
does not change the estimates, because of the bound in (
2.2
). We conclude that the bounds in (
2.32
) hold.

From
\Theta_{j}^{g}
to
(w_{j}^{g,p},\ b_{j}^{g,p})
.
We now use the definition of
\underline{r}
in the statement of the Lemma that is
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-4
. We can transfer the estimates from the potential to the vector field with the help of the following computation, which relies on (
A.12
):

\begin{split}w_{j}^{g,p}&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\xi_{j}^{g}\\
&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\mathrm{curl}[\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]\\
&=\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+1/2\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+(\mathrm{D}v^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}[\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]\right].\end{split}

Similarly, for
b_{j}^{g,p}
. From this commuting the time derivative with
\mathrm{curl}
, and the bounds in (
2.32
) we deduce:

||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}w_{j}^{g,p}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}b_{j}^{g,p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ \sigma=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ r\geq 0.

Note the loss of one good derivative. We use the same idea to deduce the Alfvén transport bound.

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}w_{j}^{g,p}&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}})w_{j}^{g,p}+w_{j}^{g,p}\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\\
&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}})\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+1/2\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+(\mathrm{D}v^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}[\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]\right]\\
&+w_{j}^{g,p}\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\\
&=\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+1/2\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+1/2(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j})\Theta_{j}^{g}\right]\\
&+\mathrm{curl}\left[(\mathrm{D}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}v^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}[\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]+(\mathrm{D}v^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]\right]\\
&+\mathrm{curl}\left[(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}\left[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+1/2\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+(\mathrm{D}v^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}[\tilde{\eta}_{j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]\right]\right]\\
&+w_{j}^{g,p}\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\\
\end{split}

Similarly, for
b_{j}^{g,p}
. We deduce from (
2.32
) the bound above and the estimates in Lemma
2.4
that:

(2.34)

||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}w_{j}^{g,p}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}b_{j}^{g,p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0.

Estimates on
\nabla\pi^{g}
.
We will use the bounds in (
2.34
) without mention. By definition, we have:

\begin{split}\nabla\pi^{gr}_{j}=-2\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}\left[w^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}-b^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla B_{\ell,j}\right]+\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div}\underbrace{\sum_{I:I_{t}=j}f_{p(I)}A_{I}}_{=:F}\\
\end{split}

We will now use that
\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}
is a CZ-type operator together with Proposition
B.7
to conclude estimates on
\nabla\pi^{gr}_{j}
. We have:

\begin{split}||\nabla\pi^{gr}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||v_{\ell,j}||_{r+1+\alpha}||w^{g,p}_{j}||_{0}+||v_{\ell,j}||_{1}||w^{g,p}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||B_{\ell,j}||_{r+1+\alpha}||b^{g,p}_{j}||_{0}+||B_{\ell,j}||_{1}||b^{g,p}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||F||_{1+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(M-m_{0}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\leq\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}.\\
\end{split}

By means of Lemmas
2.7
,
2.3
and Proposition
B.7
to deal with the commutators, we now bound:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\nabla\pi^{gr}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||[z_{\ell,j}^{\pm},\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}][-2w^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}+2b^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla B_{\ell,j}+\mathrm{div}F]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}[-2w^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}+2b^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla B_{\ell,j}+\mathrm{div}F]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\underbrace{||z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}||_{r+1+\alpha}(||w^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}||_{\alpha}+||\mathrm{div}F||_{\alpha})+||z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}||_{1+\alpha}(||w^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathrm{div}F||_{r+\alpha})}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+3}\ell^{-3\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-3\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}}\\
&+\underbrace{||(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}w^{g,p}_{j})\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+3}\ell^{-2\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}}+\underbrace{||w^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}v_{\ell,j}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+3}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}\delta_{q+1}}+\underbrace{||(w^{g,p}_{j}\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla v_{\ell,j}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+3}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}\delta_{q+1}}\\
&+\underbrace{||\mathrm{div}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}F||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\ell^{-\alpha}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1}}+\underbrace{||[z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\cdot\nabla,\mathrm{div}]F||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}}\\
&+\text{magnetic field terms}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1},\end{split}

and from the identity

\nabla\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\pi^{gr}_{j}=\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\nabla\pi^{gr}_{j}+(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}\nabla\pi^{gr}_{j},

we conclude that:

||\nabla\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\pi^{gr}_{j}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(r-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0.

The implicit constants in the above bounds depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
.
∎

Lemma 2.10

(Estimates on
R^{cut}
)
.

Let
r\geq 0
be an integer and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-4
. We have:

\begin{split}&||R^{cut}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1},\\
&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}R^{cut}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq N-m_{0}-1.\end{split}

The implicit constants depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
.

Remark 5
.

Note that with the current Iterative Assumptions (
1.16
), we are not able to guarantee the improved transport estimates as in
[
24
]
.

Proof of Lemma
2.10
.

We first use the definition of
\mathbb{H}_{2}
and
\mathbb{T}
given in (
6.11
) to rewrite
R^{cut}_{j}
as:

\begin{split}R^{cut}_{j}&=\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g})+(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}+\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathbb{H}_{2}[\Theta_{j}^{g},\Theta_{j}^{g}]\right]\\
&=\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g})+(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}\right]\\
&+\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\left[\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]+\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{+})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]+\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathbb{T}\left[\Theta_{j}^{g}\times\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{-}+\Theta_{j}^{g}\times\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{+}\right]^{\top}\right]\\
&=\underbrace{\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g})+(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}\right]}_{T_{1}}\\
&+\underbrace{\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\left[\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]+\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{+})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]\right]+\mathcal{R}\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}\left[\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\left[\Theta_{j}^{g}\times\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{-}+\Theta_{j}^{g}\times\nabla z_{\ell,j}^{+}\right]^{\top}\right]}_{T_{2}}.\\
\end{split}

Estimates on
T_{1}
.
Since
\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}
is a CZ-type operator, we can use Proposition
B.7
to deal with it. The bounds in Lemmas
2.4
,
2.9
and (
2.2
), then give:

\begin{split}||T_{1}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}+||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}+||(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}.\end{split}

Using in adddition the commutator estimates in Proposition
B.7
and the bounds in Lemmas
2.1
,
2.3
to correct the transport operator, we obtain:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}T_{1}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||[z_{\ell,j}^{\pm},\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}][(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g})+(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||(z_{q}^{\pm}-z_{\ell,j}^{\pm})\cdot\nabla\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}[(\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j})(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g})+(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+\underbrace{||\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g})||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}}+\underbrace{||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}+||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}}\\
&+\underbrace{||(\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}+||(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j})\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\\
\end{split}

for
0\leq r\leq N-m_{0}-1
.

Estimates on
T_{2}
.
We show explicit bounds only for the first term, namely
\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]
, the others can be treated similarly. As above, we deduce:

||\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}.

Now note that:

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\left[\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]\right]&=\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]+\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]-\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{\pm})^{\top}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]\\
&+\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta_{j}^{g}]\end{split}

and as above, we conclude that:

\begin{split}&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||[z_{\ell,j}^{\pm},\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}][\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||(z_{q}^{\pm}-z_{\ell,j}^{\pm})\cdot\nabla\mathcal{R}\mathrm{curl}[\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+\underbrace{||\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}}+\underbrace{||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}\delta_{q+1}}+\underbrace{||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{\pm})^{\top}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-3\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}\delta_{q+1}}\\
&\lesssim||z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{\alpha}+||z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}||_{\alpha}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||z_{q}^{\pm}-z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{1+\alpha}+||z_{q}^{\pm}-z_{\ell,j}^{\pm}||_{\alpha}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\mathrm{D}z_{\ell,j}^{-})^{\top}[\Theta_{j}^{g}]||_{r+1+\alpha}\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\\
\end{split}

for
0\leq r\leq N-m_{0}-1
.

Conclusion.
From the fact that at most two
\tilde{\eta}_{j}
are non-zero at the same time we conclude that:

\begin{split}&||R^{cut}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\sup_{j}||R_{j}^{cut}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0,\\
&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}R^{cut}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\sup_{j}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}R_{j}^{cut}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq N-m_{0}-1.\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
.
∎

2.3.1.
Splitting of the Perturbation.

Following Lemma
5.1
we construct
(w^{g},b^{g})
as a perturbation of
(v_{q},B_{q})
along the LDF
\xi^{g}
. In the following, we split
(w^{g},b^{g})
in simpler components.

Velocity Field.
Since
\xi^{g}
is designed to behave correctly with
(v_{\ell,j},\ B_{\ell,j})
, which are not globally defined in time, we need to be extra careful. Let us first separate the non-mollified part:

(2.35)

\begin{split}w^{g}&=\partial_{t}X^{g}\circ(X^{g})^{-1}+(X^{g}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})v_{q}\\
&=\underbrace{\partial_{t}X^{g}\circ(X^{g})^{-1}+(X^{g}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})v_{\ell}}_{\bar{w}^{g}}+\underbrace{(X^{g}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})(v_{q}-v_{\ell})}_{\mathring{w}^{g}}\end{split}

and according to Lemma
5.1
we have:

(2.36)

\begin{split}\bar{w}^{g}&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\sum_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{g}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}+\sum_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{g}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\theta^{g}_{w}\right],\\
\mathring{w}^{g}&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\sum_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{g}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{v_{q}-v_{\ell}}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathring{\theta}^{g}_{w}\right]\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\theta^{g}_{w}&=\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}}{(k_{0}^{g}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}^{g}+1}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}}{(k_{0}^{g}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\mathring{\theta}^{g}_{w}&=\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}}{(k_{0}^{g}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathcal{L}_{v_{q}-v_{\ell}}\Theta^{g}_{j}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathrm{d}s.\\
\end{split}

Magnetic field.
We proceed similarly, we first split:

\begin{split}&b^{g}=\underbrace{(X^{g}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})B_{\ell}}_{\bar{b}^{p}}+\underbrace{(X^{g}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})(B_{q}-B_{\ell})}_{\mathring{b}^{p}},\end{split}

then Lemma
5.1
gives:

(2.37)

\begin{split}\bar{b}^{g}&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\sum_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{g}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}+\sum_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{g}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j}}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\theta^{g}_{b}\right],\\
\mathring{b}^{g}&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\sum_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{g}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{q}-B_{\ell}}\Theta_{j}^{g}+\mathring{\theta}^{g}_{b}\right]\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\theta^{g}_{b}&=\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}}{(k_{0}^{g}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}}{(k_{0}^{g}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\mathring{\theta}^{g}_{b}&=\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}}{(k_{0}^{g}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathcal{L}_{B_{q}-B_{\ell}}\Theta^{g}_{j}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\mathrm{d}s.\\
\end{split}

2.4.
Estimates on the Perturbation

In this subsection, we study the higher-order explicit terms and remainders of the Lie-Taylor series associated with the perturbation
(w^{g},\ b^{g})
of
(v_{q},\ B_{q})
as in (
2.36
), (
2.37
).

Key quantities will be:

(2.38)

\mathcal{T}_{g}=\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}=\ell^{-\alpha}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{\gamma_{a}+2\beta+2\gamma_{CZ}}

which measures the smallness gain corresponding to one Lie-derivation, and objects which we call loss functions,
L,\ L_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
, taking the form:

\begin{split}&L(r)=\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}},\\
&L_{\mathcal{A}}(r)=1_{r\leq\underline{r}}+1_{r\geq\underline{r}+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\bar{L},\end{split}

for parameters
\underline{r}
and
\bar{L}
e.g.
\bar{L}=\delta_{q}^{-1/2}
. These keep track of the range of derivatives
0\leq r\leq\underline{r}
over which we have the desired estimates and the loss that occurs afterwards. The subscript
\mathcal{A}
will indicate that the loss function is related to an Alfvén transport estimate.

2.4.1.
Estimates on the Lagrangian Perturbation

Let
X^{g}:[-1,1]_{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\to\mathbb{T}^{3}
solve:

(2.39)

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}X_{s}^{g}(x,t)=\xi^{g}(X_{s}^{g}(x,t),t),\\
X_{0}^{g}(x,t)=x.\end{cases}

In Lemma
2.9
we gave estimates for
\xi^{g}=\mathrm{curl}\sum_{j}\Theta^{g}_{j}
. After choosing
a
sufficiently large to reabsorb the implicit constant, we can assume

(2.40)

||\mathrm{D}\xi||_{0}\leq C\mathcal{T}_{g}\leq C\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{\gamma_{a}+2\beta}\leq 1

and from this we deduce the following Lemma.

Lemma 2.11

(Estimates on
X^{g}
)
.

Let
r\geq 0
integer and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-6
. The Lagrangian perturbation
X^{g}
associated with
\xi^{g}
satisfies:

\begin{split}&||\mathrm{D}X^{g}_{s}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\lesssim\mathcal{T}_{g}\ \ \text{ for }\ \ |s|\leq 1,\\
&||\partial_{t}^{j}X^{g}_{s}||_{0},\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{j-1}\mathcal{T}_{g}\ \ \text{ for }\ \ j=1,2\ \ \&\ \ |s|\leq 1,\\
&||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathrm{D}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[r+j-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\ \ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \ \&\ \ r\geq 1\ \ \&\ \ |s|\leq 1\\
\end{split}

where the implicit constants depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
.

Remark 6

(Loss of Good Derivatives)
.

The additional loss of two derivatives in
2.11
when compared to Lemma
2.9
is there because we need to pass from the potential to the actual LDF and then control the derivative of the Lagrangian flow with the derivative of the LDF as in (
2.40
).

Proof of Lemma
2.11
.

The estimates on the pure space derivatives are standard, see Proposition
B.5
, treating time as a parameter or adapt the argument we are about to give.

We will deduce the pure time derivative bounds from those for
\xi^{g}
, which can be read immediately from the ones on
\Theta_{j}^{g}
in Lemma
2.9
. For notational convenience, we assume
0\leq s\leq 1
; the bounds for
-1\leq s\leq 0
follow identically. From the Lagrangian flow equation for
X^{g}_{s},
(
2.39
), we deduce:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}(x,t)&=\partial_{t}\int_{0}^{s}\xi^{g}(X^{g}_{s}(x,t),t)\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{t}(\xi^{g})(X^{g}_{s}(x,t),t)+\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}(X^{g}_{s}(x,t),t)[\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}]\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

Using this formula and the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
, we deduce:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}&\leq\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}(\xi^{g})\circ X^{g}_{s})||_{r}+||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{r}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{0}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\overset{r=0}{\leq}\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}\xi^{g}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}||_{0}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}\xi^{g}||_{1}||\mathrm{D}X^{g}_{s}||_{r-1}+||\partial_{t}\xi^{g}||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}^{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{0}^{s}\left(||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}||_{1}||\mathrm{D}X^{g}_{s}||_{r-1}+||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}^{r}\right])||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}||_{0}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

Given that
||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}||_{0}\lesssim\mathcal{T}^{g}\leq 1
we can run a standard Grönwall argument. We first deduce the estimate for
r=0
, plug this one into the bound for
r\geq 1
and conclude:

||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\ \text{ for }\ |s|\leq 1\ \ \&\ \ r\geq 0.

We now run the same argument to deduce the second-order pure-time-derivative bound. We first compute:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}^{2}X^{g}_{s}(x,t)&=\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{t}^{2}(\xi^{g})(X^{g}_{s}(x,t),t)+2\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}\xi^{g})(X^{g}_{s}(x,t),t)[\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}]\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{D}^{2}\xi^{g}(X^{g}_{s}(x,t),t)[\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s},\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}]+\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}(X^{g}_{s}(x,t),t)[\partial_{t}^{2}X^{g}_{s}]\mathrm{d}s,\end{split}

from this obtain

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{2}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}&\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}^{2}\xi^{g}\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{r}+||\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}\xi^{g})\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{r}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}\xi^{g})\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{0}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{0}^{s}||\mathrm{D}^{2}\xi^{g}\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{r}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}^{2}+||\mathrm{D}^{2}\xi^{g}\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{0}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}||\partial_{t}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{0}^{s}||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{r}||\partial_{t}^{2}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{0}||\partial_{t}^{2}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r+2-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}+\int_{0}^{s}||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}\circ X^{g}_{s}||_{r}||\partial_{t}^{2}X^{g}_{s}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\xi^{g}||_{0}||\partial_{t}^{2}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

and conclude that:

||\partial_{t}^{2}X^{g}_{s}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\ \text{ for }\ |s|\leq 1\ \ \&\ \ r\geq 0.

The implicit constants in the above bounds depend on
r,\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
.
∎

2.4.2.
Estimates on the Lie-Taylor Expansion

We split the estimates between the mollified terms and the non-mollified terms. Out of convenience, we state bounds only for the potentials
\Theta_{j}^{g}
. The bounds for
\xi_{j}^{g}
follow immediately by commuting the Lie-derivatives with the
\mathrm{curl}
operator by means of the identity (
A.12
). The key technical tool is the Inductive Lemma
8.1
as in Remark
29
, which allows us to deduce the estimates for an arbitrary term of the Lie-Taylor expansion from those of the leading term.

Lemma 2.12

(Lie-Derivative and Transport Estimates - Mollified Terms)
.

Fix
\bar{N}
a non-negative integer. Let
r,\ \sigma,\ k\geq 0
integers,
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-5
. There exist constants
C,C^{\prime}
which depend on
\bar{N},\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
but are uniform in the parameters
j,\ k,\ \sigma,\ r
, such that the following estimates hold.

Lie-derivatives bounds.
For
\sigma=0,1,2
and
0\leq r+k+\sigma\leq\bar{N}
, we have:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+k+\sigma-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},\\
&||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+k+\sigma-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.\\
\end{split}

Alfvén-transport bounds.
For
0\leq r+k\leq\bar{N}-1
, we have:

\begin{split}&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r},\ ||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1},\\
&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r},\ ||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{g}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-\alpha}\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.\\
\end{split}

Proof of Lemma
2.12
.

We want to apply the Inductive Lemma
8.1
as in Remark
29
, with the following
F_{0}^{j}
:

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j},\ \mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j},\ \mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j},\ \mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}.

We let:

\mathcal{J}=\left\{J=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k}):j_{i}\sim j\right\}

be the set of all ordered
k
-tuples of indices
j_{i}\in\{j,\ j+1,\ j-1\}
neighbouring
j
, note that the cardinality of this set is
2^{k}
, which is in line with the implicit constant scaling exponentially in
k
. Since the support of the other
\xi^{g}_{j_{i}}
do not intersect that of
\xi_{j}^{g}
by construction, we can write:

\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}F_{0}^{j}=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{1}}}\dots\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{k}}}F_{0}^{j}

In the notation of the Inductive Lemma, we are thus interested in sequences of vector fields
(\sigma_{i})_{i}:=(\xi_{j_{i}}^{p})_{i}
with
j_{i}\sim j
. We now need to provide estimates on
F_{0}^{j}
and
\xi_{j_{i}}^{g}
, we remark that we are not trying to be precise with the exact loss
\lambda_{q}\leadsto\ell^{-1}
quantified by
\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}
, we will pick the worst lower bound, namely
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-5
coming from the mollification correction terms, between all the estimates even if a specific term admits a better bound.

F_{0}^{j}
of transport type.
We first compute:

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}&=\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}+(\mathrm{D}v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j},\\
\partial_{t}[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}]&=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}+(\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j}+(\mathrm{D}v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\partial_{t}\Theta^{g}_{j},\\
\partial_{t}^{2}[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}]&=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}+(\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}^{2}v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j}+(\mathrm{D}v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\partial_{t}^{2}\Theta^{g}_{j}+2(\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\partial_{t}\Theta^{g}_{j}\\
\end{split}

and by means of the bounds in Lemmas
2.4
and
2.9
we deduce:

||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma-(\underline{r}+1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ \sigma=0,1,2\ \ \&\ \ r\geq 0.

We move to the transport estimates. We first compute:

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}&=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}+(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\mathrm{D}v_{\ell,j}^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{\pm}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}+(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}\\
&+\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j}+\mathrm{D}(v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta^{g}_{j}\end{split}

and by means of Lemmas
2.2
,
2.3
and
2.9
we deduce:

\begin{split}&||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\leq||\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{\pm}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm}_{\ell,j})^{\top}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathrm{D}(v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\leq\underbrace{||\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{\pm}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}}+\underbrace{||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}||_{1}||(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}||_{r+1+\alpha}||(\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{+}+\mathcal{A}_{\ell,j}^{-})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{0}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}}\\
&+\underbrace{||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}v_{\ell,j}||_{1}||\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}v_{\ell,j}||_{r+1+\alpha}||\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{0}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\ell^{-2\alpha}\delta_{q}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}}+\underbrace{||v_{\ell,j}||_{1}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||v_{\ell,j}||_{r+1+\alpha}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{0}}_{\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}+1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\\
\end{split}

Here we used that:

\tau^{c}\ell^{-\alpha}\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\leq 1

and will do so in what follows, without further mention. Similar computation for
\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}
lead to:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma-(\underline{r}+1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ \sigma=0,1,2\ \ \&\ \ r\geq 0,\\
||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}+1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0.\end{split}

F_{0}^{j}
coming from the mollification correction.
We first compute:

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}&=(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla\Theta^{g}_{j}+\mathrm{D}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j},\\
\partial_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}]&=\partial_{t}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla\Theta^{g}_{j}+(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\Theta^{g}_{j}+\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j}+\mathrm{D}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\partial_{t}\Theta^{g}_{j},\\
\partial_{t}^{2}[\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}]&=\partial_{t}^{2}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla\Theta^{g}_{j}+(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}^{2}\Theta^{g}_{j}+2\partial_{t}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\Theta^{g}_{j}\\
&+\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}^{2}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j}+\mathrm{D}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\partial_{t}^{2}\Theta^{g}_{j}+2\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})^{\top}\partial_{t}\Theta^{g}_{j},\\
\end{split}

and by means of the bounds in Lemmas
2.3
,
2.9
we deduce:

||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma-\underline{r}](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ \sigma=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ r\geq 0.

We now use the identity in (
A.17
) to write:

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}=\mathcal{L}_{(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})}\Theta^{g}_{j}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}.

Lemmas
2.2
and
2.3
now give:

\begin{split}||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})||_{r+\alpha}+||(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})||_{r+\alpha}+||v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}||_{0}||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}||_{r+1+\alpha}+||v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}||_{r+\alpha}||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}||_{1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q},\\
\end{split}

using in addition the bounds in Lemma
2.9
, we deduce:

\begin{split}&||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\leq||\mathcal{L}_{(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\leq||[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})]\cdot\nabla\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathrm{D}[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})]^{\top}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})\cdot\nabla(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathrm{D}(v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j})^{\top}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\delta_{q}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}.\end{split}

Similarly, one can show:

||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}.

Estimates on the
\sigma_{i}
.
As the estimates on
\xi_{j_{i}}^{p}
can be deduced from
2.9
upon using the identity (
A.12
). We need only deal with the error in the transport, namely

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\xi^{g}_{j_{i}}=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}}})\xi^{g}_{j_{i}}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}}}\xi^{g}_{j_{i}},

by means of Lemmas
2.3
,
2.9
we first deduce:

(2.41)

\begin{split}||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}}})\xi^{g}_{j_{i}}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},\\
||\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}}}\xi^{g}_{j_{i}}||_{r+\alpha}&\leq||(z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}})\cdot\nabla\xi^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}+||\xi^{g}_{j}\cdot\nabla(z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}})||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim(||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{0}+||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}}-z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{0})||\xi^{g}_{j}||_{r+1+\alpha}\\
&+(||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}+||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}}-z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha})||\xi^{g}_{j}||_{1}\\
&+||\xi^{g}_{j}||_{0}(||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{r+1+\alpha}+||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}}-z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{r+1})\\
&+||\xi^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}(||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}-z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{1}+||z^{\pm}_{\ell,j_{i}}-z_{\ell}^{\pm}||_{1})\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\\
\end{split}

and we conclude from
(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}<1
that:

||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,j}})\xi^{g}_{j_{i}}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0.

Conclusion.
With the estimates on the various
F_{0}^{j}
and
\sigma_{i}=\xi_{j_{i}}
at hand we can apply the Inductive Lemma
8.1
as in Remark
29
, with:

\begin{split}&A=\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ or }\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},\\
&A_{\mathcal{A}}=\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ or }\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-\alpha}\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},\\
&\bar{\varsigma}_{i}=\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1},\ \bar{\varsigma}_{i,\mathcal{A}}=\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},\\
&L(r)=L_{\mathcal{A}}(r)=\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}.\\
\end{split}

Note that
L=L_{\mathcal{A}}
, we do not lose a derivative because the objects were constructed by solving (
2.29
) exactly. We conclude that:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{1}}}\dots\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{k}}}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}&\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{g}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\text{ for }\sigma=0,1,2\ \text{ and }0\leq r+k+\sigma\leq\bar{N}\\
||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{1}}}\dots\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{k}}}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}&\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{g}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\text{ for }\ 0\leq r+k\leq\bar{N}-1\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{1}}}\dots\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}&\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{g}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\text{ for }\sigma=0,1,2\ \text{ and }0\leq r+k+\sigma\leq\bar{N},\\
||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{1}}}\dots\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}_{j_{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}&\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{g}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\text{ for }0\leq r+k\leq\bar{N}-1.\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants and
C
depend on
\bar{N},\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
but not on
r,\ k
. Similar bounds hold for the terms involving the magnetic field.

The claimed estimates then follow by summing over
J=(j_{i},\dots,j_{k})\in\mathcal{J}
and redefining
C,\ C^{\prime}
accordingly.
∎

Lemma 2.13

(Remainders - Galbrun Stage)
.

For
k_{0}\geq 0
let:

\begin{split}\theta^{k_{0}}&=\begin{cases}\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}+1}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell,j}})\Theta^{g}_{j}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}+1}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell,j}}\Theta^{g}_{j}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s\end{cases}\\
\theta^{k_{0}}_{c}&=\begin{cases}\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}+1}\mathcal{L}_{B_{\ell}-B_{\ell,j}}\Theta_{j}^{g}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\sum_{j}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{g})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k_{0}+1}\mathcal{L}_{v_{\ell}-v_{\ell,j}}\Theta_{j}^{g}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s.\end{cases}\end{split}

Fix
\bar{N}
a non-negative integer and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-5
. There exist constants
C,\ C^{\prime}
, which depend on
\bar{N},\ C_{0},\ c_{0},\ \alpha
such that for
r\geq 0
integer and
\sigma=0,1,2
satisfying
0\leq r+(k_{0}+1)+\sigma\leq\bar{N}
we have:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\theta^{k_{0}}||_{r+\alpha}\leq C^{\prime}\frac{(C)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+2)!}\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma+(k_{0}+1)-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k_{0}+1}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},\\
&||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\theta^{k_{0}}_{c}||_{r+\alpha}\leq C^{\prime}\frac{(C)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+2)!}\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma+(k_{0}+1)-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k_{0}+1}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.\\
\end{split}

The proof of this Lemma is a variation of the proof of Lemma
3.14
using the estimates in Lemmas
2.4
and
2.9
this time, and we omit it.

Remark 7
.

From Lemma
2.13
with
k_{0}=k_{0}^{g},\ \bar{N}=N
, we deduce that:

||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\theta^{g}_{w}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\theta^{g}_{b}||_{r+\alpha}\leq C^{\prime}\frac{(C)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}}{(k_{0}^{g}+2)!}\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}\lambda_{q}^{[r+\sigma+(k_{0}^{g}+1)-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k_{0}^{g}+1}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}

for
0\leq r+(k_{0}^{g}+1)+\sigma\leq N
, where
\theta_{w}^{g},\ \theta_{b}^{g}
are as in (
2.36
) and (
2.37
).

Lemma 2.14

(Lie-Derivative and Transport Estimates - Non Mollified Terms)
.

Let
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
r,\ k,\ \sigma\geq 0
integers. Moreover, let
L,\ L_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
be the loss functions:

(2.42)

\begin{split}&L(r)=\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}},\\
&L_{\mathcal{A}}(r)=1_{r\leq\underline{r}-1}+1_{r\geq\underline{r}}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\bar{L}\end{split}

with
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-5,\ \bar{L}=\delta_{q}^{-1/2}
. There exist constants
C,\ C^{\prime}
which depend on all the parameters but not on
a
and are uniform in
k,\ r,\ j,\ \sigma
, such that the following bounds hold.

Lie-derivatives bounds.
For
\sigma=0,1,2
and
0\leq r+k+\sigma\leq N-m_{0}-2
, we have:

||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{v_{q}-v_{\ell}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{q}-B_{\ell}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+\sigma}L(r+k+\sigma)\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k}\ell^{-\alpha}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.

Alfvén-transport bounds.
For
0\leq r+k\leq N-m_{0}-3
, we have:

||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{v_{q}-v_{\ell}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{g}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{B_{q}-B_{\ell}}\Theta^{g}_{j}||_{r+\alpha}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r}L_{\mathcal{A}}(r+k)\mathcal{T}^{k}_{g}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-\alpha}\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.

Remainder bounds.
For
\sigma=0,1,2
and
0\leq r+\sigma\leq N-(k_{0}^{g}+1)-m_{0}-2
, we have:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathring{\theta}^{g}_{w}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\mathring{\theta}^{g}_{b}||_{r+\alpha}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}(C)^{k_{0}^{g}+1}}{(k_{0}^{g}+2)!}\lambda_{q}^{r}\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k_{0}^{p}+1}L(k_{0}^{g}+1+\sigma)(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.\\
\end{split}

The proof of this Lemma is mutatis mutandis as that of Lemma
2.12
. We remark, however, the following key differences:

•

We cannot pick
\bar{N}
arbitrarily large, the presence of
v_{q}-v_{\ell},\ B_{q}-B_{\ell}
forces us to set
\bar{N}=N-m_{0}-2
, the loss of
m_{0}
coming from the mollification error estimate, and the additional two are one to interpolate the
C^{\alpha}
norm and the other because even for
k=0
we have one Lie-derivative acting. This is also why
\underline{r}
is smaller compared to the one in Lemma
2.9
.

•

We state the bound for the transport derivative this way out of convenience, the loss
L_{\mathcal{A}}
just saying that if
r+k>\underline{r}-1
we don’t have information on the transport derivative of
v_{q}-v_{\ell},\ B_{q}-B_{\ell}
and we switch to the pure time derivative estimate losing a
\delta_{q}^{1/2}
. To match the loss function of the pure derivative bound, we also shift
\underline{r}
to
\underline{r}-1
, compare this to the Alfvén transport bound in Lemma
2.12
.

2.4.3.
Estimates on the Vector Fields.

With all the estimates for the intermediate objects making the Lie-Taylor expansions of the Galbrun perturbation, we are ready to prove estimates on the actual vector fields.

Lemma 2.15

(Estimates on
(\mathring{w}^{g},\mathring{b}^{g})
)
.

Let
r\geq 0
integer and
\underline{r}=M-m_{0}-6,\ \bar{L}=1/\delta_{q}^{1/2}
. Let
L_{g,\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
be one of the following admissible loss functions:

(2.43a)

\displaystyle L_{g,\mathcal{A}}(r)=

\displaystyle 1_{r\leq\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-2}+1_{\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-1\leq r\leq\underline{r}-1}\left[1+\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-r}\bar{L}\right]+1_{r\geq\underline{r}}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\bar{L}

(2.43b)

\displaystyle L_{g,\mathcal{A}}(r)=

\displaystyle 1_{r\leq\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-1}+1_{r\geq\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\lambda_{q}}\right)^{r-(\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-1)}=\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-1)]^{+}(b-1)}

Under the choice of parameters in
1.5
, we have:

(2.44)

\eqref{precise}\leq 2\eqref{lossy}

and
