\begin{split}\tilde{a}_{I}-a_{I}&=\underbrace{\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left(\chi_{I}\gamma_{\zeta}(\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}\left(\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}\right)\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{T})\right)^{I_{t}}_{\ell_{t}}-\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\chi_{I}\gamma_{\zeta}(\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}\left(\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}\right)\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{T})}_{T_{1}}\\
&+\underbrace{\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\chi_{I}\gamma_{\zeta}(\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}\left(\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}\right)\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{T})-\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\chi_{I}\gamma_{\zeta}(\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]^{2}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}\left(\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}\right)\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}^{T})}_{T_{2}}\end{split}

The estimate on
T_{1}
follows immediately from (
3.19
) and (
3.26
):

||T_{1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell_{t}/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}.

We now estimate
T_{2}
. Define for any matrices
A,M\in B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})
:

f(A,M)=\gamma_{\zeta}(\det[A]^{-2}AMA^{T}),

from (
3.24
) and Lemma
2.25
it follows that for some implicit constant which depends on
r
we have

(3.28)

\begin{split}||f|_{B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})\times B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})}||_{r}\lesssim 1.\end{split}

We now compute:

(3.29)

\begin{split}f(A_{1},M)-f(A_{0},M)&=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{s}\left[f(sA_{1}+(1-s)A_{0},M)\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}(\partial_{A}f)(sA_{1}+(1-s)A_{0},M)[A_{1}-A_{0}]\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

and we use this with
A_{1}=\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I},\ A_{2}=\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}
and
M=\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}
to rewrite:

(3.30)

T_{2}=f(A_{1},M)-f(A_{0},M).

From the bound in (
3.28
), the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
and the expression for (
3.30
) in (
3.29
), we obtain:

\begin{split}||T_{2}||_{r}&\leq\int_{0}^{1}||(\partial_{A}f)(s\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}+(1-s)\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I},\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1})[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}-\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}-\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]||_{0}\int_{0}^{1}||(\partial_{A}f)(s\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}+(1-s)\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I},\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1})||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}-\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]||_{r}\int_{0}^{1}||(\partial_{A}f)(s\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}+(1-s)\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I},\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1})||_{0}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}-\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]||_{0}||f|_{B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})\times B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})}||_{2}(||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}||_{r}+||\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}||_{r}+||R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}||_{r})\\
&+||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}-\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]||_{0}||f|_{B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})\times B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})}||_{r+1}(||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}||_{1}+||\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}||_{1}+||R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}||_{1})^{r}\\
&+||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}-\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]||_{r}||f|_{B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})\times B_{1/2}(\mathrm{Id})}||_{1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\end{split}

and we conclude:

\begin{split}||\tilde{a}_{I}-a_{I}||_{r}\leq||T_{1}||_{r}+||T_{2}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[(\ell_{t}/\tau^{c})+\mathcal{T}_{g}\right].\end{split}

For the Alfvén transport estimate, we consider each term separately. We first rewrite:

\begin{split}\tilde{z}_{q}^{\pm}&=\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}+(\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell}-\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I})+(\tilde{z}_{q}^{\pm}-\tilde{z}_{\ell}^{\pm}),\\
\tilde{z}_{q}^{\pm}&=z^{\pm}_{\ell,I}+(z^{\pm}_{\ell}-z^{\pm}_{\ell,I})+(z^{\pm}_{q}-z^{\pm}_{\ell})+(\tilde{z}_{q}^{\pm}-z^{\pm}_{q}).\end{split}

Now, by means of Lemmas
2.1
,
2.3
,
2.27
,
3.1
and
3.3
, and the estimate in (
3.27
) we proved above, we conclude:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}(\tilde{a}_{I}-a_{I})||_{r}&\leq||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r}+||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}a_{I}||_{r}\\
&\leq||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r}+||(\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell}-\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I})\cdot\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r}+||(\tilde{z}_{q}^{\pm}-\tilde{z}_{\ell}^{\pm})\cdot\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r}\\
&+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}a_{I}||_{r}+||(z^{\pm}_{\ell}-z^{\pm}_{\ell,I})\cdot\nabla a_{I}||_{r}+||(z^{\pm}_{q}-z^{\pm}_{\ell})\cdot\nabla a_{I}||_{r}+||(\tilde{z}_{q}^{\pm}-z^{\pm}_{q})\cdot\nabla a_{I}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\underbrace{\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}}_{1/\tau^{c}\mathcal{T}_{g}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\end{split}

for
0\leq r\leq N-m_{0}
. Here we used
\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}},\ \lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}<1
, see (
1.24
), (
1.25
). The implicit constant depends on
r
and all the other parameters, but not on
a
.

Pure time derivatives.
We write the pure time derivatives as:

(3.31)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}_{I}&=\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\tilde{a}_{I}-z^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla\tilde{a}_{I},\\
\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{a}_{I}&=\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\mp}_{\ell,I}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\tilde{a}_{I}-(\partial_{t}\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I})\cdot\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}-2\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{a}_{I}-(\tilde{z}^{\mp}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla)^{2}\tilde{a}_{I}\\
\end{split}

and from the bounds in Lemma
3.4
and (
3.25
), (
3.27
) above we conclude:

||\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[r+j-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ r\geq 0.

Support Property.
Note that by construction of the space partition of unity in Section
2.2
with the obvious notation, we have:

(3.32)

p(I_{1})=p(I_{2})\Longrightarrow\text{dist}(U_{j^{\prime}_{1}},\ U_{j^{\prime}_{2}})>1/3\tau^{c}

and

\text{supp}_{x}\chi_{I_{1}}\subset U_{j^{\prime}_{1}},\ \text{supp}_{x}\chi_{I_{2}}\subset U_{j^{\prime}_{2}}.

The issue here is that we mollify the slow coefficients along the Alfvén direction through the operator
(\dots)^{I_{t}}_{\ell_{t}}
, namely

\tilde{a}_{I}=\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left(\chi_{I}\gamma_{\zeta}(\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{J}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{J}\left(\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}\right)\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{J}^{T})\right)^{I_{t}}_{\ell_{t}}

and this translates the support of the function. The last property in Lemma
3.2
ensures that:

\begin{split}\text{supp}_{t,x}\tilde{a}_{I_{i}}&\subset B_{2(C_{0}+1)\ell_{t}}\left(\text{supp}_{x,t}\left(\chi_{I}\gamma_{\zeta}(\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{J}]^{-2}\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{J}\left(\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}/\delta_{q+1}\right)\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{J}^{T}\right)\right)\\
&\subset B_{2(C_{0}+1)\ell_{t}}\left(U_{j^{\prime}_{i}}\times B_{2/3\tau^{c}}(t_{j_{i}})\cap\text{supp}_{x,t}R_{\ell}\right)\\
\end{split}

and given (
3.32
), upon choosing
a
sufficiently large so that:

4(C_{0}+1)\ell_{t}=4(C_{0}+1)\tau^{a}<1/3\tau^{c}\ \text{ and }\ 2/3\tau^{c}+2(C_{0}+1)\tau^{a}<\tau^{c}

we can ensure:

\text{supp}_{x}\ \tilde{a}_{I_{1}}\cap\text{supp}_{x}\ \tilde{a}_{I_{2}}=\emptyset\ \text{ and }\ \text{supp}_{x,t}\ \tilde{a}_{I}\Subset B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{j^{\prime}})\times B_{\tau^{c}}(t_{j})\ \text{ and }\ \text{supp}_{x,t}\ \tilde{a}_{I}\subset B_{2(C_{0}+1)\ell_{t}}(\text{supp}_{x,t}R_{\ell})

for
I_{i}
as above and
I
any index. The support properties are still guaranteed, and the claim in the Lemma follows with
C=2(C_{0}+1)
.
∎

Lemma 3.8

(Chart Update Error)
.

Let
\underline{r}=M-2m_{0}-k_{0}^{g}-9
. Define the chart update error to be

R^{p,crt}=\sum_{I}g_{I}^{2}\left[\tilde{A}_{I}-A_{I}\right]

then, the following bounds hold:

\begin{split}&||R^{p,crt}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0,\\
&||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}R^{p,crt}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq N-m_{0}.\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants depend on
r
and all the other parameters, but not on
a
.

Remark 17
.

The bounds given here differ from the ones for the ‘flow error’ in
[
24
]
because of our choice
\gamma_{t}=\gamma_{a}
.

Proof of Lemma
3.8
.

In what follows the
C^{0}_{t}C^{r}_{x}
norms are thought on the space-time domain
Q_{I}
of the chart. Recall that indexing
I
all the objects we have:

\begin{split}A_{I}&=\delta_{q+1}a_{I}^{2}\Psi_{I}^{2*}\zeta\otimes\Psi_{I}^{2*}\zeta=\delta_{q+1}a_{I}^{2}\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]^{2}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}^{-1}\zeta\otimes\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}^{-1}\zeta,\\
\tilde{A}_{I}&=\delta_{q+1}\tilde{a}_{I}^{2}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}\zeta\otimes\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}\zeta=\delta_{q+1}\tilde{a}_{I}^{2}\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]^{2}\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{-1}\zeta\otimes\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{-1}\zeta.\end{split}

The proof of this Lemma is an application of the work done in Section
7
, in particular of the chart stability Lemma
7.2
from which we deduce:

||(\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I})^{-1}-(\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I})^{-1}||_{r},\ ||\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]-\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}.

Given that for each space-time point at most one
g_{I}^{2}(\tilde{A}_{I}-A_{I})
is non-zero, see Lemma
3.9
, and the bounds in Lemma
3.7
, we conclude:

\begin{split}||R^{p,crt}||_{r}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}\left[(\ell_{t}/\tau^{c})+\mathcal{T}_{g}\right]\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1},\end{split}

for
r\geq 0
, where we used
\ell_{t}=\tau^{a}
and
(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\geq\mathcal{T}_{g}
.

Using the fact that
\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]=\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}]=0
and that
(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}})\tilde{\Psi}^{*}_{I}\zeta=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}_{\ell,I}})\Psi^{*}_{I}\zeta=0
from Remark
27
and Lemmas
2.6
,
3.6
, and arguing as in the proof of Lemma
3.7
, we first deduce:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}[g^{2}(A_{I}-\tilde{A}_{I})]||_{r}&\leq||(\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}g^{2})(A_{I}-\tilde{A}_{I})]||_{r}+||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}A_{I}||_{r}+||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}\tilde{A}_{I}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1}\left[(\ell_{t}/\tau^{c})+\mathcal{T}_{g}\right]\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}[\underbrace{\ell_{t}/(\tau^{a}\tau^{c})}_{\leq 1/\tau^{c}}+\underbrace{1/\tau^{a}\mathcal{T}_{g}}_{\leq 1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}/\delta_{q}}]\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1},\end{split}

for
0\leq r\leq N-m_{0}
and from the fact that at most one
g_{I}^{2}(\tilde{A}_{I}-A_{I})
is non-zero et each space-time point we conclude that:

||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}R^{p,crt}||_{r}\leq\sup_{I}||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}[g^{2}(A_{I}-\tilde{A}_{I})]||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}.

The implicit constants in the above bounds depend on
r
and all the parameters, but not on
a
.
∎

3.4.
Nash LDF

With the time-oscillating profiles from Lemma
2.8
, the updated coefficients from
3.7
, charts from
3.6
and cut-offs from (
2.24
), we are ready to define the Nash LDF
\xi^{p}
. Using a similar notation to Lemma
3.6
given any
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, any
\lambda>0
and unit vector
k
we define:

\varphi_{\lambda,k}(x)=\varphi(\lambda x\cdot k)

and think of
\varphi_{\lambda,k}
as a function
\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
. We make the explicit choice
\varphi=\sqrt{2}\sin
, but this plays no role. It follows that:

||\varphi||_{r}\leq\sqrt{2}\lesssim 1.

Given an index
I=(\zeta,J)\in\mathcal{I}
and
(k,\nu,\zeta)
the orthonormal basis associated to
\zeta
from the Geometric Lemma
2.5
we define label maps:

I\to\nu_{I},\ I\to k_{I},\ I\to\zeta_{I}

associating the index
I
to its orthonormal basis. Abusing of notation we will use the label
I
even if the labeled object depends only on some components of the index e.g. we write
\ \tilde{\Psi}_{I},\ \tilde{B}_{\ell,I},\ \alpha_{I}
instead of
\tilde{\Psi}_{J},\ \tilde{B}_{\ell,j},\ \alpha_{p(I)}
. We now define
\Theta^{p},\ \Theta^{p}_{I}
to be the 1-forms:

(3.33)

\Theta^{p}_{I}=\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I}),\ \ \ \ \Theta^{p}=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}\\

and
\xi^{p}
to be the divergence-free vector field having
\Theta^{p}
as potential, namely,

\xi_{I}^{p}=\mathrm{curl}[\Theta_{I}^{p}],\ \ \ \ \xi^{p}=\mathrm{curl}[\Theta^{p}]=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\xi_{I}^{p}.

Computing the
\mathrm{curl}
explicitly, we obtain the following representation:

(3.34)

\begin{split}\xi^{p}&=\mathrm{curl}[\Theta^{p}]\\
&=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\tilde{\Psi}^{2*}_{I}\mathrm{curl}\left[\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I}\right]+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\\
&=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\tau^{a}\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\tilde{\Psi}^{2*}_{I}\left[\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I}\right]+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\\
\end{split}

The definition is motivated by the Lie-transport properties of Lemma
3.6
and the geometric decomposition
3.21
.

Lemma 3.9

(Disjointedness)
.

Let
I,I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
with
I\neq I^{\prime}
, then

\text{supp}_{x,t}\Theta_{I}\cap\text{supp}_{x,t}\Theta_{I^{\prime}}=\emptyset,

since the property is retained for all space and time derivatives, in particular, we have

\text{supp}_{x,t}\xi_{I}\cap\text{supp}_{x,t}\Theta_{I^{\prime}}=0.

This is a simple consequence of our Ansatz (
3.33
) and Lemmas
2.8
,
3.7
, and ultimately due to the presence of the fast-oscillating time profiles
\{\alpha_{I}\}_{I}
and a minimal partition in space
\{\theta_{I}\}_{I}
. Let
p
be the type map in (
2.28
), we distinguish two cases:

•

p(I)=p(I^{\prime})
in this case the indices are associated to the same family in the covering
\mathcal{C}
of
\mathbb{T}^{3}
and are thus disjoint by construction. See Lemma
3.7
.

•

p(I)\neq p(I^{\prime})
the time profiles
\alpha_{I}=\alpha_{p(I)}
and
\alpha_{I^{\prime}}=\alpha_{p(I^{\prime})}
are disjoint in time. See lemma
2.8
. No interactions also in this case.

3.4.1.
Splitting of the Perturbation

In the language of Lemma
5.1
, we now construct a perturbation
(w^{p},b^{p})
of
(\tilde{v}_{q},\tilde{B}_{q})
along
\xi^{p}
; the disjointness properties from Lemma
3.9
will play a crucial role. We follow the same strategy as for the Galbrun perturbations, see Section
2.3
for more details.

Velocity Field.
According to Lemma
5.1
we now expand the perturbation
w^{g}
along
\xi^{p}
of
\tilde{v}_{q}
. Since
\xi^{p}_{I}
is designed to behave correctly with
(\tilde{v}_{\ell,I},\ \tilde{B}_{\ell,I})
, which are not globally defined in time, we need to be extra careful. Let us first separate the non-mollified part:

(3.35)

\begin{split}w^{p}&=\partial_{t}X^{p}\circ(X^{p})^{-1}+(X^{p}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})\tilde{v}_{q}\\
&=\underbrace{\partial_{t}X^{p}\circ(X^{p})^{-1}+(X^{p}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})\tilde{v}_{\ell}}_{\bar{w}^{p}}+\underbrace{(X^{p}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})(\tilde{v}_{q}-\tilde{v}_{\ell})}_{\mathring{w}^{p}}\end{split}

and according to Lemma
5.1
we have:

(3.36)

\begin{split}\bar{w}^{p}&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\sum_{I}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{p}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta^{p}_{I}+\sum_{I}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{p}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell}-\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}+\theta_{w}^{p}\right],\\
\mathring{w}^{p}&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\sum_{I}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{p}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{q}-\tilde{v}_{\ell}}\Theta_{I}^{p}+\mathring{\theta}_{w}^{p}\right]\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\theta_{w}^{p}&=\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}}{(k_{0}^{p}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}^{p}+1}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta^{p}_{I}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}}{(k_{0}^{p}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell}-\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\mathring{\theta}_{w}^{p}&=\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}}{(k_{0}^{p}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{q}-\tilde{v}_{\ell}}\Theta_{I}^{p}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

Magnetic field.
We proceed similarly, we first split:

\begin{split}&b^{g}=\underbrace{(X^{p}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})\tilde{B}_{\ell}}_{\bar{b}^{p}}+\underbrace{(X^{p}_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})(\tilde{B}_{q}-\tilde{B}_{\ell})}_{\mathring{b}^{p}}\end{split}

and Lemma
5.1
gives:

(3.37)

\begin{split}\bar{b}^{p}&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\sum_{I}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{p}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta^{p}_{I}+\sum_{I}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{p}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell}-\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}+\theta^{p}_{b}\right],\\
\mathring{b}^{p}&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\sum_{I}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}^{p}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{q}-\tilde{B}_{\ell}}\Theta_{I}^{p}+\mathring{\theta}^{p}_{b}\right]\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\theta^{p}_{b}&=\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}}{(k_{0}^{p}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta^{p}_{I}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}}{(k_{0}^{p}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell}-\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\mathring{\theta}^{p}_{b}&=\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}}{(k_{0}^{p}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{q}-\tilde{B}_{\ell}}\Theta_{I}^{p}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}^{p}+1}\mathrm{d}s.\\
\end{split}

Note that in the higher-order terms of the Lie-Taylor expansion, there are no cross terms involving different indices; this is a consequence of Lemma
3.9
.

We now study the leading terms:

(3.38)

w^{p,p}_{I}=\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}\right],\ b_{I}^{p,p}=\mathrm{curl}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}\right],\ w^{p,p}=\sum_{I}w^{p,p}_{I},\ b^{p,p}=\sum_{I}b^{p,p}_{I}.

Using the definition of
\Theta_{I}^{p}
in (
3.33
) and the commutation (
A.12
) we compute:

(3.39)

\begin{split}w^{p,p}_{I}&=\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\left[\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\right]\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}\Biggr[\tilde{a}_{I}\underbrace{\alpha_{I}^{\prime}}_{=g_{I}}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})+\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla)(\tilde{a}_{I})\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\Biggr]\\
&=\underbrace{\tilde{a}_{I}g_{I}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I})}_{w_{I}^{p,o}}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}g_{I}\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\\
&+\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,I}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,I})(\tilde{a}_{I})\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I})+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,I}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,I})(\tilde{a}_{I})\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\\
&=w_{I}^{p,o}+w_{I}^{p,c}\\
b_{I}^{p,p}&=\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\left[\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\right]\right]=\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}\left[\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla(\tilde{a}_{I})\alpha_{I}\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\right]\\
&=\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{+}_{\ell,I}-\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{-}_{\ell,I})(\tilde{a}_{I})\Psi_{I}^{2*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I})+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{+}_{\ell,I}-\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{-}_{\ell,I})\tilde{a}_{I}\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\end{split}

where we used the transport properties of the charts in Lemma
3.6
and the fact that by definition
\alpha_{I}^{\prime}=g_{I}
, see Lemma
2.8
. This makes the computation (
1.14
) in the overview Subsection
1.3
rigorous. In light of the geometric decomposition in (
2.26
) it is clear how we will use the term

w^{p,o}_{I}=\tilde{a}_{I}g_{I}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I})

to correct the ‘well prepared’ Reynolds stress coming from the Galbrun stage. As it is often done in convex integration, we refer to
w_{I}^{p,o}
as the oscillation part and
w_{I}^{p,c}
as the corrector part of the leading term of the velocity field perturbation.

Lemma 3.10

(Leading Terms)
.

For any
I\in\mathcal{I}
,
r\geq 0
integer and
j=0,1,2
we have:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}w^{p,p}_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}w^{p,p}_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}.\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants depend on
r
and all the other parameters, but not on
I
and
a
. Moreover, given any
\bar{N}
and
\beta,\ b
, there exists
a
sufficiently large such that the implicit constant is fixed for
0\leq r\leq\bar{N}
and independent of
C_{0}
and the other parameters.

We also have the following bounds for the corrector and magnetic terms:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}w^{p,c}_{I}||_{r},||\partial_{t}^{j}b^{p,p}_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}w^{p,c}_{I}||_{r},||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}b^{p,p}_{I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{1}{\ell_{t}}\right].\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants depend on
r
and all the other parameters, but not on
a
.

Proof of Lemma
3.10
.

We will prove the bounds for the velocity field only; the ones for the magnetic field follow as those for
w^{p,c}
. We set
\underline{r}=M-2m_{0}-k_{0}^{g}-8
.

Oscillation term
w^{p,o}_{I}
.
From the definition in (
3.39
), the bounds in Lemmas
3.6
,
3.7
and the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
, we deduce that:

(3.40)

\begin{split}||w^{p,o}_{I}||_{r}&\leq\sup_{t}|g_{I}|\ ||\tilde{a}_{I}\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]\varphi^{\prime}(\lambda_{q+1}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}\cdot k_{I})\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{-1}\zeta_{I}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\sup_{t}|g_{I}|\left[||\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r}||\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{-1}\zeta||_{0}||\varphi^{\prime}||_{0}+||\tilde{a}_{I}||_{0}||\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}]||_{r}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{-1}\zeta||_{0}||\varphi^{\prime}||_{0}\right]\\
&+\sup_{t}|g_{I}|||\tilde{a}_{I}||_{0}||\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}]||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{-1}\zeta||_{r}||\varphi^{\prime}||_{0}\\
&+\sup_{t}|g_{I}|||\tilde{a}_{I}||_{0}||\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}]||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{-1}\zeta||_{0}||\varphi^{\prime}(\lambda_{q+1}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}\cdot k_{I})||_{r}\\
&\overset{r=0}{\lesssim}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\leq}C_{r}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+C^{\prime}_{r}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\lambda_{q+1}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}||_{r-1}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}||_{0}^{r}\right]\\
&\leq C_{r}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+C^{\prime}_{r}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[C_{r}^{\prime\prime}\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-1-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}2^{r}\right]\\
&\leq(C^{\prime}_{r}2^{r})\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[1+\frac{C_{r}}{(C^{\prime}_{r}2^{r})}\left(\frac{\ell^{-1}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r}+\frac{C^{\prime\prime}_{r}}{2^{r}}\left(\frac{\ell^{-1}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r-1}\right]\end{split}

Here, the implicit constant in the bound for
r=0
is independent of
C_{0}
. Moreover,
C_{r},\ C^{\prime\prime}_{r}
depend on
r,\ C_{0}
and the other parameters, while
C_{r}^{\prime}
depends on
r
but not on
C_{0}
and the other parameters. Upon choosing
a
sufficiently large, we can ensure that:

1+\frac{C_{r}}{(C^{\prime}_{r}2^{r})}\left(\frac{\ell^{-1}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r}+\frac{C^{\prime\prime}_{r}}{2^{r}}\left(\frac{\ell^{-1}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r-1}\leq 2\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}

and conclude that:

||w^{p,o}_{I}||_{r}\leq C_{\bar{N}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}

for some fixed
C_{\bar{N}}
which depends on
\bar{N}
but is independent of
C_{0}
and the other parameters. Arguing similarly for the
\partial_{t},\ \partial_{t}^{2}
case, the claimed bound follows.

To be precise, one should distinguish the case
r=1
as well to ensure that
C^{\prime\prime}_{1}
does not depend on
C_{0}
, this is the case because according to Lemma
3.6
we have:

||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},

this is also what is behind the claim for
r=0
.

We now recall Remark
27
which gives
\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\det[\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}]=0
. The estimates and transport properties in Lemmas
3.6
,
3.7
, then give:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}w^{p,p}_{I}||_{r}&\lesssim 1/\tau^{a}\sup_{t}|g_{I}^{\prime}|\ ||\tilde{a}_{I}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I})||_{r}+\sup_{t}|g_{I}|\ ||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}(\tilde{a}_{I})\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I})||_{r}\\
&+\sup_{t}|g_{I}|\ ||\tilde{a}_{I}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I})\cdot\nabla\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}||_{r}.\end{split}

We can now argue as above and deduce that upon taking
a
sufficiently large, we have:

||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}w^{p,p}_{I}||_{r}\leq C_{\bar{N}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}1/\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}

where
C_{\bar{N}}
is a possibly different constant compared to the one above and is independent of
C_{0}
and the other parameters.

Corrector term
w_{I}^{p,c}
.
According to (
3.39
) we have:

\begin{split}w_{I}^{p,c}&=\underbrace{\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,I}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,I})(\tilde{a}_{I})\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{2*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I})}_{T_{1}}\\
&+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}g_{I}\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})}_{T_{2,a}}+\underbrace{\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\ell,I}+\mathcal{A}^{-}_{\ell,I})(\tilde{a}_{I})\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})}_{T_{2,b}}.\end{split}

The estimates on
T_{1}
follow exactly as the ones for
w_{I}^{p,o}
and we omit the details:

\begin{split}&||T_{1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0,\\
&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}T_{1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{c}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0,\end{split}

We now prove bounds for
T_{2,a}
.
T_{2,b}
has the same structure, and we omit the details. From the estimates in Lemmas
3.6
and
3.7
, we deduce:

\begin{split}||T_{2,a}||_{r}&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sup_{I}|g_{I}|\left[||\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r+1}||\Psi_{I}^{1*}\nu_{I}||_{0}||\Psi_{I}^{*}\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}||_{0}+||\tilde{a}_{I}||_{1}||\Psi_{I}^{1*}\nu_{I}||_{r}||\Psi_{I}^{*}\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}||_{0}\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sup_{I}|g_{I}|\ ||\tilde{a}_{I}||_{1}||\Psi_{I}^{1*}\nu_{I}||_{0}||\Psi_{I}^{*}\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}.\\
\end{split}

Given the properties of the chart from Lemma
3.6
, we deduce:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}T_{2,a}||_{r}&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sup_{I}|g_{I}|\left[||[\nabla\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\tilde{a}_{I}]\times\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})||_{r}+||(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm}_{\ell,I})^{\top}\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})||_{r}\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sup_{I}|g_{I}|\ ||\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm}_{\ell,I})^{\top}[\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})]||_{r}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}\tau^{a}}\sup_{I}|\partial_{t}g_{I}|\ ||\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}.\end{split}

Similarly, one can show:

\begin{split}&||T_{2,b}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}T_{2,b}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{c}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right].\end{split}

Gathering the estimates above, we conclude:

\begin{split}||w^{p,c}_{I}||_{r}&\lesssim||T_{1}||_{r}+||T_{2,a}||_{r}+||T_{2,b}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}w^{p,c}_{I}||_{r}&\lesssim||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}T_{1}||_{r}+||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}T_{2,a}||_{r}+||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}T_{2,b}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{c}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(1/\tau^{a})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{c}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{1}{\ell_{t}}\right]\end{split}

for
r\geq 0
, where used that
\tau^{a}=\ell_{t}
. The implicit constants depend on
r
and all the other parameters, but not on
a
.
∎

Remark 18

(Inductive Lemma)
.

We remark that this is exactly the type of vector fields we will use in combination with the Inductive Lemma
8.1
. Indeed, in the computation of the new Reynolds stress, see (
3.61
), we will have a Lie-Taylor expansion in which the Lie-derivation happens repeatedly along:

\begin{split}\sigma_{1}&=\xi_{I}^{p}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}[\underbrace{\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}\tilde{a}_{I}}_{=:\varsigma_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})]\\
\sigma_{2}&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\xi_{I}^{p}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}[\underbrace{(\alpha^{\prime}_{I}\tilde{a}_{I}+\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla)(\tilde{a}_{I}))}_{=:\varsigma_{2}}\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})]\\
\sigma_{3}&=\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\xi_{I}^{p}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}[\underbrace{\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla(\tilde{a}_{I})}_{=:\varsigma_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})]\end{split}

In the notation of Lemma
8.1
, we then have:

\begin{split}&\bar{\varsigma}_{1}=\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\ \bar{\varsigma}_{1,\mathcal{A}}=\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&\bar{\varsigma}_{2}=\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\ \bar{\varsigma}_{2,\mathcal{A}}=[1/\tau^{a}+\tau^{a}/(\tau^{c}\ell_{t})]\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&\bar{\varsigma}_{3}=(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\ \bar{\varsigma}_{2,\mathcal{A}}=[1/\tau^{c}+\tau^{a}/(\tau^{c}\ell_{t})]\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&L^{\varsigma}(r)=\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\\
\end{split}

where
\underline{r}=M-2m_{0}-k_{0}^{g}-8
.

3.5.
Estimates on the Perturbation

To prove estimates on the perturbation
(w^{p},\ b^{p})
, we first need bounds on the various terms appearing in their Lie-Taylor expansion coming from Lemma
5.1
. As for the Galbrun stage, a key quantity is the smallness gain
\mathcal{T}_{p}
corresponding to each additional Lie-derivative. This gain occurs only when the Lie derivative acts on objects with a specific, well-designed geometry. We define a corresponding quantity
\mathcal{M}_{p}
telling us how much we lose when we take a Lie derivative in a direction on which we have no geometric control, e.g.
\tilde{v}_{q}-\tilde{v}_{\ell}
. Note that this is an issue we did not have in the Galbrun stage, where all the quantities were ‘slow’ in space and thus did not produce gradients of order
\lambda_{q+1}
. Not surprisingly,
\mathcal{M}_{p}
corresponds to the
C^{0}
size of
\mathrm{D}\xi^{p}
. More precisely, we set:

(3.41)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{T}_{p}=\lambda_{q}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})(\delta_{q+1}/\delta_{q})^{1/2}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{\gamma_{a}+\beta+\gamma_{CZ}},\\
&\mathcal{M}_{p}=\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{p}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\lambda_{q}}\right)^{1-(\gamma_{a}+\beta+\gamma_{CZ})}.\end{split}

Moreover, to deal with the degrading bounds on
\tilde{v}_{q},\ \tilde{B}_{q}
, we define for
\underline{r}=M-2m_{0}-k_{0}^{g}-8
loss functions
L_{p},L_{p,\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
with:

(3.42)

\begin{split}L_{p}(r)&=\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}},\\
L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r)&=1_{r\leq\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-2}+1_{\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-1\leq r\leq\underline{r}-1}\left[1+\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-r}\bar{L}\right]+1_{r\geq\underline{r}}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\bar{L}.\\
\end{split}

These are nothing but a shift of
m_{0}+k_{0}^{g}+2
of the loss functions appearing in Lemma
2.22
. This is not strictly necessary, but it simplifies the estimates by ensuring that:

(3.43)

L_{p}(r)\leq L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r)

where the left-hand side is the loss function associated with the Alfvén transport of the slow-coefficients
\mathcal{\tilde{A}}_{\ell,I}^{\pm}\tilde{a}_{I}
from Lemma
3.7
.

We now show that
L_{p,\mathcal{A}}
, is an admissible loss function in the sense of Definition
8.1
, we need only verify that:

\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r+r^{\prime})\leq L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r)\ \text{ for }\ r,r^{\prime}\geq 0.

Let:

I_{1}=[0,\ \underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-2]\cap\mathbb{N},\ \ \ \ I_{2}=[\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-1,\ \underline{r}-1]\cap\mathbb{N},\ \ \ \ I_{3}=\{r\geq\underline{r}\}\cap\mathbb{N}

and we recall that because of our choice of parameters in
1.5
, see (
1.24
), (
1.32
), we have:

\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{k_{0}^{g}}\bar{L}\leq 1/2,\ \ \ \ 1-(\gamma_{a}+2\beta+\gamma_{\ell}+2\gamma_{CZ})\geq 0,\ \ \ \ \frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\leq\frac{1}{2}

We now distinguish the following cases:

Case
r\in I_{1}
.
If
r+r^{\prime}\in I_{1}
, the claim is trivial, if
r+r^{\prime}\in I_{2}
we have
r^{\prime}\geq 1,\ \underline{r}-1-r\geq k_{0}^{g}+1\geq k_{0}^{g}
and we can bound:

\begin{split}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r+r^{\prime})&=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}\left[1+\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-(r+r^{\prime})}\bar{L}\right]\\
&\leq\left[\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}+\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}\underbrace{[1-(\gamma_{a}+2\beta+\gamma_{\ell}+2\gamma_{CZ})]}_{\geq 0}}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-r}\bar{L}\right]\\
&\leq\left[\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}+\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{k_{0}^{g}}\bar{L}\right]\\
&\leq 1=L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r).\end{split}

If
r+r^{\prime}\in I_{3}
since
r\in I_{1}
we have
r^{\prime}\geq k_{0}^{g},\ r\leq\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-2\leq\underline{r}-1
and thus:

\begin{split}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r+r^{\prime})&=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{[r+r^{\prime}-(\underline{r}-1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\bar{L}\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}(1-\gamma_{\ell})}\lambda_{q}^{\underbrace{[r-(\underline{r}-1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}_{\leq 0}}\bar{L}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{k_{0}^{g}(\gamma_{a}+2\beta+2\gamma_{CZ})}\bar{L}\\
&\leq\mathcal{T}_{g}^{k_{0}^{g}}\bar{L}\\
&\leq 1=L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r)\end{split}

where we used that by definition, see (
2.38
), we have:

\mathcal{T}_{g}=\lambda_{q}^{2}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\geq\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{\gamma_{a}+2\beta+2\gamma_{CZ}}.

Case
r\in I_{2}
.
If
r+r^{\prime}\in I_{2}
, similarly as above we compute:

\begin{split}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r+r^{\prime})&=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}\left[1+\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-(r+r^{\prime})}\bar{L}\right]\\
&\leq\left[\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}+\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}\underbrace{[1-(\gamma_{a}+2\beta+\gamma_{\ell}+2\gamma_{CZ})]}_{\geq 0}}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-r}\bar{L}\right]\\
&\leq\left[1+\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-r}\bar{L}\right]\\
&=L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r).\end{split}

If
r+r^{\prime}\in I_{3}
since
r\in I_{2}
we have
r^{\prime}\geq\underline{r}-r\geq\underline{r}-1-r,\ r\leq\underline{r}-1
and we compute:

\begin{split}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r+r^{\prime})&=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{[r+r^{\prime}-(\underline{r}-1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\bar{L}\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}(1-\gamma_{\ell})}\lambda_{q}^{\underbrace{[r-(\underline{r}-1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}_{\leq 0}}\bar{L}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{(\underline{r}-1-r)(\gamma_{a}+2\beta+2\gamma_{CZ})}\bar{L}\\
&\leq\mathcal{T}_{g}^{\underline{r}-1-r}\bar{L}\\
&\leq 1+\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-r}\bar{L}=L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r).\end{split}

3.5.1.
Estimates on the Lagrangian Perturbation.

Let
X^{p}:[-1,1]_{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\to\mathbb{T}^{3}
solve:

(3.44)

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}X_{s}^{p}(x,t)=\xi^{p}(X_{s}^{p}(x,t),t),\\
X_{0}^{p}(x,t)=x.\end{cases}

In this Subsection, we want to improve on the usual Grönwall bound:

||\mathrm{D}X^{p}_{s}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\leq e^{\int_{0}^{s}||\mathrm{D}\xi||_{0}\mathrm{d}s}-1

this is needed because,
|s|\leq 1
and
||\mathrm{D}\xi^{p}||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}
blows-up along the scheme

\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\gg\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}},

the key idea is that we have better control over the
k
-th momentum of
\xi^{p}
for any
k\geq 1
, relative to its full differential, namely

||(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k}\xi^{p}||_{0}\ll||\mathrm{D}^{k}\xi^{p}||_{0}

this observation is useful because a Taylor expansion of
X^{p}_{s}
in the parameter
s
at
s=0
, gives:

(3.45)

X_{s}^{p}=\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{s^{k}}{k!}\partial_{s}^{k}X_{s}^{p}|_{s=0}+r^{k_{0},s}_{X}=\mathrm{Id}+s\xi^{p}+\sum_{k=2}^{k_{0}}\frac{s^{k}}{k!}(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k-1}\xi^{p}+r^{k_{0},s}_{X}

where the remainder can be explicitly written as:

r^{k_{0},s}_{X}=\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{s}^{k_{0}+1}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}=\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

We now show that we indeed have better control over the
k
-th momentum.

Lemma 3.11

(Momentum Estimates)
.

Let
\zeta
be any constant unit vector and
\bar{N}\geq 2
a non-negative integer. Set
\underline{r}=M-2m_{0}-k_{0}^{g}-8
. Then there exist constants
C,\ C^{\prime}
such that for

j=0,1,2,\ \ \ \ 0\leq r+j+k\leq\bar{N}

the following bounds hold:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k}\xi^{p}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathcal{L}^{k}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\zeta||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\mathcal{M}_{p}\\
\end{split}

The constants,
C,\ C^{\prime}
depend on
\bar{N}
and all the parameters but not on
a
and are uniform in
k,\ r,\ j,\ \zeta
.

Proof of Lemma
3.11
.

The second statement is a simple application of the Inductive Lemma
8.1
. We omit the details, as the same type of argument will appear several times in the proof of Lemma
3.13
.

The first statement follows from a slight variation of the proof of Lemma
8.1
. By means of the disjointedness properties from Lemma
3.9
and the definition of
\xi_{I}^{p}
in (
3.34
), we first write:

\begin{split}&(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k}\xi^{p}=\sum_{I}(\xi_{I}^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k}\xi_{I}^{p},\\
&\xi_{I}^{p}=\tau^{a}\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\tilde{\Psi}^{2*}_{I}\left[\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I}\right]+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I}).\end{split}

We prove the claim by induction; the
k=0
case follows from Lemmas
3.7
,
3.6
and the representation above, now assume that:

F_{I,k}=(\xi^{p}_{I}\cdot\nabla)^{k}\xi^{p}_{I}\simeq\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]Y_{I,k}=\phi_{k}(\lambda_{q+1}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}\cdot k_{I})Y_{I,k}

with

||\partial_{t}^{j}Y_{I,k}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[r+k+j-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ and }\ ||\phi_{k}||_{r}\lesssim 1

where we use the same convention for
\simeq
used in the proof of Lemma
8.1
to indicate a finite sum of terms of that structure, and obeying the same estimates. It will be apparent that the number of these grows at most exponentially in
k
, and this is the reason for the shape of the implicit constant, claimed in the statement of the Lemma.

We are interested in the bounds for
F_{I,k+1}
, we compute:

\begin{split}F_{I,k+1}&=\xi_{I}\cdot\nabla F_{I,k}\\
&=\left[\tau^{a}\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\tilde{\Psi}^{2*}_{I}\left[\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I}\right]+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\right]\cdot\nabla\left[\phi_{k}(\lambda_{q+1}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}\cdot k_{I})Y_{I,k}\right]\\
&=\left[\lambda_{q+1}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k}^{\prime})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]Y_{I,k}\otimes\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}k_{I}+\Psi_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]\mathrm{D}Y_{I,k}\right]\left[\tau^{a}\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\tilde{\Psi}^{2*}_{I}\left[\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}^{\prime}\zeta_{I}\right]\right]\\
&+\left[\lambda_{q+1}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k}^{\prime})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]Y_{I,k}\otimes\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}k_{I}+\Psi_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]\mathrm{D}Y_{I,k}\right]\left[\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\right]\\
&=\Psi_{I}^{*}[(\varphi^{\prime}\phi_{k})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]\tau^{a}\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}(\tilde{\Psi}^{2*}_{I}\zeta_{I}\cdot\nabla)Y_{I,k}\\
&+\Psi_{I}^{*}[(\varphi\phi_{k}^{\prime})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}(\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}\nu_{I})\cdot\Psi_{I}^{1*}k_{I}Y_{I,k}+\Psi_{I}^{*}[(\varphi\phi_{k})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\alpha_{I}(\nabla\tilde{a}_{I}\times\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}\nu_{I})\cdot\nabla Y_{I,k}\end{split}

The key point here is that the main term in
\xi_{I}^{p}
, because of its geometry, kills the
\lambda_{q+1}
term in
\mathrm{D}F_{k}
, while the corrector term has a
\lambda_{q}/\lambda_{q+1}
embedded smallness compensating for the loss. From the computation above, it is clear that
F_{I,k+1}
can be written as:

F_{I,k+1}\simeq\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k+1})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]Y_{I,k+1}

and from Lemmas
3.6
,
3.7
it follows that:

||Y_{I,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[k+1-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k+1}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ and }\ ||\phi_{k+1}||_{r}\lesssim 1.

Commuting time and space derivatives from the calculations above and the estimates in Lemmas
3.6
,
3.7
, again, we conclude:

||\partial_{t}^{j}Y_{I,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[k+1+j-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k+1}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \ \&\ \ 0\leq r+(k+1)+j\leq\bar{N}.

In particular, by means of the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
and Lemma
3.6
, for
r\geq 1
we deduce:

\begin{split}||F_{k,I}||_{r}&\lesssim||\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]Y_{I,k}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim||\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]||_{r}||Y_{I,k}||_{0}+||\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{*}[(\phi_{k})_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}]||_{0}||Y_{I,k}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\left[\lambda_{q+1}||\phi_{k}||_{1}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}||_{r-1}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}||\phi_{k}||_{r}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}||_{0}^{r}\right]||Y_{I,k}||_{0}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{p}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\end{split}

similarly, one can show:

||\partial_{t}^{j}F_{k,I}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{p}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}.

From the fact that for each spacetime point there is at most one
\xi_{I}^{p}
which is not zero, and the bound we just showed, we conclude that:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{j}(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k}\xi^{p}||_{r}&\leq\sup_{I}||\partial_{t}^{j}(\xi_{I}^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k}\xi_{I}^{p}||_{r}\\
&=\sup_{I}||\partial_{t}^{j}F_{k,I}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{p}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\end{split}

where the implicit constant is of the form
C^{\prime}(C)^{k+1}
for some
C,\ C^{\prime}
which depend on
\bar{N}
and all the other parameters but not on
a
and are uniform in
k,\ r,\ j
.
∎

We are ready to prove bounds for the Lagrangian perturbation.

Lemma 3.12

(Estimates on
X^{p}
)
.

Let
\bar{N}
be a non-negative integer and
\zeta
any constant unit vector. For
j=0,1,2
and any
|s|\leq 1
, we have:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}X^{p}_{s}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \ \text{ for }\ \ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}+1-j\ \ \&\ \ (r,j)\neq(0,0)\\
&||\partial_{t}^{j}\left[\mathrm{D}X^{p}\circ(X^{p})^{-1}[\zeta]\right]||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j+1}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}=\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\mathcal{M}_{p}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-j\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants depend on
\bar{N},\ r
and all the other parameters but not on
a
.

What happens here is that, thanks to Lemma
3.11
and the assumptions, we have:

\begin{split}||(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}||_{1}\lesssim\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}\end{split}

and in particular, choosing
k_{0}
sufficiently large, we can ensure

\frac{1}{k_{0}!}||(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}||_{1}\leq 1,

this allows us to run the usual Grönwall argument to control derivatives of the Lagrangian flow of a vector field, but at the level of the first derivative of the
k_{0}
momentum of the vector field instead of the derivative of the vector field itself.

Proof of Lemma
3.12
.

We first recall the expressions of
\mathcal{T}_{p}
and
\mathcal{M}_{p}
from (
3.41
). Now, given any
b,\ \gamma_{a},\ \gamma_{\ell},\ \bar{N}
we can find
k_{0}
so large that for
0\leq r\leq\bar{N}
we have:

(3.46)

\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{r+2}\leq\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{\bar{N}+2}\leq\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{k_{0}(\beta+\gamma_{a}-\gamma_{\ell})+(\bar{N}+2)(\beta+\gamma_{a}-\gamma_{\ell}-1)}\overset{!}{\leq}1

We now let
C,C^{\prime}
be the implicit constants from Lemma
3.11
, applied with
k_{0}+\bar{N}+3
being the total number of derivatives on which we want the estimates (this number is called
\bar{N}
in the statement of
3.11
, not to be confused with the one we are given as data now). In this sense, we say that
C,\ C^{\prime}
depend on
\bar{N}
and all the other parameters.

For notational convenience we assume
0\leq s\leq 1
the case
-1\leq s\leq 0
can be handled similarly. The composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
will be used repeatedly without mention.

Estimates on the Lagrangian Perturbation.
By taking one derivative of the expression of
X_{s}^{p}-\mathrm{Id}
given in (
3.45
) we can estimate:

(3.47)

\begin{split}||\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{s^{k}}{k!}\left[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k-1}\xi^{p}\right]_{1}+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}\right]_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}\right]_{1}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{p}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}+1}\frac{s^{k}}{k!}(C)^{k-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-1-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k-1}\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{[k_{0}-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\underbrace{\frac{1}{k_{0}!}(C)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}}_{\lesssim 1}\int_{0}^{s}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{p}\underbrace{\sum_{k=+1}^{k_{0}}\frac{\left(C\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k-1}}{k!}}_{\lesssim 1}+\int_{0}^{s}\left[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}\right]_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{p}+\int_{0}^{s}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
\end{split}

An application of Grönwall Lemma gives:

\begin{split}||\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}&\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{p}\end{split}

and the case
r=0
for pure space derivatives is proved.

For the
r\geq 1
case, we can bound the remainder as follows:

\begin{split}||r_{X}^{k_{0},s}||_{r}&\leq\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}||(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})||_{r}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}]_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r-1}+||(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}||_{r+1}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}^{r}\right]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{k_{0}!}(C)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}\int_{0}^{s}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}(C)^{k_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{r+1}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{k_{0}!}(C)^{k_{0}}\int_{0}^{s}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}(C)^{k_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\mathcal{M}_{p}\end{split}

where we used Lemma
3.11
together with the case
r=0
and (
3.46
), we conclude that:

\begin{split}||\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}||_{r}&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{1}{k!}||(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k-1}\xi^{p}||_{r+1}+||r^{k_{0},s}_{X}||_{r+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\mathcal{M}_{p}\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{1}{k!}(C)^{k-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-1-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}^{k-1}_{p}}_{\lesssim 1}+\int_{0}^{s}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\mathcal{M}_{p}+\int_{0}^{s}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
\end{split}

for
0\leq r\leq\bar{N}
, where we argued as above, to address the sum. We now apply Grönwall Lemma and conclude:

||\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\mathcal{M}_{p}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq s\leq 1\ \ \&\ \ 1\leq r\leq\bar{N}.

For the pure time derivatives case, we first compute:

(3.48)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}r^{k_{0},s}_{X}&=\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\partial_{t}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})+\mathrm{D}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})\left[\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}\right]\right](s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},\\
\partial_{t}^{2}r^{k_{0},s}_{X}&=\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\partial_{t}^{2}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})+\mathrm{D}^{2}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})\left[\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p},\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}\right]\right](s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[2\mathrm{D}[\partial_{t}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}]](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})\left[\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}\right]+\mathrm{D}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})\left[\partial_{t}^{2}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}\right]\right](s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.\end{split}

From the bounds in Lemma
3.11
, we deduce:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}r^{k_{0},s}_{X}||_{r}&\lesssim\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})||_{r}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[||\mathrm{D}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})||_{r}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}](X_{s^{\prime}}^{p})||_{0}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}\right](s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\overset{r=0}{\leq}\frac{s^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}C^{k_{0}}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}C^{k_{0}}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[||\partial_{t}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}||_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}+||\partial_{t}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}^{r}\right](s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[||\mathrm{D}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}]||_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r-1}+||\mathrm{D}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}]||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}^{r}\right]||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}||\mathrm{D}[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k_{0}}\xi^{p}]||_{0}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\frac{s^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}C^{k_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{r+1}+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}C^{k_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{r+1}\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}C^{k_{0}}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{p}\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
\end{split}

and given our choice of
k_{0}
in (
3.46
) we conclude:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}r^{k_{0},s}_{X}||_{r}&\lesssim\begin{cases}\mathcal{M}_{p}+\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}&\ \text{ for }\ r=0,\\
\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\mathcal{M}_{p}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}&\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1.\end{cases}\end{split}

We now take the time derivative of the full expression in (
3.45
), we plug the bound just found and the ones in Lemma
3.11
and deduce:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}X_{s}^{p}||_{r}&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{s^{k}}{k!}||\partial_{t}\left[(\xi^{p}\cdot\nabla)^{k-1}\xi^{p}\right]||_{r}+||\partial_{t}r^{k_{0},s}_{X}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\mathcal{M}_{p}+\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{s}||\partial_{t}X_{s^{\prime}}^{p}||_{r}(s-s^{\prime})^{k}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
\end{split}

where we argued, as in (
3.47
), to deal with the sum in the parameter
k
. An application of Grönwall Lemma, first for the case
r=0,\ 0\leq s\leq 1
and then for the case
0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-1,\ 0\leq s\leq 1
, allows us to conclude that:

||\partial_{t}X_{s}^{p}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\mathcal{M}_{p}=\lambda_{q+1}^{r+1}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq s\leq 1\ \ \&\ \ 1\leq r\leq\bar{N}-1,

now arguing as above, but making use of the expression for
\partial_{t}^{2}
in (
3.48
) instead of
\partial_{t}
one can show:

||\partial_{t}^{2}X_{s}^{p}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+1}\mathcal{M}_{p}=\lambda_{q+1}^{r+2}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq s\leq 1\ \ \&\ \ 1\leq r\leq\bar{N}-2

where the implicit constants in the bounds above depend on
\bar{N},\ r
and all the other parameters, but not on
a
and
s
for
0\leq s\leq 1
.

Estimates on the push-forward of a constant vector.
To prove the second assertion of the Lemma, we argue similarly. For any constant vector
\zeta
we have:

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}X^{p}((X^{p})^{-1})[\zeta]&=(X^{p})_{*}\zeta\\
&=\zeta+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k}\zeta+\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta(1-s)^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\zeta+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k}\zeta+\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta(X_{-s}^{p})](1-s)^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

where the expansion can be deduced from the ideas behind Lemma
5.1
and we used that since
\xi^{p}
is independent of
s
,
X_{s}
has the semi-group property
X_{s}\circ X_{s^{\prime}}=X_{s+s^{\prime}}
and in particular we have
X_{s}^{-1}=X_{-s}
.

From the results above for
1\leq r\leq\bar{N}
we deduce:

\begin{split}||[\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})]||_{r}&\lesssim[\mathrm{D}X^{p}]_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{-s}^{p}||_{r-1}+||\mathrm{D}X^{p}||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X_{-s}^{p}||_{0}^{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{r+1}_{p},\\
||(\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta)(X^{p}_{-s})||_{r}&\lesssim[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta]_{1}||\mathrm{D}X^{p}_{-s}||_{r-1}+||\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X^{p}_{-s}||_{0}^{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}^{r+1}_{p},\\
\end{split}

moreover,

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})||_{r}&\leq||\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}X_{s}^{p})(X_{-s}^{p})||_{r}+||\mathrm{D}^{2}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})[\partial_{t}X_{-s}^{p},\cdot]||_{r}\\
&\lesssim[\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}X_{s}^{p})]_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{-1}^{p}||_{r-1}+||\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}X_{s}^{p})||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X_{-1}^{p}||_{0}^{r}\\
&+||\mathrm{D}^{2}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})||_{r}||\partial_{t}X_{-s}^{p}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}^{2}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})||_{0}||\partial_{t}X_{-s}^{p}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+1}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{r+1}+\left[||\mathrm{D}^{2}X_{s}^{p}||_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{-s}^{p}||_{r-1}+||\mathrm{D}^{2}X_{s}^{p}||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X_{-s}^{p}||_{0}^{r}\right]\mathcal{M}_{p}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+1}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{r+2},\\
||\partial_{t}[(\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta)(X^{p}_{-s})]||_{r}&\lesssim[\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta]_{1}||\mathrm{D}X^{p}_{-s}||_{r-1}+||\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X^{p}_{-s}||_{0}^{r}+||\mathrm{D}[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta](X_{-s}^{p})[\partial_{t}X_{-s}^{p}]||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+1}(C)^{k_{0}}\mathcal{M}^{r+1}_{p}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}+||\mathrm{D}[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta||_{0}||\partial_{t}X_{-s}^{p}||_{r}\\
&+\left[[\mathrm{D}[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta]_{1}||\mathrm{D}X_{-s}^{p}||_{r-1}+||\mathrm{D}[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta||_{r}||\mathrm{D}X_{-s}^{p}||_{0}^{r}\right]||\partial_{t}X_{-s}^{p}||_{0}\\
&\lesssim(C)^{k_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+1}\mathcal{M}^{r+2}_{p}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}},\\
\end{split}

similarly,

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{2}\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})||_{r}&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+2}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{r+3},\\
||\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta)(X^{p}_{-s})||_{r}&\lesssim(C)^{k_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+2}\mathcal{M}^{r+3}_{p}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right)^{k_{0}}.\\
\end{split}

Given our choice of
k_{0}
in (
3.46
), we deduce that:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{j}[\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})(\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta)(X^{p}_{-s})]||_{r}\lesssim(C)^{k_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\mathcal{M}_{p}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-j.\end{split}

We are ready to estimate:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{j}[\mathrm{D}X^{p}\circ(X^{p})^{-1}\zeta-\zeta]||_{r}&\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{1}{k!}||\partial_{t}^{j}[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k}\zeta]||_{r}+\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{1}||\partial_{t}^{j}[\mathrm{D}X_{s}^{p}(X_{-s}^{p})[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}}^{k_{0}+1}\zeta(X_{-s}^{p})]]||_{r}(1-s)^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\mathcal{M}_{p}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}-1}\frac{1}{(k+1)!}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{[k-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}^{k}_{p}+\frac{1}{(k_{0}+1)!}C^{k_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\mathcal{M}_{p}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\mathcal{M}_{p}\\
\end{split}

for
0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-j
. The implicit constant depends on
\bar{N},\ r
and all the other parameters, but not on
a
.
∎

Remark 19
.

The relevance of the second claim in Lemma
3.12
can be seen from the following computation. We will write
|\cdot|_{op}
for the operator norm on matrices, we have:

\begin{split}[\mathrm{D}X\circ X^{-1}]_{r}&=\sum_{|\theta|=r}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{D}X\circ X^{-1}|_{op}\\
&=\sum_{|\theta|=r}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sup_{|\zeta|=1}|\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{D}X\circ X^{-1}[\zeta]|\\
&=\sum_{|\theta|=r}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sup_{|\zeta|=1}|\partial_{\theta}(\mathrm{D}X\circ X^{-1}[\zeta])|.\\
\end{split}

It thus suffices to estimate
|\partial_{\theta}(\mathrm{D}X\circ X^{-1}\zeta)|
independently of
\zeta
with
|\zeta|=1
as in the Lemma
3.12
to deduce bounds for
||\mathrm{D}X\circ X^{-1}||_{r}
.

3.5.2.
Estimates on the Lie-Taylor Expansion.

In this Subsection, we provide bounds on all the objects in the decomposition of the perturbation from Subsection
3.4.1
. The key tool for studying these Lie-Taylor expansions is the Inductive Lemma
8.1
, which guarantees estimates for all
k\geq 1
from those for
k=0
and careful bookkeeping of the geometry. We invite the reader to visit Section
8
, where this machinery is developed, before proceeding.

Lemma 3.13

(Lie-Derivative and Transport Estimates - Mollified Terms)
.

Fix
\bar{N}\geq 0
an integer, let
j,\ k,\ r\geq 0
also integers,
I\in\mathcal{I}
and
\underline{r}=M-2m_{0}-k_{0}^{g}-8
. There exist constants
C,\ C^{\prime}
which depend on
\bar{N}
and all the parameters but not on
a
and are uniform in
I,\ j,\ k,\ r
, such that the following estimates hold.

Lie-derivatives bounds.
For
j=0,1,2
and
0\leq r+k+j\leq\bar{N}
, we have:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell}-\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r},\ ||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell}-\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}.\end{split}

Alfvén-transport bounds.
For
0\leq r+k\leq\bar{N}-1
, we have:

\begin{split}&||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}_{\ell,I}^{\pm}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{1}{\ell_{t}}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\right],\\
&||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}_{\ell,I}^{\pm}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{1}{\ell_{t}}\right],\\
&||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}_{\ell,I}^{\pm}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell}-\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r},\ ||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}_{\ell,I}^{\pm}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell}-\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}(\ell\lambda_{q})^{m_{0}}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}.\end{split}

Proof of Lemma
3.13
.

We first study the transport terms and then move to the mollification correction terms. We will adopt a notation similar to the one appearing in the Inductive Lemma
8.1
.

Transport Terms.
Given the Ansatz (
3.33
), and the properties in Lemma
3.6
we can explicitly compute:

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}&=\frac{\tau^{a}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\left[\tilde{a}_{I}\alpha_{I}\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\tilde{a}_{I}g_{I}\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})+\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla)(\tilde{a}_{I})\tilde{\Psi}_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I})\right]\\
\end{split}

where we used
\alpha_{I}^{\prime}=g_{I}
, see Lemma
2.8
. In particular, in the notation of Lemma
8.1
, we see that:

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}=F_{0}=a\Psi^{1*}(\phi\nu)

where

a=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\tilde{a}_{I}g_{I}+\tau^{a}\alpha_{I}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}\cdot\nabla)(\tilde{a}_{I})\right]\ \text{ and }\ \Psi^{1*}(\phi\nu)=\Psi_{I}^{1*}(\varphi_{\lambda_{q+1},k_{I}}\nu_{I}),

the transport properties and estimates on the chart are guaranteed by Lemma
3.6
, which in combination with Lemma
3.7
also gives:

\begin{split}\lambda_{q+1}||\partial_{t}^{j}a||_{r}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j+[r+j-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
\lambda_{q+1}||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}a||_{r}&\lesssim\sup_{t}|g_{I}|\ ||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r}+1/\tau^{a}\sup_{t}|g_{I}^{\prime}|\ ||\tilde{a}_{I}||_{r}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{2}\sup_{t}|\alpha_{I}|\ ||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}(\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{+}_{\ell,I}+\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{-}_{\ell,I})(\tilde{a}_{I})||_{r}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}|\alpha_{I}^{\prime}|\ ||(\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{+}_{\ell,I}+\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{-}_{\ell,I})(\tilde{a}_{I})||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+(1/\tau^{a})\lambda_{q}^{r+[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+\tau^{a}/(\tau^{c}\ell_{t})\lambda_{q}^{r+[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\lambda_{q}^{r+[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\end{split}

we now apply Lemma
8.1
with:

\begin{split}&z^{\pm}=\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I},\\
&\sigma_{i}=\sigma=\xi_{I},\\
&\bar{\varsigma}_{i}=\bar{\varsigma}=\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\ \bar{\varsigma}_{i,\mathcal{A}}=\bar{\varsigma}_{\mathcal{A}}=\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&L^{1}(r)=L^{\varsigma}(r)=L^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(r)=\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}},\\
&A_{1}=\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\ A_{1,\mathcal{A}}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
&A_{3}=A_{3,\mathcal{A}}=0.\end{split}

We are allowed to choose
\bar{N}
in the statement of the Lemma as large as we like, as all the terms are mollified, and we can set
\bar{C}
to be the largest of the implicit constants in the estimates above and in Lemma
3.7
, for
0\leq r\leq\bar{N}
multiplied by the number of terms in the definition of
a
, that is, two. The Lemma gives decompositions:

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}&=F_{k}\simeq a_{k}\tilde{\Psi}^{1*}_{I}(\phi_{k}\nu_{I}),\\
(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{z}_{\ell,I}^{\pm}})\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{z}_{\ell,I}^{\pm}})F_{k}\simeq a_{k,\mathcal{A}}\tilde{\Psi}^{1*}_{I}(\phi_{k}\nu_{I})\\
\end{split}

where abusing notation, we do not rename the fast coefficients, together with the estimates:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{j}a_{k}||_{r}&\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+k+j}L(r+k+j)A_{1}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}=C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q}^{r+k+j+[r+k+j-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\tau^{a})^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{(k+1)/2}\\
&=C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q}^{r+j+[r+k+j-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
\end{split}

for
0\leq r+k+j\leq\bar{N}
and
j=0,1,2
, moreover

\begin{split}||a_{k,\mathcal{A}}||_{r}&\leq C^{\prime}(C^{k})\lambda_{q}^{r+k}\left[L_{\mathcal{A}}(k+r)A_{1,\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}+L(k+r)A_{1}\max_{j\in\{1,\dots,k\}}\bar{\varsigma}_{j,\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1,\dots,k\ i\neq j}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\right]\\
&\lesssim(C)^{k}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\lambda_{q}^{r+k+[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\tau^{a})^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{(k+1)/2}\\
&+(C)^{k}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q}^{r+k+[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\tau^{a})^{k-1}\delta_{q+1}^{(k+1)/2}\\
&\lesssim(C)^{k}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\lambda_{q}^{r+[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
\end{split}

for
0\leq r+k\leq\bar{N}-1
and finally:

||\partial_{t}^{j}\Psi^{1*}_{I}(\phi_{k}\nu_{I})||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0\text{ and }j=0,1,2.

We conclude that:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}&\lesssim||a_{k}||_{r}||\tilde{\Psi}^{1*}_{I}(\phi_{k}\nu_{I})||_{0}+||a_{k}||_{0}||\tilde{\Psi}^{1*}_{I}(\phi_{k}\nu_{I})||_{r}\\
&\lesssim(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}+(C^{k})\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\leq(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\underbrace{\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r(1-\gamma_{\ell})}}_{\leq 1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&+(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
\end{split}

for
0\leq r+k\leq\bar{N}
. Similarly, applying
\partial_{t}^{j}
for
j=1,2
and using the product rule, together with the estimates above, one can show:

||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}\lesssim(C^{k})\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}

for
0\leq r+k+j\leq\bar{N}
, moreover

\begin{split}||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}&\leq||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{z}_{\ell,I}^{\pm}})\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}+||\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}\cdot\nabla\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim||a_{k,\mathcal{A}}||_{r}||\tilde{\Psi}^{1*}_{I}(\phi_{k}\nu_{I})||_{0}+||a_{k,\mathcal{A}}||_{0}||\tilde{\Psi}^{1*}_{I}(\phi_{k}\nu_{I})||_{r}\\
&+||\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}||\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}||_{1}+||\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{0}||\tilde{z}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}||_{r+1}\\
&\lesssim(C)^{k}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\lambda_{q}^{r+[r+k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&+(C)^{k}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\lambda_{q+1}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&+(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}(1/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\end{split}

for
0\leq r+k\leq\bar{N}-1
. The implicit constants and
C
in the above statements depend on
\bar{N}
and all the other parameters, but not on
a
and are uniform in
I,\ r,\ j,\ k,
satisfying the above constraints.

Proceeding exactly as above and given the extra
\tau^{a}/\tau^{c}
smallness in
\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}
one can show that:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}&\lesssim(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}^{1/2},\\
||\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{\pm}_{\ell,I}\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}||_{r}&\lesssim(C)^{k}\lambda_{q+1}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[k-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{c}}+\frac{\tau^{a}}{\tau^{c}\ell_{t}}\right]\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\end{split}

where the same ranges of parameters and remarks about the constants apply.

Mollification correction terms.
The proof of these bounds is contained in that of Lemma
3.15
, with the following differences:

•

Here we can set
\bar{N}
as large as we like, while in
3.15
we pick the largest possible number of derivatives given our inductive assumptions, namely
\bar{N}=N-m_{0}-1
.

•

In
3.15
the loss function for the Alfvén transport is more complex due to the presence of the non-mollified terms, here we can set

L_{\mathcal{A}}(r)=\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}},

which can be handled as above.

∎

Lemma 3.14

(Remainders - Nash Stage)
.

For
k_{0}\geq 0
let:

\begin{split}\theta^{k_{0}}_{w}&=\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}+1}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}})\Theta^{p}_{I}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\theta^{k_{0}}_{b}&=\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta^{p}_{I}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\theta^{k_{0}}_{c}&=\begin{cases}\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}_{\ell}-\tilde{B}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\sum_{I}\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s}^{p})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi^{p}_{I}}^{k_{0}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{v}_{\ell}-\tilde{v}_{\ell,I}}\Theta_{I}^{p}\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s.\\
\end{cases}\end{split}

Fix
\bar{N}
a non-negative integer and
\underline{r}=M-2m_{0}-k_{0}^{g}-8
. There exist constants
C,C^{\prime}
, such that, for
r\geq 0
integer and
j=0,1,2
satisfying
0\leq r+(k_{0}+1)+j\leq\bar{N}
, we have:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{k_{0}}_{w}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}\frac{(C)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+2)!}\lambda_{q+1}^{r+j}\mathcal{M}_{p}^{r+j+1}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q}^{[k_{0}+1-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{p}^{k_{0}+1}\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\right],\\
