\bar{\bar{v}}(x)=\cos(\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}x\cdot e_{3})\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x\cdot e_{2})e_{1},\\
\bar{B}=e_{3}\end{cases}\leadsto\begin{cases}v_{0}=\eta\bar{v}+f\bar{\bar{v}},\\
B_{0}=X_{*}\bar{B}.\end{cases}

Second Example.
The following example leads to a solution that does not conserve energy and cross helicity.

\begin{cases}\bar{v}(x)=\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x\cdot e_{1})e_{2},\\
\bar{B}(x)=e_{3}+\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x\cdot e_{1})e_{2}\end{cases}\leadsto\begin{cases}v_{0}=\eta\bar{v},\\
B_{0}=X_{*}\bar{B}.\end{cases}

Third Example.
The following example leads to a solution that does not conserve energy and has non-trivial magnetic helicity.

\begin{cases}\bar{v}(x)=\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x\cdot e_{3})e_{1}+\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x\cdot e_{3})e_{2},\\
\bar{B}(x)=\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x\cdot e_{3})e_{1}-\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x\cdot e_{3})e_{2}\\
\end{cases}\leadsto\begin{cases}v_{0}=\eta\bar{v},\\
B_{0}=X_{*}\bar{B}.\end{cases}

Note that one should, in principle, take the integer part in the arguments of the functions above to make the objects well defined on
\mathbb{T}^{3}
; we omit this detail. We remark that the above setups cannot arise as reformulations of ideal MHD as a
2\frac{1}{2}
- Euler flow, either because of parallel gradients or the geometry of the vector fields themselves. We now check that these choices of initial data give rise to subsolutions satisfying the Iterative Assumptions (
1.16
) and the claimed non-conservation of energy/cross helicity. Combinations of the above are also possible. We give explicit computations for the first and second examples only.

Details of the first example.
Note that:

e_{3}\cdot\nabla\bar{v}=0\ \text{ and }\ (e_{3}\cdot\nabla\bar{\bar{v}})(x)=-\lambda^{\parallel}\sin(\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}x_{3})\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{1})e_{2}.

We will use
\bar{v}
to show that the energy is not conserved, while
\bar{\bar{v}}
is there to show that one can accommodate for the presence of parallel gradients, which can be as large as the time we apply the vector field for, in the sense that:

f^{\prime}\sim\lambda_{0},

see the definition of
f(t)
in (
4.8
).

We can compute
X_{t}
explicitly, indeed

X_{t}=\mathrm{Id}+\eta^{[1]}\bar{v}+f^{[1]}\bar{\bar{v}}\ \text{ and }\ X_{t}^{-1}=\mathrm{Id}-\eta^{[1]}\bar{v}-f^{[1]}\bar{\bar{v}}

where

\eta^{[1]}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\eta(s)\mathrm{d}s\ \text{ and }\ f^{[1]}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}f(s)\mathrm{d}s.

In particular, we have:

\begin{split}&f^{[1]}(t)=0\ \text{ for }t\leq 1\ \&\ t\geq 2\ \text{ and }\ |f^{[1]}(t)|\leq 1/\lambda_{0}^{\parallel},\\
&\eta^{[1]}(t)=-t\ \text{ for }t\leq 0\ \text{ and }\eta^{[1]}(t)=\text{const}\ \text{ for }t\geq 1.\end{split}

By construction,
B_{0}
solves:

\partial_{t}B_{0}+\mathrm{curl}[B_{0}\times v_{0}]=0,\ \mathrm{div}\ B_{0}=0.

Moreover, we can explicitly compute:

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}X_{t}(x)&=\mathrm{Id}-\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{2})\eta^{[1]}(t)e_{1}\otimes e_{2}\\
&-\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\cos(\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}x_{3})\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{2})f^{[1]}(t)e_{1}\otimes e_{2}-\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}\sin(\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}x_{3})\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{2})f^{[1]}(t)e_{1}\otimes e_{3},\\
B_{0}(x,t)&=\mathrm{D}X_{t}\circ X_{t}^{-1}(x)[e_{3}]=e_{3}-\sin(\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}x_{3})\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{2})\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}f^{[1]}(t)e_{1}.\end{split}

By inspection, we deduce that as soon as
a
is chosen sufficiently large to have:

\lambda_{0}^{\parallel},\ \sup_{t}|\eta^{\prime}|\leq\lambda_{0}\delta_{0},\ \sup_{t}|\eta^{\prime\prime}|\leq\lambda_{0}^{2},\ \delta_{0}^{1/2}<1/2,

we can ensure that:

(4.9)

\begin{split}||B_{0}||_{0},\ ||v_{0}||_{0}&\leq C_{0}(1-\delta_{q}^{1/2}),\\
|B_{0}|&\geq c_{0}(1+\delta_{0}^{1/2}),\\
||\partial_{t}^{j}B_{0}||_{r},||\partial_{t}^{j}v_{0}||_{r}&\leq C_{0}\lambda_{0}^{r+j}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2,\ \&\ \ 0\leq r\leq N-j\ \ \&\ \ (r,j)\neq(0,0),\\
||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{0}B_{0}||_{r},\ ||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{0}v_{0}||_{r}&\leq C_{0}\lambda_{0}^{r+1}\delta_{0}\text{ for }0\leq r\leq M-1,\\
\end{split}

for some
C_{0}>\bar{C}_{0}
, a constant sufficiently large, and
c_{0}<1/2
, another constant, sufficiently small, both independent of the parameters.

Plugging
(v_{0},B_{0})
as above in the ideal MHD system (
1.2
), we see that the errors can be reabsorbed in a Reynolds stress
R_{0}
as in (
1.15
) of the form:

\begin{split}R_{0}&=f^{\prime}\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}}\cos(\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}x_{3})\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{2})(e_{1}\otimes e_{2}+e_{2}\otimes e_{1})+\eta^{\prime}\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}}\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{q}^{1/2}x_{2})(e_{1}\otimes e_{2}+e_{2}\otimes e_{1})\\
&+\frac{\lambda^{\parallel}_{0}\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}f^{[1]}(t)}{\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}}\cos(\lambda_{0}^{\parallel}x_{3})\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{2})(e_{1}\otimes e_{2}+e_{2}\otimes e_{1})\end{split}

where the first row comes from the time derivative of the cut-offs in
v_{0}
while the last one comes from
-B_{0}\cdot\nabla B_{0}
. We remark that:

|f^{\prime}|\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{\parallel},\ |f^{\prime\prime}|\lesssim(\lambda_{0}^{\parallel})^{2}\ \text{ and }\ \lambda_{0}^{\parallel}|f^{[1]}(t)|\lesssim 1,

moreover, our choice of
b,\ \gamma_{CZ}
from Subsection
1.5
, see (
1.32
), guarantees that

\beta(2b+1)<1-2\gamma_{CZ}

and we deduce:

\begin{split}||R_{0}||_{r}&\leq C(\lambda^{\parallel}_{0}+\sup_{t}|\eta^{\prime}|)\lambda_{0}^{r-2\alpha}\delta_{1}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq M,\\
||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}R_{0}||_{r}&\leq C((\lambda^{\parallel}_{0})^{2}+\sup_{t}|\eta^{\prime\prime}|)\lambda_{0}^{r-2\alpha}\delta_{1}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq M-1,\end{split}

for some constant
C
that may depend on
M
but not on
r
. We can now choose
a
so large that:

C((\lambda^{\parallel}_{0})^{2}+\sup_{t}|\eta^{\prime\prime}|)\lambda_{0}^{-\alpha}\leq\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\ \text{ and }\ C(\lambda^{\parallel}_{0}+\sup_{t}|\eta^{\prime\prime}|)\lambda_{0}^{-\alpha}\leq 1

and we conclude:

(4.10)

\begin{split}||R_{0}||_{r}&\leq\lambda_{q}^{r-\alpha}\delta_{1}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq M,\\
||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}R_{0}||_{r}&\leq\lambda_{q}^{r+1-\alpha}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\delta_{1}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq M-1.\end{split}

With
p_{0}=0
, we have that
(v_{0},\ B_{0},\ p_{0},\ R_{0})
solves (
1.15
) and

(4.11)

v_{0}=\begin{cases}\bar{v}\ \text{ for }\ t\leq 2/3\\
0\ \text{ for }\ t\geq 2\\
\end{cases},\ B_{0}=e_{3}\ \text{ for }t\leq 1-1/4\ \&\ t\geq 2,\ R_{0}=0\ \text{ for }\ t\leq 2/3\ \&\ t\geq 2.

From this, we deduce that
(v_{0},\ B_{0},\ p_{0})
is an exact solution in
t\leq 2/3\ \&\ t\geq 2
and

\mathcal{E}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(0)>\mathcal{E}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(3).

Details of the second example.
As above we can compute
X_{t}
explicitly, indeed

X_{t}=\mathrm{Id}+\eta^{[1]}\bar{v}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ X_{t}^{-1}=\mathrm{Id}-\eta^{[1]}\bar{v}.

By construction
B_{0}
is a solution of
1.1
and

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}X_{t}(x)&=\mathrm{Id}-\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{1})\eta^{[1]}(t)e_{2}\otimes e_{1}\\
B_{0}(x,t)&=\mathrm{D}X_{t}\circ X_{t}^{-1}(x)[\bar{B}(X_{t}^{-1}(x))],\\
&=\left[\mathrm{Id}-\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}X_{t}^{-1}(x)\cdot e_{1})\eta^{[1]}(t)e_{2}\otimes e_{1}\right][e_{3}+\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}X_{t}^{-1}(x)\cdot e_{1})e_{2}]\\
&=\left[\mathrm{Id}-\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{1})\eta^{[1]}(t)e_{2}\otimes e_{1}\right][e_{3}+\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{1})e_{2}]\\
&=e_{3}+\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{1})e_{2},\end{split}

moreover,

(\partial_{t}+v_{0}\cdot\nabla)B_{0}=B_{0}\cdot\nabla B_{0}=B_{0}\cdot\nabla v_{0}=0

and

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+v_{0}\cdot\nabla)v_{0}(x,t)&=\eta^{\prime}(t)\cos(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{1})e_{2}\\
&=\mathrm{div}\left[\eta^{\prime}(t)\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}}\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{1})(e_{1}\otimes e_{2}+e_{2}\otimes e_{1})\right]\\
&=\mathrm{div}\ R_{0}(x,t),\end{split}

where we defined:

R_{0}(x,t)=\eta^{\prime}(t)\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}}\sin(\lambda_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}x_{1})(e_{1}\otimes e_{2}+e_{2}\otimes e_{1}).

From the above, we deduce that, with
p_{0}=0
, the quadruple
(v_{0},\ B_{0},\ p_{0},\ R_{0})
solves (
1.1
) and

(4.12)

v_{0}=\begin{cases}\bar{v}\ \text{ for }\ t\leq 2/3\\
0\ \text{ for }\ t\geq 2\\
\end{cases},\ B_{0}=\bar{B},\ R_{0}=0\ \text{ for }\ t\leq 2/3\ \&\ t\geq 2,

that is,
(v_{0},\ B_{0},\ p_{0})
is an exact solution in
t\leq 2/3\ \&\ t\geq 2
. Moreover, a simple computation shows that the total energy and cross helicity are not conserved:

\mathcal{E}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(0)>\mathcal{E}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(3)\ \text{ and }\ \mathcal{H}^{\times}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(0)>\mathcal{H}^{\times}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(3).

We can now argue as above and conclude that, also in this example, (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) are satisfied.

Convergence of the scheme.
With (
4.9
), (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
) at hand and given
0\leq\gamma<1/5
we can apply the Iterative Proposition
1.2
with
\beta
satisfying
0\leq\gamma<\beta<1/5
and obtain a sequence of sub-solutions
(v_{q},\ B_{q},\ p_{q},\ R_{q})
. From (
1.17
), and (
1.16f
), we deduce:

\begin{split}||v_{q+1}-v_{q}||_{\gamma}&\lesssim||v_{q+1}-v_{q}||_{0}^{1-\gamma}||v_{q+1}-v_{q}||_{1}^{\gamma}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{1/2}\lambda_{q+1}^{\gamma}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\gamma-\beta}.\end{split}

Similarly,

||B_{q+1}-B_{q}||_{\gamma}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\gamma-\beta}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ ||R_{q}||_{\gamma}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\gamma-2\beta}.

The additional use of the Poisson problem determining
p_{q}
, namely

\Delta p_{q}=\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div}\left[R_{q}-v_{q}\otimes v_{q}+B_{q}\otimes B_{q}\right],

and standard Schauder estimates allow us to conclude that
(v_{q},\ B_{q},\ p_{q},\ R_{q})
is a Cauchy sequence in
C_{t}C_{x}^{\gamma}
, in particular:

(v_{q},\ B_{q},\ p_{q},\ R_{q})\overset{C_{t}C_{x}^{\gamma}}{\longrightarrow}(v,\ B,\ p,\ 0)

and passing to the limit in the distributional formulation of
1.15
we see that
(v,B,p)
solves
1.2
weakly. Moreover, from (
1.17
) it follows that

\sup_{t}[v-v_{0}],\ \sup_{t}[B-B_{0}]\subset(1/2,5/2)

and we deduce:

\mathcal{E}_{(v,B)}(0)=\mathcal{E}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(0)>\mathcal{E}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(3)=\mathcal{E}_{(v,B)}(3),

which shows that the energy is not conserved. Additionally, in the second example, we have:

\mathcal{H}^{\times}_{(v,B)}(0)=\mathcal{H}^{\times}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(0)>\mathcal{H}^{\times}_{(v_{0},B_{0})}(3)=\mathcal{H}^{\times}_{(v,B)}(3),

which shows that the cross-helicity is not conserved.

5.
Lie-Taylor Series Expansion

Here we develop the theory of perturbations led by a Lagrangian Displacement Field (LDF)
\xi
. This object appears in a different form and with a different goal compared to our work in Newcomb
[
42
]
and Vladimirov, Moffat, Ilin
[
39
]
. In these works, the authors study the stability of constrained energy minimisers and use this object to perform energy variations. Being interested in linear stability theory, the knowledge of the leading term of the perturbation suffices in that case, but not in ours.

5.1.
Perturbation and Lie-Taylor Series

We study smooth perturbations
(v,B)
of a smooth solution
(\bar{v},\bar{B})
of the Faraday-Ohm system on
\mathbb{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, namely

(5.1)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\bar{B}+\mathrm{curl}[\bar{B}\times\bar{v}]=0,\\
\bar{B}|_{t=t_{0}}=B_{0},\\
\mathrm{div}\ \bar{B}=\mathrm{div}\ \bar{v}=0.\end{cases}

We recall that for sufficiently regular
\bar{v},B_{0}
, this is equivalent to:

\bar{B}(x,t)=(\bar{X}_{t})_{*}B_{0}(x),\ \mathrm{div}\ B_{0}=0

see (
A.14
) with the correct identifications and (
A.10
), where
\bar{X}
is the Lagrangian flow map of
\bar{v}
, that is, a solution of:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\bar{X}_{t}(x)=\bar{v}(\bar{X}_{t}(x),t),\\
\bar{X}|_{t=0}=\mathrm{Id}.\end{cases}

We require the perturbation
(v,B)
to also be a solution of (
5.1
). A natural way to construct it is then to perturb the flow of
\bar{v}
via some volume-preserving time-dependent diffeomorphism
X(\cdot,t):\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{T}^{3}
close to the identity and such that

(5.2)

X(\cdot,0)=\mathrm{Id}

namely

\bar{X}\leadsto X\circ\bar{X},

we then set
v
to be the divergence-free vector field having
X\circ\bar{X}
as its Lagrangian flow map:

v=\partial_{t}(X\circ\bar{X})\circ(X\circ\bar{X})^{-1},

where the composition happens in space, that is, as maps
\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{T}^{3}
. The associated perturbed magnetic field
B
having to solve (
5.1
), will then be:

(5.3)

B=(X\circ\bar{X})_{*}B_{0}=X_{*}\bar{X}_{*}B_{0}=X_{*}\bar{B}.

Note that (
5.2
) is there only to ensure the initial condition for the magnetic field is preserved:

B|_{t=0}=B_{0}

and one might want to remove it in general, moreover,
v
has a pushforward structure as well, indeed:

(5.4)

\begin{split}v&=\partial_{t}(X\circ\bar{X})\circ(X\circ\bar{X})^{-1}\\
&=\partial_{t}X(\bar{X}\circ(X\circ\bar{X})^{-1})+\mathrm{D}X(\bar{X}\circ(X\circ\bar{X})^{-1})\partial_{t}\bar{X}\circ(X\circ\bar{X})^{-1}\\
&=\partial_{t}X\circ(X)^{-1}+\mathrm{D}X(X^{-1})\bar{v}(X^{-1})\\
&=\partial_{t}X\circ X^{-1}+X_{*}\bar{v}.\end{split}

We rewrote the problem of constructing a perturbation of (
5.1
) to that of constructing a time-dependent volume-preserving diffeomorphism
X(\cdot,t)
close to the identity. Passing to the Lie Algebra of such a group, that is, divergence-free time-dependent vector fields, we can construct a continuous family indexed by
s\in[-1,1]
of these as:

X_{s}(\cdot,t)=\exp_{\mathrm{Id}}^{s\xi(\cdot,t)}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{T}^{3},

for
\xi(x,t):\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
some divergence-free vector field, satisfying
\xi(\cdot,0)=0
so that (
5.2
) is retained. Explicitly, we solve:

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}X_{s}(x,t)=\xi(X_{s}(x,t),t),\\
X|_{s=0}(x,t)=x\end{cases}

and think of
X
as
X=X_{1}
and
X^{-1}=X_{-1}
. We call the vector field
\xi
the Lagrangian Displacement Field (LDF) after
[
39
]
, where a similar object is named the first-order displacement field. This gives us a continuous family of divergence field vector fields
(v_{s},B_{s})
starting at
(\bar{v},\bar{B})
solving (
5.1
). The identities (
5.4
) and (
5.3
) applied with
X=X_{s}
give us:

\begin{cases}v_{s}=\partial_{t}X_{s}\circ(X_{s})^{-1}+(X_{s})_{*}\bar{v},\\
B_{s}=(X_{s})_{*}\bar{B}.\end{cases}

We now study the derivative of
v_{s}
with respect to the parameter
s
we just introduced. We compute:

(5.5)

\begin{split}\partial_{s}v_{s}&=\partial_{s}[\partial_{t}X_{s}\circ(X_{s})^{-1}]+\partial_{s}[(X_{s})_{*}\bar{v}]\\
&=(\partial_{s}\partial_{t}X_{s})\circ(X_{s})^{-1}+\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}X_{s})((X_{s})^{-1})[\partial_{s}(X_{s})^{-1}]-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}[(X_{s})_{*}\bar{v}]\\
&=(\partial_{t}\partial_{s}X_{s})\circ(X_{s})^{-1}-\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}X_{s})((X_{s})^{-1})\mathrm{D}((X_{s})^{-1})[\xi]-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}[(X_{s})_{*}\bar{v}]\\
&=\partial_{t}(\xi(X_{s}))\circ(X_{s})^{-1}-\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}X_{s}\circ(X_{s})^{-1})[\xi]-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}[(X_{s})_{*}\bar{v}]\\
&=\partial_{t}\xi+\mathrm{D}\xi[\partial_{t}X_{s}\circ(X_{s})^{-1}]-\mathrm{D}(\partial_{t}X_{s}\circ(X_{s})^{-1})[\xi]-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}[(X_{s})_{*}\bar{v}]\\
&=\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\partial_{t}X_{s}\circ(X_{s})^{-1})-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}[(X_{s})_{*}\bar{v}]\\
&=\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}v_{s}\\
\end{split}

where we used the equations solved by
X_{s},\ X^{-1}_{s},\ (X_{s})_{*}\bar{v}
, see (
A.15
) with the correct identifications and (
A.14
). Since
\xi
is independent of
s
we deduce that for any
k\geq 0
that:

(5.6)

\begin{split}\partial_{s}^{k+1}v_{s}&=\partial_{s}^{k}(\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}v_{s})\\
&=-\partial_{s}^{k-1}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\partial_{s}v_{s}\\
&=(-1)^{1}\partial_{s}^{k-1}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}v_{s})\\
&=\dots\\
&=(-1)^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}(\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}v_{s})\end{split}

Since for
s=0
we have
v_{0}=\bar{v}
, we can write a truncated Lie-Taylor series expansion in the parameter
s
at
s=0
of the form:

(5.7)

\begin{split}v_{s}&=\bar{v}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{s^{k}}{k!}\partial_{s}^{k}v_{s}|_{s=0}+r^{k_{0},s}_{w}\\
&=\bar{v}+s\left(\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\bar{v}\right)-\frac{s^{2}}{2}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\left(\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\bar{v}\right)+\dots+r^{k_{0},s}_{w}\\
&=\bar{v}+\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\frac{(-1)^{k}s^{k+1}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}})\xi\\
\end{split}

where the remainder
r^{k_{0},s}_{w}
can be written as:

(5.8)

r^{k_{0},s}_{w}=\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{k_{0}+1}v_{s^{\prime}}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

We will study this in more detail in Subsection
5.2
. Now recall that the magnetic field associated with
v_{s}
is
B_{s}
given by the pushforward formula:

B_{s}=(X_{s}\circ\bar{X})_{*}B_{0}=(X_{s})_{*}[(\bar{X})_{*}B_{0}]=(X_{s})_{*}\bar{B},

from (
A.14
) or arguing as above we find expressions for
\partial_{s}^{k}B_{s}
for any
k\geq 1
, namely

\partial_{s}^{k}B_{s}=(-1)^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}B_{s},

we can then write a truncated Lie-Taylor expansion for the parameter
s
around zero of the form:

(5.9)

\begin{split}B_{s}&=\bar{B}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{s^{k}}{k!}\partial_{s}^{k}B_{s}|_{s=0}+r^{k_{0},s}_{b}\\
&=\bar{B}-s\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\bar{B}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}\bar{B}-\dots+r^{k_{0},s}_{b}\\
&=\bar{B}+\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\frac{(-1)^{k}s^{k+1}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\bar{B}}\xi\\
\end{split}

where, as before, the remainder
r^{k_{0},s}_{b}
has the explicit expression:

r^{k_{0},s}_{b}=\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{k_{0}+1}B_{s^{\prime}}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

5.2.
Remainders

In this section, we derive explicit expressions for the remainder terms in the Lie-Taylor series above. This will allow estimates of
v,B
in terms of
\bar{v},\bar{B}
and
X
, where the ones on
X
follow from the ones on
\xi
and Grönwall Lemma type of arguments.

Velocity Field:
We want to find a suitable representation for
r^{k_{0},s}_{w}
. The problem here is that Taylor’s Theorem gives (
5.8
) and we don’t have an explicit expression or estimates for
v_{s}
. We define
w
to be:

\partial_{t}\xi+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}}\xi=w

and compute

\begin{split}\partial_{s}(v_{s}-\bar{v})&=\partial_{s}v_{s}\\
&=\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}v_{s}\\
&=\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\bar{v}-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(v_{s}-\bar{v})\\
&=\underbrace{\partial_{t}\xi+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}}\xi}_{=w}-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(v_{s}-\bar{v})\\
&=w-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(v_{s}-\bar{v})\end{split}

where we used (
5.5
). This shows that the difference
F=v_{s}-\bar{v}
solves the forced Lie-transport equation:

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}F+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}F=w,\\
F|_{s=0}=0.\end{cases}

Note that since
\xi
is independent of
s
we have
\partial_{s}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}=\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\partial_{s}
and thus applying
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}
to the equation above and commuting we get:

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}F+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}F=\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}w\\
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}F|_{s=0}=0\end{cases}

for any
k\geq 0
. In particular, denoting
\Phi_{s}=X_{s}^{-1}
the inverse flow of
\xi
we have an explicit representation of
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}F=\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}(v_{s}-\bar{v}_{q})
as the solution of this forced Lie transport given by the Duhamel formula, see (
A.14
). Namely:

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}(v_{s}-\bar{v})&=\int_{0}^{s}\Phi_{s}^{*}X_{s^{\prime}}^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}w)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{0}^{s}(X_{s^{\prime}}\circ\Phi_{s})^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}w)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{0}^{s}(X_{s^{\prime}}\circ X_{-s})^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}w)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{0}^{s}X_{s^{\prime}-s}^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}w)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
\end{split}

here we used that since
\xi
does not depend on
s
we have
\Phi_{s}=X_{-s}
and
X_{s}\circ X_{s^{\prime}}=X_{s+s^{\prime}}
. With this and (
5.6
) at hand, we can rewrite the velocity remainder as follows:

(5.10)

\begin{split}r^{k_{0},s}_{w}&=\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{k_{0}+1}v_{s^{\prime}}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}}(\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}v_{s^{\prime}})(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}}(\partial_{t}\xi-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\bar{v}-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(v_{s^{\prime}}-\bar{v}))(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}}(w-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(v_{s^{\prime}}-\bar{v}))(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}s^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}}w-\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}(v_{s^{\prime}}-\bar{v})(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}s^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}}w+\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{k_{0}!}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\int_{0}^{s^{\prime}}X_{l-s^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}w\mathrm{d}l\right](s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}s^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}}w+\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{s}X_{-s^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}w(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}s^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}}w+\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{s}(X_{s^{\prime}})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}w(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
\end{split}

where we used Fubini’s theorem to compute with
f(u)=X_{u}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}w
the multiple integral

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\int_{0}^{s^{\prime}}f(l-s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}l\right](s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}&=\int_{0}^{s}\left[\int_{-s^{\prime}}^{0}f(u)\mathrm{d}u\right](s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{-s}^{0}\left[\int_{-u}^{s}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]f(u)\mathrm{d}u\\
&=-\frac{1}{k_{0}+1}\int_{-s}^{0}(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}+1}|_{s^{\prime}=-u}^{s^{\prime}=s}f(l)\mathrm{d}l\\
&=\frac{1}{k_{0}+1}\int_{-s}^{0}(s+u)^{k_{0}+1}f(u)\mathrm{d}u\\
&=\frac{1}{k_{0}+1}\int_{0}^{s}f(-s^{\prime})(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\end{split}

to switch the integral, one sees that the integration happens on
0\leq s^{\prime}\leq s
and
-s^{\prime}\leq u\leq 0
, that is, on the triangle
0\leq-u\leq s^{\prime}\leq s
, and thus for fixed
0\leq-u\leq s
we have
s^{\prime}
in the range
-u\leq s^{\prime}\leq s
.

Magnetic Field
: Computing as above with

b=-\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\bar{B}=\mathcal{L}_{\bar{B}}\xi

replacing the role of
w
, one can show that:

(5.11)

r^{k_{0},s}_{b}=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}}s^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}}b+\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{s}(X_{s^{\prime}})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}b(s-s^{\prime})^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

5.3.
Lagrangian Perturbation Lemma

We have constructed a continuous family
(v_{s},B_{s})
of solutions of (
5.1
) sharing the same initial condition and starting at
(\bar{v},\bar{B})
. It has been constructed by means of an auxiliary divergence-free vector field
\xi
. We now set
s=1
, write
(v_{1},B_{1})=(v,B),\ X_{1}=X
and collect the work above in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.1

(Lagrangian Perturbation Lemma)
.

Let
\xi
be a time-dependent divergence-free vector field on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
such that
\xi|_{t=0}=0
, let
(\bar{v},\bar{B})
be a smooth solution of (
5.1
). Let
X=\exp_{\mathrm{Id}}^{\xi}
we call
(w,b)
given by:

\begin{cases}w=\partial_{t}X\circ X^{-1}+(X_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})\bar{v}\\
b=(X_{*}-\mathrm{Id}_{*})\bar{B}\end{cases}

a perturbation of
(\bar{v},\bar{B})
along the LDF
\xi
. We have that
(\bar{v}+w,\bar{B}+b)
is still a solution of (
5.1
) with initial condition
\bar{B}|_{t=0}
and can be expanded in a truncated at level
k_{0}
Lie-Taylor series of the following form:

\begin{split}w&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}})\xi+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}})\xi+r^{k_{0}}_{w},\\
b&=\mathcal{L}_{\bar{B}}\xi+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\bar{B}}\xi+r_{b}^{k_{0}},\\
r^{k_{0}}_{w}&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}})\xi\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
r^{k_{0}}_{b}&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\bar{B}}\xi\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

Moreover, assuming
\xi=\mathrm{curl}\ \Theta
this can be rewritten as:

\begin{split}w&=\mathrm{curl}\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}})\Theta+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}})\Theta+\theta^{k_{0}}_{w}\right],\\
b&=\mathrm{curl}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\bar{B}}\Theta+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\bar{B}}\Theta+\theta_{b}^{k_{0}}\right],\\
\theta^{k_{0}}_{w}&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\bar{v}})\Theta\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\theta^{k_{0}}_{b}&=\frac{(-1)^{k_{0}+1}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(X_{s})_{*}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{k_{0}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\bar{B}}\Theta\right](1-s)^{k_{0}+1}\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

where the Lie derivatives happen in the 1-form sense.

The first assertion follows immediately from (
5.7
), (
5.9
), (
5.10
), (
5.11
). The statement about the expansion in terms of the potential
\Theta
follows from the properties of the pushforward and Lie derivative recalled in Appendix
A
, namely (
A.10
) and (
A.12
), given that
\bar{v},\ \bar{B},\ \xi
are all divergence-free, with the Lie derivations occurring in the 1-form sense.

6.
Forced Galbrun’s Equation

This subsection is inspired by the work of Giri-Radu in
[
24
]
; the equation at hand, however, is of second order, and the 3D setting requires additional effort to write a formulation for the potential.

6.1.
Rewriting

In the following, we consider the linearization of the MHD momentum equation set on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},t_{0}+\tau^{c})
at
(v,B)
with forcing
f\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}F
. Namely, we look for time-dependent vector fields
(w,b)
and pressure
\pi
solving the problem:

(6.1)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}w+\left[v\cdot\nabla w+w\cdot\nabla v\right]-\left[B\cdot\nabla b+b\cdot\nabla B\right]+\nabla\pi=f\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}F,\\
\mathrm{div}\ w=\mathrm{div}\ b=0.\\
\end{cases}

Here
f=f(t/\tau^{a})
is a fixed one-periodic zero-average function rescaled using a parameter
\tau^{a}
e.g. the ones constructed in Lemma
2.8
, in particular, the non-rescaled
f
admits a bounded primitive
f^{[1]}
defined explicitly as:

f^{[1]}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}f(s)\mathrm{d}s,

we think of them as functions
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and always rescale them with
\tau^{a}
, moreover,
F
is a given matrix field, e.g.
A_{I}
in (
2.27
). We also assume that on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},t_{0}+\tau^{c})
the quadruple
(v,\ B,\ p,\ R)
solves:

(6.2)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}v+v\cdot\nabla v+B\cdot\nabla B+\nabla p=\mathrm{div}\ R,\\
\partial_{t}B+\mathrm{curl}[B\times v]=0,\\
\mathrm{div}\ v=\mathrm{div}\ B=0.\\
\end{cases}

To simplify notation, we set:

D_{t}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla,\ z^{\pm}=v\pm B,\ \mathcal{A}^{\pm}=\partial_{t}+z^{\pm}\cdot\nabla.

We now gather the precise assumptions on the Hölder norms of
v,\ B,\ p,\ R,\ F
and their transport properties.

Standing Assumptions 1
.

Fix
\underline{r}\geq 2
an integer. We assume the following estimates on the vector fields:

(6.3a)

\displaystyle||v||_{r},||B||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1,

(6.3b)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}v||_{r},||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}B||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0,

(6.3c)

\displaystyle||p||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1,

(6.3d)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}p||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q}^{3/2}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1

and the following bounds on the forcings:

(6.4a)

\displaystyle||R||_{r},||F||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0,

(6.4b)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}R||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0,

(6.4c)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}F||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 0,

(6.4d)

\displaystyle\sup_{t}|f|,\ \sup_{t}|f^{[1]}|\lesssim 1

where all the implicit constants depend only on
r
(in the applications, also on the parameters, but not on
a
). Note that the fractional Hölder norm bounds can be obtained from these by interpolation, and to simplify the notation, we set a common lower bound for the good derivative range
\underline{r}
, but it is possible to work in a more general setting, see also Remark
24
.

We will restrict the time domain of the solution to
|t-t_{0}|<\tau^{c}
that is
t\in(t_{0}-\tau^{c},\ t_{0}+\tau^{c})
with
\tau^{c}
given by (
1.21
). For later applications, we assume that
F\equiv 0
on:

I^{cut}=(t_{0}-\tau^{c},\ t_{0}-2/3\tau^{c}]\cup[t_{0}+2/3\tau^{c},\ t_{0}+\tau^{c}).

Going back to the linearization, we think of
(w,b)
as the leading terms of a Lagrangian perturbation along some vector field
\xi
with 1-form potential
\Theta
as in Lemma
5.1
, this gives:

(6.5)

w=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\xi,\ b=\mathcal{L}_{B}\xi,\ \xi=\mathrm{curl}[\Theta].

We are interested in rewriting
6.1
above in terms of the 1-form potential
\Theta
, we first compute:

(6.6)

\begin{split}f\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}F&=\partial_{t}w+\left[v\cdot\nabla w+w\cdot\nabla v\right]-\left[B\cdot\nabla b+b\cdot\nabla B\right]+\nabla\pi\\
&=\partial_{t}\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\xi\right]+v\cdot\nabla\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\xi\right]+\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\xi\right]\cdot\nabla v+\nabla\pi\\
&-\left[B\cdot\nabla\mathcal{L}_{B}\xi+\mathcal{L}_{B}\xi\cdot\nabla B\right]\\
&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})^{2}\xi-(\mathcal{L}_{B})^{2}\xi+2(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\xi\cdot\nabla v-2\mathcal{L}_{B}\xi\cdot\nabla B+\nabla\pi\\
&=\mathrm{curl}[\underbrace{(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{1}_{v})^{2}\Theta-(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{B})^{2}\Theta}_{T_{1}}]+\underbrace{2\mathrm{div}\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{1}_{v})\Theta\times\nabla v-\mathcal{L}^{1}_{B}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}+\nabla\pi}_{T_{2}}\\
\end{split}

where we used the identities (
A.12
) and (
A.3
). Taking the divergence of (
6.6
) we see that:

\Delta\pi=-2\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div}\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{1}_{v})\Theta\times\nabla v-\mathcal{L}^{1}_{B}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}

and writing the Leray projector as
\mathbb{P}=\mathrm{Id}-\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}
we end up with:

(6.7)

\begin{split}T_{2}&=2\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}\left[(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{1}_{v})\Theta\times\nabla v-\mathcal{L}^{1}_{B}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]\\
&=\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}\left[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta\times\nabla z^{-}+\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta\times\nabla z^{+}+2\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v-2\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right],\end{split}

we now rewrite
T_{1}
in (
6.6
) as:

(6.8)

\begin{split}T_{1}&=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{1}_{v})(\mathrm{D}_{t}\Theta+\mathrm{D}v^{\top}[\Theta])-\mathcal{L}^{1}_{B}(B\cdot\nabla\Theta+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}[\Theta])\\
&=\mathrm{D}_{t}^{2}\Theta+\mathrm{D}_{t}(\mathrm{D}v^{\top}[\Theta])+\mathrm{D}v^{\top}[\mathrm{D}_{t}\Theta+\mathrm{D}v^{\top}[\Theta]]\\
&-(B\cdot\nabla)^{2}\Theta-B\cdot\nabla(\mathrm{D}B^{\top}[\Theta])-\mathrm{D}B^{\top}[B\cdot\nabla\Theta+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}[\Theta]]\\
&=\mathrm{D}_{t}^{2}\Theta+\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{D}_{t}v)^{\top}[\Theta]-(\mathrm{D}v\mathrm{D}v)^{\top}[\Theta]+\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\mathrm{D}_{t}\Theta+\mathrm{D}v^{\top}[\mathrm{D}_{t}\Theta+\mathrm{D}v^{\top}[\Theta]]\\
&-(B\cdot\nabla)^{2}\Theta-\mathrm{D}(B\cdot\nabla B)^{\top}[\Theta]+(\mathrm{D}B\mathrm{D}B)^{\top}[\Theta]-\mathrm{D}B^{\top}[B\cdot\nabla\Theta]-\mathrm{D}B^{\top}[B\cdot\nabla\Theta+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}[\Theta]]\\
&=\mathrm{D}_{t}^{2}\Theta-(B\cdot\nabla)^{2}\Theta+\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B)^{\top}[\Theta]+2\mathrm{D}v^{\top}[\mathrm{D}_{t}\Theta]-2\mathrm{D}B^{\top}[B\cdot\nabla\Theta]\\
&=\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta+(\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B])^{\top}[\Theta]+(\mathrm{D}z^{-})^{\top}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]+(\mathrm{D}z^{+})^{\top}[\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\end{split}

where we used that:

\mathrm{D}_{t}B\cdot\nabla=(\partial_{t}B+[v,B])\cdot\nabla+B\cdot\nabla D_{t}

and since by assumption
\partial_{t}B+[v,B]=0
, see (
6.2
), we can write:

(6.9)

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}&=\left(D_{t}-B\cdot\nabla\right)(D_{t}+B\cdot\nabla)\\
&=D_{t}^{2}-(B\cdot\nabla)^{2}+\mathrm{D}_{t}B\cdot\nabla-B\cdot\nabla D_{t}\\
&=D_{t}^{2}-(B\cdot\nabla)^{2}+(\partial_{t}B+[v,B])\cdot\nabla\\
&=D_{t}^{2}-(B\cdot\nabla)^{2}.\end{split}

Note, in passing, that this also shows the Alfvén transport operators commutation:

\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}=\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}.

Substituting (
6.7
) in (
6.6
) and applying
\mathrm{curl}^{-1}=-\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{curl}
, we finally reach

\begin{split}&\mathrm{curl}^{-1}\mathrm{curl}[T_{1}]+\mathrm{curl}^{-1}\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}\left[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta\times\nabla z^{-}+\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta\times\nabla z^{+}+2\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+2\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}\\
&=f\mathrm{curl}^{-1}\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}F,\end{split}

where we fix the gauge of
\mathrm{curl}^{-1}
to be Coulomb, i.e. requiring the output to be divergence-free. We now set
\mathbb{T}
to be the CZ-type operator appearing in the rewriting, namely

\mathbb{T}=\mathrm{curl}^{-1}\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}

and substitution of the expression of
T_{1}
found in (
6.8
) then leads to:

\begin{split}&\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta+(\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B])^{\top}[\Theta]+(\mathrm{D}z^{-})^{\top}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]+(\mathrm{D}z^{+})^{\top}[\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]+\\
&+\mathbb{T}\left[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta\times\nabla z^{-}+\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta\times\nabla z^{+}+2\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v-2\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}+\nabla\tilde{\pi}\\
&=f\mathbb{T}\mathrm{div}F,\end{split}

for some
\tilde{\pi}
coming from the Gauge condition, namely

\mathrm{curl}^{-1}\mathrm{curl}[T_{1}]=T_{1}+\nabla\tilde{\pi}.

We forget about this gradient, as we are not interested in keeping any divergence-free constraint on
\Theta
and solve instead:

(6.10)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta+(\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B])^{\top}[\Theta]+(\mathrm{D}z^{-})^{\top}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]+(\mathrm{D}z^{+})^{\top}[\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\\
&+\mathbb{T}\left[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta\times\nabla z^{-}+\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta\times\nabla z^{+}+2\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v-2\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}\\
&=f\mathbb{T}\mathrm{div}F,\end{split}

the two possibly different solutions for the potential lead to
(w,b)
solving the same equation, which is what really matters to us. Note in addition that one of the terms involving
\Theta
sees the fact that
(v,\ B,\ p,\ R)
solve (
6.2
), indeed

\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B])^{\top}[\Theta]=\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{div}\ R-\nabla p)^{\top}[\Theta]

this will be important later when doing estimates. We now set for any input vectors
a,b,c\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
:

(6.11)

\begin{split}&H_{1}(a)=(\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B])^{\top}[a],\ \tilde{H}_{1}(a)=2\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}a\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}a\times\nabla B\right]^{\top},\\
&H_{2}(b,c)=(\mathrm{D}z^{-})^{\top}[b]+(\mathrm{D}z^{+})^{\top}[c],\ \tilde{H}_{2}(b,c)=\left[b\times\nabla z^{-}+c\times\nabla z^{+}\right]^{\top}.\\
\end{split}

note that apart from the parameters
a,b,c
, all the objects depend also on
(x,t)
and we think of them as matrices acting on vectors. To simplify the notation further, we group the local and non-local parts into two single operators, one of zeroth order with respect to
\Theta
and the other at first order in the Alfvén transport of
\Theta
. Namely, we set:

\mathbb{H}_{1}=H_{1}+\mathbb{T}\tilde{H}_{1},\ \mathbb{H}_{2}=H_{2}+\mathbb{T}\tilde{H}_{2}.

For later use, we remark that solving (
6.10
) is the same as solving:

(6.12)

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{1}_{v})^{2}\Theta-(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{B})^{2}\Theta+\mathbb{T}\tilde{H}_{1}(\Theta)+\mathbb{T}\tilde{H}_{2}(\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta)=f\mathbb{T}F.\end{split}

We formalise the above manipulations of (
6.10
) in the following Lemma.

Lemma 6.1

(Galbrun Equation)
.

The forced linearization (
6.1
) of the MHD momentum equation solved on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},t_{0}+\tau^{c})
under the Standing Assumptions
1
and (
6.2
), using the first-order approximation (
6.5
) of a perturbation along a Lagrangian displacement field
\xi
with potential
\Theta
, see Lemma
5.1
, corresponds to the second-order initial value problem:

(6.13)

\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta+\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]=f\mathbb{T}F,\\
\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}=G,\\
\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}=H.\\
\end{cases}

We now prove some bounds on the CZ operators
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
, which will be needed later to prove a priori estimates on the solution. To do this, it is useful to define:

\begin{split}h_{1}^{r}&=\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q})\\
h_{1,t}^{r}&=\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-3)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q})\\
\end{split}\ \text{ and }\ \begin{split}h_{2}^{r}&=\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2})\\
h_{2,t}^{r}&=\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}(\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2})\end{split}

these correspond to the bounds for the coefficients in the operators
\mathbb{H}_{1},\ \mathbb{H}_{2}
, and we remark the loss of two good derivatives in
h_{1}
.

Lemma 6.2

(Operator Norms)
.

Under the Standing Assumptions
1
, we have the following estimates:

(6.14a)

\displaystyle||\mathbb{H}_{1}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim h_{1}^{r}||\Theta||_{0}+h_{1}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha},

(6.14b)

\displaystyle||\mathbb{H}_{2}(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2})||_{r+\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim h_{2}^{r}(||\Theta_{1}||_{0}+||\Theta_{2}||_{0})+h_{2}^{0}(||\Theta_{1}||_{r+\alpha}+||\Theta_{2}||_{r+\alpha}),

(6.14c)

\displaystyle||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta||_{r+\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\left[h_{1}^{r}||\Theta||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\right],

(6.14d)

\displaystyle||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathbb{H}_{2}](\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2})||_{r+\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\left[h_{2}^{r}(||\Theta_{1}||_{\alpha}+||\Theta_{2}||_{\alpha})+h_{2}^{0}(||\Theta_{1}||_{r+\alpha}+||\Theta_{2}||_{r+\alpha})\right],

(6.14e)

\displaystyle||[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta||_{r+\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q}\left[h_{1,t}^{r}||\Theta||_{0}+h_{1,t}^{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\right],

(6.14f)

\displaystyle||[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H}_{2}](\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2})||_{r+\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q}\left[h_{2,t}^{r}(||\Theta_{1}||_{0}+||\Theta_{2}||_{0})+h_{2,t}^{0}(||\Theta_{1}||_{r+\alpha}+||\Theta_{2}||_{r+\alpha})\right].

The implicit constants depend on
r
and
\alpha
.

Remark 23
.

The commutator with the Alfvén transport has a slightly different bound because we use the estimate from Proposition
B.7
, which loses an additional
\alpha
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

The proof is just a lengthy sequence of computations. We will use Proposition
B.7
several times to deal with the operator
\mathbb{T}
and commutators. We show explicit bounds only for
\mathbb{H}_{1}
; the ones for
\mathbb{H}_{2}
follow similarly. We have:

\begin{split}||\mathbb{H}_{1}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||\mathbb{T}\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B][\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{div}R-\nabla p][\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\Theta||_{0}(||v||_{r+1+\alpha}||v||_{1}+||B||_{r+1+\alpha}||B||_{1})+||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}(||v||_{1}^{2}+||B||_{1}^{2})\\
&+||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}(||R||_{2}+||p||_{2})+||\Theta||_{0}(||R||_{r+2+\alpha}+||p||_{r+2+\alpha})\\
&\lesssim h_{1}^{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}+h_{1}^{r}||\Theta||_{0}.\\
\end{split}

The commutator estimates are done similarly, using the assumptions on the transport derivatives:

\begin{split}&||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\mathbb{T}\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}-\mathbb{T}\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B][\Theta]-\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B][\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\mathbb{T}\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}v)^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathbb{T}\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}v)||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathbb{T}\mathrm{D}(z^{\pm})^{\top}\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathbb{T}\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\mathrm{D}(z^{\pm})^{\top}\mathrm{D}v^{\top}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathbb{T}\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}B)^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathbb{T}\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}B)||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathbb{T}\mathrm{D}(z^{\pm})^{\top}\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathbb{T}\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\mathrm{D}(z^{\pm})^{\top}\mathrm{D}B^{\top}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathbb{T}]\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{div}[\mathcal{A}^{\pm}R][\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}+||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathrm{D}\mathrm{div}](R)[\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathrm{D}[\nabla\mathcal{A}^{\pm}p][\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}+||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathrm{D}\nabla](p)[\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\left[h_{1}^{r}||\Theta||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\right]\end{split}

where we used that according to (
6.2
), we have
\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B=\mathrm{div}\ R-\nabla p
, together with the following computations:

\begin{split}&||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathbb{T}]\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||z^{\pm}||_{1+\alpha}||\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B||_{r+\alpha}+||z^{\pm}||_{r+1+\alpha}||\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B||_{\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+3}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}\delta_{q}^{3/2}||\Theta||_{\alpha}+\lambda_{q}^{3}\ell^{-2\alpha}\delta_{q}^{3/2}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\\
&=\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\left[h_{1}^{r}||\Theta||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\right].\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\mathrm{D}\mathrm{div}R&=\mathrm{D}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\mathrm{div}R-(\mathrm{D}\mathrm{div}R)(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm})=\mathrm{D}\left[\mathrm{div}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}R-\nabla v:\nabla R\right]-(\mathrm{D}\mathrm{div}R)(\mathrm{D}z^{\pm})\\
\end{split}

where
(\nabla v:\nabla R)^{i}=\partial_{j}v^{k}\partial_{k}R^{ij}
, from which we deduce:

||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathrm{D}\mathrm{div}](R)[\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\left[h_{1}^{r}||\Theta||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\right].

Similar bounds hold for the terms involving
p
.

We are left with the commutator with the pure time-derivative, which is non-zero as the vector fields are time-dependent. We first compute:

\begin{split}||\partial_{t}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B]||_{r+\alpha}&=||\partial_{t}[\mathrm{div}R-\nabla p]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&=||\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}\partial_{t}R+\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}[\partial_{t}[v\cdot\nabla v-B\cdot\nabla B]]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&=||\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}\partial_{t}R+2\nabla\Delta^{-1}\mathrm{div}[[\partial_{t}v\cdot\nabla v-\partial_{t}B\cdot\nabla B]]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\partial_{t}R||_{r+1+\alpha}+||[\partial_{t}v\cdot\nabla v-\partial_{t}B\cdot\nabla B]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\\
\end{split}

where we used that:

\mathrm{div}\ a=\mathrm{div}\ b=0\Longrightarrow\mathrm{div}[a\cdot\nabla b]=\mathrm{div}[b\cdot\nabla a]

and deduced the pure time derivatives of
v,\ R
by writing:

\partial_{t}v=\mathcal{A}^{\pm}v-z^{\pm}\cdot\nabla v,

similarly, for
B,\ R
. We use this bound in the following estimate:

\begin{split}&||[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\partial_{t}\mathbb{T}\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}-\mathbb{T}\left[\mathrm{D}v^{\top}\partial_{t}\Theta\times\nabla v+\mathrm{D}B^{\top}\partial_{t}\Theta\times\nabla B\right]^{\top}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+||\partial_{t}\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B][\Theta]-\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B][\partial_{t}\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\partial_{t}v||_{r+1+\alpha}||\Theta||_{0}||v||_{1}+||\partial_{t}v||_{1}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}||v||_{1}+||\partial_{t}v||_{1}||\Theta||_{0}||v||_{r+1+\alpha}\\
&+||\partial_{t}B||_{r+1+\alpha}||\Theta||_{0}||B||_{1}+||\partial_{t}B||_{1}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}||B||_{1}+||\partial_{t}B||_{1}||\Theta||_{0}||B||_{r+1+\alpha}\\
&+||\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B]||_{r+\alpha}||\Theta||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}[\mathrm{D}_{t}v-B\cdot\nabla B]||_{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}\left[h_{1,t}^{r}||\Theta||_{0}+h_{1,t}^{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\right].\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants in the above bounds depend on
r
and
\alpha
.
∎

6.2.
Existence and Uniqueness

Lemma 6.3

(Existence and Uniqueness)
.

Let
r\geq 1
integer,
0<\alpha<1
and
0<T<\infty
. Let
z^{\pm}\in C([-T,T],C^{r+1+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3}))
vector fields and
F\in C([-T,T],C^{r+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3}))
a 1-form, define
\mathcal{A}^{\pm}=\partial_{t}+z^{\pm}\cdot\nabla
and assume

\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}=\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+},

moreover, let

\mathbb{H}_{1}:C^{r^{\prime}+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})\to C^{r^{\prime}+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3}),\ \ \ \ \mathbb{H}_{2}:C^{r^{\prime}+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})\times C^{r^{\prime}+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})\to C^{r^{\prime}+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})

be bounded linear operators for
0\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
. Consider the initial value problem

(6.15)

\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta+\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]=F,\\
\Theta|_{t=0}=G,\\
\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta|_{t=0}=H\\
\end{cases}

for a 1-form
\Theta
. Then for any pair
G,H\in C([-T,T],C^{r+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
the problem (
6.15
) admits a unique solution such that:

\Theta\in C([-T,T],C^{r+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3})).

Proof of Lemma
6.3
.

We adopt the notation and definitions in Subsection
6.3
. We begin with the following claim:

(6.16)

X_{s^{\prime}}^{+}\circ\Phi^{+}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi_{t}^{-}=X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{s^{\prime}-s+t}\circ X_{s^{\prime}-s+t}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+}

whenever all the objects are well defined, equivalently

X_{s^{\prime},s}^{+}\circ X_{s,t}^{-}=X_{s^{\prime},s^{\prime}-s+t}^{-}\circ X^{+}_{s^{\prime}-s+t,t}.

Recall from Subsection
3.2
that

\mathbb{X}^{\pm}_{s}(x,t)=(X^{\pm}_{t+s,t}(x),t+s)

and since
\mathcal{A}^{\pm}
commute by assumption, we have:

\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}=\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Longrightarrow\mathbb{X}^{+}_{a}\circ\mathbb{X}_{b}^{-}=\mathbb{X}^{-}_{b}\circ\mathbb{X}_{a}^{+}

for any
a,b
such that the composition is well defined, we now apply it with
a=s^{\prime}-s,\ b=s-t
and obtain:

\begin{split}(X_{s^{\prime},s}^{+}\circ X_{s,t}^{-}(x),s^{\prime})&=(X_{s^{\prime},\cdot}^{+}\circ\mathbb{X}_{s-t}^{-}(x,t),s^{\prime})\\
&=\mathbb{X}^{+}_{s^{\prime}-s}\circ\mathbb{X}_{s-t}^{-}(x,t)\\
&=\mathbb{X}_{s-t}^{-}\circ\mathbb{X}^{+}_{s^{\prime}-s}(x,t)\\
&=\mathbb{X}_{s-t}^{-}(X^{+}_{s^{\prime}-s+t,t}(x),s^{\prime}-s+t)\\
&=(X_{s^{\prime},s^{\prime}-s+t}^{-}\circ X^{+}_{s^{\prime}-s+t,t}(x),s^{\prime})\end{split}

where
\circ
can be either a composition in space or space-time. This is the claim in (
6.16
). It follows that:

(6.17)

\begin{split}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{s^{\prime}-s+t}\circ X_{s^{\prime}-s+t}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}(F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{s^{\prime}-s+t}\circ X_{s^{\prime}-s+t}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s\right]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}(F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{u}\circ X_{u}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u\right]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{u}(F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{+}\circ\Phi^{+}_{u}\circ X_{u}^{-}\circ\Phi_{t}^{-},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}u\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{+}\circ\Phi^{+}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi_{t}^{-},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

where we used the fact that the integration is over the triangle
t_{0}\leq s^{\prime}\leq s\leq t
to switch the order of integration using Fubini’s theorem.

Local Existence and Uniqueness.
As we will prove detailed a priori estimates later, we will only be brief here. We want to find a solution of (
6.15
) on
\mathbb{T}^{3}
for some short time interval
[t_{0},t_{0}+\tau]
(or
[t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}]
) first. We will do so by a Banach Fixed Point argument. A solution of (
6.13
) by the Duhamel formula, see (
A.14
) for the scalar case applied twice, corresponds to a fixed point of the map:

(6.18)

\begin{split}\mathbb{F}[\Theta]&=\underbrace{G(\Phi_{t}^{+})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}H(\Phi^{-}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+})\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s}_{I}\\
&+\underbrace{\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(-\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]-\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta])(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s}_{II}.\\
\end{split}

We now choose a space to set the argument, namely, the closed ball:

\mathbb{B}_{R,\tau,t_{0}}=\left\{\Theta\in C([t_{0},t_{0}+\tau];C^{r+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3}):\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta\in C([t_{0},t_{0}+\tau];C^{r+\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{3}),\ ||\Theta||_{\mathbb{B}}\leq R\right\}

where the norm is defined as:

||\Theta||_{\mathbb{B}}:=\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{0}+\tau]}\left[||\Theta(\cdot,t)||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta(\cdot,t)||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta(\cdot,t)||_{r+\alpha}\right],

in particular,
\mathbb{B}_{R,\tau,t_{0}}
is a complete metric space. Let
R\geq 1
be so large that for any
0<\tau<1
for
I
as in (
6.18
) we have:

||I||_{\mathbb{B}}\leq R/2

this depends on the
C^{r}
norm of the initial data and the
C^{r+1}
norm of
z^{\pm}
but not on
\tau
.

Now from (
6.17
) we deduce:

\mathcal{A}^{\pm}II=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta])(X^{\mp}_{s}\circ\Phi_{t}^{\mp},s)\mathrm{d}s

where we used that, eventually commuting the flows using (
6.17
), when
\mathcal{A}^{\pm}
hit the integrand, we get zero, leaving us with the time derivatives of the integral itself.

From this and (
6.18
) we deduce:

||\mathbb{F}\Theta||_{\mathbb{B}}\leq R/2+\tau C||\mathbb{H}_{1}||_{op}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}+\tau C^{\prime}||\mathbb{H}_{2}||_{op}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta||_{r+\alpha})

for some
C,C^{\prime}
depending on the
C^{r+1}
norm of
z^{\pm}
and we can choose
\tau
small enough to ensure

\tau[C||H_{1}||_{op}+C^{\prime}||H_{2}||_{op}]\leq 1/2

and we conclude that for
\Theta\in\mathbb{B}_{R,\tau}
we have:

(6.19)

||\mathbb{F}\Theta||_{\mathbb{B}}\leq R\Longrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mathbb{B}_{R,\tau,t_{0}})\subset\mathbb{B}_{R,\tau,t_{0}}.

We move to the contraction property. We set
\Delta=\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1}
and compute:

(6.20)

\begin{split}\mathbb{F}[\Theta_{1}]-\mathbb{F}[\Theta_{2}]&=-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta_{1}]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta_{1},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta_{1}])(\dots,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta_{2}]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta_{2},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta_{2}])(\dots,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Delta]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Delta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Delta])(\dots,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

from which we deduce:

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}[\mathbb{F}[\Theta_{1}]-\mathbb{F}[\Theta_{2}]]&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Delta]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Delta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Delta])(X^{\mp}_{s}\circ\Phi_{t}^{\mp},s)\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

and we conclude that:

||\mathbb{F}[\Theta_{1}]-\mathbb{F}[\Theta_{2}]||_{\mathbb{B}}\leq C\tau||\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}||_{\mathbb{B}}

where
C
is some constant that depends on the
C^{r+1}
norm of
z^{\pm}
and the operator norms of
\mathbb{H}_{1},\ \mathbb{H}_{2}
but not on the initial data, eventually making
\tau
smaller we can ensure:

(6.21)

||\mathbb{F}[\Theta_{1}]-\mathbb{F}[\Theta_{2}]||_{\mathbb{B}}\leq\frac{1}{2}||\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}||_{\mathbb{B}}

and the desired contraction property follows for
\mathbb{F}
in the space
\mathbb{B}_{R,\tau,t_{0}}
. Note that
\tau
does not depend on
t_{0},\ R
.

Existence then follows from (
6.19
), (
6.21
) and an application of Proposition
B.2
.

Uniqueness.
Given the regularity of the solution, uniqueness is a consequence of (
6.20
) and Grönwall Lemma.

Long Time Existence:
We can now cover
[-T,T]
with finitely many intervals of length
\tau
of the form
[t_{j},t_{j}+\tau]
. We then construct an initial solution given the data
(G,H)
on
[0,\tau]
and extend it forward and backwards finitely many times, using the value of the solution at
t_{j-1}+\tau
as initial condition. This requires increasing the value of
R=R_{j}
in the fixed point argument above finitely many times to ensure the iteratively constructed initial data lies in the space
\mathbb{B}_{\tau,R_{j},t_{j}}
, uniqueness guarantees the existence of a global solution given any finite
T
.
∎

6.3.
A Priori Estimates

With the existence and uniqueness of the problem (
6.13
) at hand, we now want to prove a priori estimates. The key observations are the following:

•

The commutation (
6.9
) of the Alfvén transport operators
\mathcal{A}^{\pm}
allows for the propagation of
C^{r+\alpha}
estimates on the solution. Despite its geometric nature, (
6.13
) effectively behaves as a system of first-order PDEs.

•

The particular choice of forcing allows, basically by integration by parts (IBP) in time, to trade a
\tau^{a}
gain of smallness on the solution for a transport derivative of the forcing
\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\mathrm{div}F
. Given the second-order nature of the equation, it is possible to gain a second
\tau^{a}
; one then needs to ensure that good second-order transport properties of the forcing are available. We do not pursue this here and gain only
\tau^{c}
coming from the restriction of the time-domain of the solution
|t-t_{0}|<\tau^{c}
. This gives a total gain of
\tau^{a}\tau^{c}
on the solution compared to the
C^{\alpha}
norm of the right-hand side. Given this IBP procedure, it is convenient to set:

(6.22)

G=0,\ H=\tau f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}

as initial data for (
6.13
).

•

The restriction on the time interval on which we solve the equation, also guarantees that:

|t-t_{0}|||z^{\pm}||_{1+\alpha}\lesssim\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}=\left(\frac{\ell^{-1}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{\alpha}

and given any
\alpha,\ b
we can always choose
a
sufficiently large to reabsorb the implicit constant, and bound:

|t-t_{0}|||z^{\pm}||_{1+\alpha}\leq 1.

This will be used several times in the Duhamel formula + Grönwall type of arguments we are about to perform, and we will do so without further mention.

Given any solution
\Theta
, we split it into two different components,

\Theta=\Theta^{p}+\Theta^{h}
. The rationale behind this is the following:

•

\Theta^{p}
will be the principal part of the solution, taking care of the forcing term and the initial conditions. Estimates will be done explicitly.

•

\Theta^{h}
will be the part of the solution taking care of the homogeneous problem with zero initial conditions. Estimates will be implicit and given by subsequent Grönwall arguments.

More precisely, they solve:

(6.23)

\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}=-\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]-\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta],\\
\Theta^{h}|_{t=t_{0}}=0,\\
\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}|_{t=t_{0}}=0\end{cases}\ \text{ and }\ \begin{cases}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}=f\mathbb{T}F,\\
\Theta^{p}|_{t=t_{0}}=0,\\
\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}|_{t=t_{0}}=\tau f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}.\end{cases}

We remark here that for any solution of the above problem, we have:

\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}=\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}-2B\cdot\nabla\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}

and thus for initial conditions such that
\Theta\equiv 0
at
t=t_{0}
one has
B\cdot\nabla\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}=v\cdot\nabla\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}=0

and we conclude that:

\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}=0\Longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}=\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}=\partial_{t}\Theta|_{t=t_{0}}.

We now set
X^{\pm},\ \Phi^{\pm}
to be the Lagrangian and inverse flow map of
z^{\pm}=v\pm B
, respectively. That is, we solve:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}X^{\pm}=z^{\pm}(X^{\pm}),\\
X^{\pm}|_{t=t_{0}}=\mathrm{Id}\end{cases}\ \text{ and }\ \begin{cases}\partial_{t}\Phi^{\pm}+z^{\pm}\cdot\nabla\Phi^{\pm}=0,\\
\Phi^{\pm}|_{t=t_{0}}=\mathrm{Id}.\end{cases}

It follows from the Standing Assumptions
1
, Propositions
B.5
,
B.4
and the remarks above that:

(6.24)

\begin{split}&||\mathrm{D}X^{\pm}-\mathrm{Id}||_{\alpha},\ ||\mathrm{D}\Phi^{\pm}-\mathrm{Id}||_{\alpha}\leq 1\\
&||\mathrm{D}X^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha},\ ||\mathrm{D}\Phi^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1.\end{split}

The goal of this Section is to prove the following Lemma.

Lemma 6.4

(A Priori Estimates)
.

Let
r\geq 0
an integer. Under the Standing Assumptions
1
we have:

(6.25a)

\displaystyle||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1},

(6.25b)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},

(6.25c)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta||_{r+\alpha},||\partial_{t}^{j}(\mathcal{A}^{\pm})^{2}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j}\lambda_{q}^{[r+j-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1.

Moreover, on

I^{cut}=[t_{0}-\tau^{c},\ t_{0}-2/3\tau^{c}]\cup[t_{0}+2/3\tau^{c},\ t_{0}+\tau^{c}]

we have the improved second-order transport estimate:

(6.26)

\begin{split}&||\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta|_{I^{cut}}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}.\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants depend on
r,\ \alpha
and the ones in the data from the Standing Assumptions
1
.

Remark 24
.

As expected, given the second-order nature of the equation, one loses two good derivatives from the
\underline{r}
we assume in the Standing Assumptions
1
. This loss, however, occurs because of the coefficients in the operator
\mathbb{H}_{1}
, see Lemma
6.2
and not because of the second-order transport. To avoid an additional loss in the special transport
(\mathcal{A}^{\pm})^{2}\Theta
estimate, we fix the same
\underline{r}
for the transport estimate in
1
, although a more natural choice would be
\underline{r}-1
. In the applications, this will not matter as
R,\ B,\ v
enjoy much better lower bounds compared to
F
, and
F
has this property by construction, see Lemma
2.7
.

Proof of Lemma
6.4
.

We will deal with each part of the solution separately and leave the ‘special’ second-order transport
(\mathcal{A}^{\pm})^{2}
estimates at the end. Those are not coming from the equation itself but follow as a consequence of the transport bounds we assume on the vector fields and forcings. At the very end, we handle the pure time-derivative bound. In the following,
||\cdot||_{r+\alpha}
will mean the
C^{r+\alpha}_{x}
Hölder norm for each fixed time, moreover for notational convenience we always assume
t_{0}\leq t<t_{0}+\tau^{c}
; the estimates for
t_{0}+\tau^{c}<t\leq t_{0}
can be done similarly, as the Duhamel representation formulas we are about to use do not change.

A Priori Estimates -
\Theta^{p}
.
Representing
\Theta^{p}
via the Duhamel formula, see (
A.14
) for the scalar case applied twice, we obtain:

\begin{split}\Theta^{p}&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}H(\Phi^{-}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+})\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(f\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s.\\
\end{split}

Set
\Psi=\Phi^{-}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+}
. We look first at the term coming from the forcing:

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}(f\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi,s^{\prime})|_{s^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{s^{\prime}=s}\right]\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s)-\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F)(\Psi,t_{0})\right]\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s)-H(\Psi)\right]\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

and we conclude that:

\begin{split}\Theta^{p}&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s)\right]\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{s}\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{s}\circ X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right]\mathrm{d}s.\\
\end{split}

This cancellation is why we chose (
6.22
) as the initial data. With this representation at hand, the bounds in the Standing Assumptions
1
and (
6.24
), the composition estimates in Propositions
B.3
, and Proposition
B.7
to deal with
\mathbb{T}
, we can compute:

(6.27)

\begin{split}&||\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\sup_{|s-t_{0}|,|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}||(f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s)||_{r+\alpha}\\
&+(\tau^{c})^{2}\tau^{a}\sup_{|s-t_{0}|,|s^{\prime}-t_{0}|,|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}||(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi,s^{\prime})||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\overset{r=0}{\leq}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\sup_{|s-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}||(f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F)(\cdot,s)||_{r+\alpha}+(\tau^{c})^{2}\tau^{a}\sup_{|s^{\prime}-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}||(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F)(\cdot,s^{\prime})||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\sup_{|s-t_{0}|,|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}\left[||f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F||_{1+\alpha}||X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+}||_{r+\alpha}+||f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F||_{r+\alpha}||X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+}||_{1+\alpha}^{r}\right]||X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+}||_{1}^{\alpha}\\
&+(\tau^{c})^{2}\tau^{a}\sup_{|s-t_{0}|,|s^{\prime}-t_{0}|,|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}||f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F||_{1+\alpha}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi||_{r+\alpha}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi||_{1+\alpha}^{r}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi||_{1}^{\alpha}\\
&+(\tau^{c})^{2}\tau^{a}\sup_{|s-t_{0}|,|s^{\prime}-t_{0}|,|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}||f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F||_{r+\alpha}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi||_{1+\alpha}^{r}||X_{s^{\prime}}^{-}\circ\Psi||_{1}^{\alpha}\\
&\overset{r\geq 0}{\lesssim}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\end{split}

where we used
\sup_{t}|f^{[1]}|\lesssim 1
to get rid of
f^{[1]}
and the computation:

\begin{split}||f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||\mathbb{T}\mathcal{A}^{-}F||_{r+\alpha}+||[\mathcal{A}^{-},\mathbb{T}]F||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}^{-}F||_{r+\alpha}+||z^{\pm}||_{r+1+\alpha}||F||_{\alpha}+||z^{\pm}||_{1+\alpha}||F||_{r+\alpha}\\
\end{split}

together with
\tau^{c}\ell^{-\alpha}\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\leq 1
.

Transport Estimates.
Applying the operator
\mathcal{A}^{+}
to this representation formula and renaming the dummy variable
s^{\prime}
into
s
we see that:

(6.28)

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}&=\tau^{a}f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi_{t}^{-},s)\mathrm{d}s.\\
\end{split}

We claim a similar representation for the
\mathcal{A}^{-}
transport. The commutativity
\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}=\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}
, the Duhamel representation formula, together with the fact remarked above that:

\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}|_{t=t_{0}}=\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}|_{t=t_{0}}=H

and repeating the integration by parts, gives indeed:

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}&=H(\Phi_{t}^{+})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(f\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s)\mathrm{d}s\\
&=H(\Phi_{t}^{+})+\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s)|_{s=t_{0}}^{s=t}-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\tau^{a}f^{[1]}(s)(\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s)\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\tau^{a}f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\tau^{a}(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{+}\circ\Phi_{t}^{+},s)\mathrm{d}s.\\
\end{split}

With the representations at hand, arguing as in (
6.27
) above, we can bound:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\tau^{a}||f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F||_{r+\alpha}+\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\sup_{|s-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}||(f^{[1]}\mathcal{A}^{\mp}\mathbb{T}F)(X_{s}^{\mp}\circ\Phi_{t}^{\mp},s)||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.\end{split}

The standard second-order transport estimates can be read directly from the equation, given the commutation of the Alfvén operators, indeed

||\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}=||\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}=||f\mathbb{T}F||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim||\mathbb{T}F||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim||F||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}.

Additionally, note that this is zero on
I^{cut}
as
F
is according to the Standing Assumptions
1
.

Special Transport.
We are left with
(\mathcal{A}^{\pm})^{2}\Theta
. We will do the
+
estimate the other one can be deduced similarly. Using again the Alfvén commutation we see that
(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta
solves:

\mathcal{A}^{-}(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta^{p}=\mathcal{A}^{+}(f\mathbb{T}F)=\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}f^{\prime}\mathbb{T}F+f\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{T}F\\

with initial conditions:

(6.29)

\begin{split}(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta^{p}|_{t=t_{0}}&=\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}|_{t=t_{0}}+2B\cdot\nabla\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}|_{t=t_{0}}\\
&=f\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}+2B|_{t=t_{0}}\cdot\nabla[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}|_{t=t_{0}}]\\
&=f\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}+(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})|_{t=t_{0}}[\tau^{a}f^{[1]}\mathbb{T}F]|_{t=t_{0}}\\
&=f\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}+\tau^{a}f^{[1]}(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}.\\
\end{split}

Representing the solution by the Duhamel formula and integrating by parts the ‘bad term’, we get:

\begin{split}(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta^{p}=f\mathbb{T}F+\tau^{a}f^{[1]}(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}(\Phi^{-}_{t})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}[f(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})\mathbb{T}F](X^{-}_{s}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t},s)\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

now estimating as above, we conclude:

\begin{split}||(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\sup_{|s-t_{0}|,|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}}\left[||F||_{r+\alpha}+\tau^{a}||(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}(\Phi^{\mp}_{t})||_{r+\alpha}+\tau^{c}||[(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})\mathbb{T}F](X^{-}_{s}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})||_{r+\alpha}\right]\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}.\end{split}

Gathering all the results we get:

(6.30a)

\displaystyle||\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1},

(6.30b)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},

(6.30c)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha},||(\mathcal{A}^{\pm})^{2}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1},

(6.30d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}|_{I^{cut}}\equiv 0

for
r\geq 0
.

A Priori Estimates -
\Theta^{h}
.
We recall that
\Theta^{h}
solves the problem (
6.23
) and argue similarly as above. The Duhamel formula, the commutation
\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}=\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}
and the zero initial conditions
\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}|_{t=t_{0}}=\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}|_{t=t_{0}}=0
allow us to write the following representation formula:

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta^{h}&=-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right)(X^{\mp}_{s}\circ\Phi^{\mp}_{t},s)\mathrm{d}s\\
&=-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta^{p}+\Theta^{h}]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}+\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}+\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}]\right)(X^{\mp}_{s}\circ\Phi^{\mp}_{t},s)\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\underbrace{-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta^{p}]\right)(X^{\mp}_{s}\circ\Phi^{\mp}_{t},s)\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}]\right)(X^{\mp}_{s}\circ\Phi^{\mp}_{t},s)\mathrm{d}s}_{I_{1}^{\pm}}\\
&\underbrace{-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta^{h}]\right)(X^{\mp}_{s}\circ\Phi^{\mp}_{t},s)\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}]\right)(X^{\mp}_{s}\circ\Phi^{\mp}_{t},s)\mathrm{d}s}_{I_{2}^{\pm}}.\\
\end{split}

We now bound
I_{1}^{\pm}
explicitly as they come from the principal part of the solution. Using the properties of
\mathbb{H}_{2}
from Lemma
6.2
, the estimates in (
6.30
), the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
and the bounds on the flow maps in (
6.24
), we compute:

(6.31)

\begin{split}||I_{1}^{\pm}||_{r+\alpha}&\overset{r=0}{\lesssim}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta^{p}]||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}]||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{1}^{0}||\Theta^{p}||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{2}^{0}\left(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}]||_{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\ell^{-2\alpha}(\tau^{c})^{2}\tau^{a}\lambda_{q}^{2}\delta_{q}\delta_{q+1}+\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\lesssim\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta^{p}]||_{1+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta^{p}]||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}]||_{1+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}]||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{1}^{1}||\Theta^{p}||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta^{p}||_{1+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{1}^{r}||\Theta^{p}||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{2}^{1}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}||_{\alpha})+h_{2}^{0}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}||_{1+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}||_{1+\alpha})\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{2}^{r}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}||_{\alpha})+h_{2}^{0}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}||_{r+\alpha})\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}(\tau^{c})^{2}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.\end{split}

The integrals
I_{2}^{\pm}
will be estimated implicitly via a double Grönwall argument. Computing as above, we first deduce:

(6.32)

\begin{split}||I^{\pm}_{2}||_{r+\alpha}&\overset{r=0}{\lesssim}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta^{h}]||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h},\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}]||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}+\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha})\mathrm{d}s\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{1}^{1}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta^{h}||_{1+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{1}^{r}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{2}^{1}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha})+h_{2}^{0}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{1+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{1+\alpha})\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}h_{2}^{r}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha})+h_{2}^{0}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha})\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+2}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{1+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s+\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha})\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha})\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{1+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{1+\alpha})\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

We now complete the argument for
r=0
, and use induction afterwards.

Base case:
r=0
.
Collecting the bounds in (
6.31
), (
6.32
) and summing the
\pm
estimates we obtain:

\begin{split}\sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}}||\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}&\lesssim\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}+\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}}||\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\end{split}

an application of Grönwall’s Lemma together with the standing assumption
|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}
leads to:

(6.33)

\begin{split}\sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}}||\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}&\lesssim\left[\tau^{a}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\right]e^{\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}}\\
&\lesssim\tau^{a}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

We now integrate along the flow, using the fact that
\Theta^{h}|_{t=t_{0}}=0
. Namely, we write:

(6.34)

\begin{split}\Theta^{h}(t)&=\Theta^{h}(X^{+}_{t}\circ\Phi^{+}_{t},t)=\Theta^{h}(X^{+}_{t}\circ\Phi^{+}_{t},t)-\Theta^{h}(\Phi^{+}_{t},t_{0})\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\partial_{s}\left[(\Theta^{h})(X^{+}_{s}\circ\Phi^{+}_{t})\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h})(X^{+}_{s}\circ\Phi^{+}_{t})\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

and using (
6.33
) together with
|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}
we deduce:
