\begin{split}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}&\lesssim\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\int_{t_{0}}^{s}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
\end{split}

and a final Grönwall argument with subsequent use of (
6.33
) gives:

(6.35)

||\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}\lesssim\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},\ \ \ \ ||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta^{h}||_{\alpha}\lesssim\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1},

this concludes the base case of the induction.

Induction Step:
r\geq 1
.
Now, assume we proved for
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r-1
that:

\begin{split}&||\Theta^{h}||_{r^{\prime}+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{[r^{\prime}-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1},\\
&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta^{h}||_{r^{\prime}+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{[r^{\prime}-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\\
\end{split}

where the implicit constants depend on
\alpha
and
r^{\prime}
. From (
6.31
), (
6.32
) and arguing as above we deduce:

\begin{split}\sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}}||\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}&\leq\sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\},\ i=1,2}||I_{i}^{\sigma}||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s,\end{split}

we now apply Grönwall lemma

(6.36)

\begin{split}\sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}}||\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\left[\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\right]e^{\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

and integrating along the flow
\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}
as in (
6.34
), together with the bounds in (
6.30
), we deduce:

\begin{split}||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||(\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h})(X^{+}_{s}\circ\Phi^{+}_{t})||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\int_{t_{0}}^{s}||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}\tau^{c}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
\end{split}

and we conclude as above that:

(6.37)

||\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}

and

(6.38)

||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}.

A Priori Estimates -
\Theta
.
Since
\Theta=\Theta^{p}+\Theta^{h}
, collecting the bounds in (
6.30
) and (
6.37
), (
6.38
), results in:

(6.39)

\begin{split}&||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1},\\
&||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\\
\end{split}

for
r\geq 0
.

Special Transport
(\mathcal{A}^{\pm})^{2}\Theta
.
We write the details for
(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta
only. Applying
\mathcal{A}^{+}
to (
6.13
) and commuting we obtain:

(6.40)

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}f^{\prime}\mathbb{T}F+f\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{T}F&=\mathcal{A}^{+}f\mathbb{T}F\\
&=\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta+\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]+\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\\
&=\mathcal{A}^{-}(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta+\mathbb{H}_{1}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\\
&+[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta+[\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta].\end{split}

Let

H=(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta|_{t_{0}}=f\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}+\tau^{a}(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}

be the initial condition for
(\mathcal{A}^{\pm})^{2}\Theta
, where the second equality follows as in (
6.29
). Integrating along the flow of
\mathcal{A}^{-}
we obtain the following representation formula:

\begin{split}(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta&=H(\Phi^{-})-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\mathbb{H}_{1}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]+\mathbb{H}_{2}[(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})\mathrm{d}s\\
&-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta+[\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}f^{\prime}\mathbb{T}F+f\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{T}F\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\underbrace{\tau^{a}(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}(\Phi^{-})+f\mathbb{T}F+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[f(\mathcal{A}^{+}-\mathcal{A}^{-})\mathbb{T}F-\mathbb{H}_{1}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})\mathrm{d}s}_{I_{1}}\\
&-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[\mathbb{H}_{2}[(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})\mathrm{d}s-\underbrace{\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta+[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})\mathrm{d}s}_{I_{2}}.\\
\end{split}

We focus on
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
separately.

Estimates on
I_{2}
.
The estimates on the commutator
[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathbb{H}_{2}],\ [\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]
involving the Alfvén transport derivatives of the coefficients of the operators
\mathbb{H}_{1},\ \mathbb{H}_{2}
were given in Lemma
6.2
. We have:

\begin{split}||I_{2}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\left[[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta+[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\overset{r=0}{\lesssim}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta+[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}^{2}\ell^{-2\alpha}\delta_{q}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta||_{\alpha})+\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}^{3}\ell^{-2\alpha}\delta_{q}^{3/2}||\Theta||_{\alpha}\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\left[[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta+[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right]||_{1+\alpha}||X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t}||_{r+\alpha}||(X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})||_{1}^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\left[[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta+[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right]||_{r+\alpha}||(X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})||_{1+\alpha}^{r}||(X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})||_{1}^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\left(h_{1}^{1}||\Theta||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta||_{1+\alpha}+h_{2}^{1}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta||_{\alpha})\right)\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r-1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}h_{2}^{0}\left(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta||_{1+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta||_{1+\alpha}\right)\\
&+\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\left(h_{1}^{r}||\Theta||_{\alpha}+h_{1}^{0}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\right)\\
&+\tau^{c}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\left[h_{2}^{r}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta||_{\alpha})+h_{2}^{0}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta||_{r+\alpha})\right]\\
&\overset{r\geq 0}{\lesssim}\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-2\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\delta_{q+1}\end{split}

Estimates on
I_{1}
.
We first use the commutator estimates in Proposition
B.7
and the Alfvén transport estimates in the Standing Assumpions
1
to bound:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\mathbb{T}F||_{r+\alpha}&\leq||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathbb{T}]F||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathbb{T}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}F||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||[\mathcal{A}^{\pm},\mathbb{T}]F||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}F||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim||z^{\pm}||_{r+1+\alpha}||F||_{\alpha}+||z^{\pm}||_{1+\alpha}||F||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}F||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}1/\tau^{c}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\end{split}

and from this, arguing as above, we conclude:

\begin{split}||I_{1}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\tau^{a}||\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{T}F|_{t=t_{0}}(\Phi^{-})||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathbb{T}F||_{r+\alpha}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\left[f\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathbb{T}F+f\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{T}F-\mathbb{H}_{1}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\\
\end{split}

Conclusion.
Gathering the estimates on
I_{1},\ I_{2}
and proceeding as before we obtain:

\begin{split}||(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||\left[\mathbb{H}_{2}[(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right](X_{s}^{-}\circ\Phi^{-}_{t})||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\overset{r=0}{\lesssim}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta||_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}\\
&+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}||(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta||_{\alpha}+\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}||(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta||_{1+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q}^{1/2}||(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\\
\end{split}

and arguing inductively by means of Grönwall Lemma, as in the estimates for
\Theta^{h}
, we conclude that:

||(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}.

Improved Estimates on
I^{cut}
.

We can read the second-order transport estimate for
\Theta^{h}
directly from (
6.23
) and (
6.39
). Indeed:

\begin{split}||\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{h}||_{r+\alpha}&\lesssim||\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}\\
&\lesssim h_{1}^{r}||\Theta||_{\alpha}+h_{1}||\Theta||_{r+\alpha}+h_{2}^{r}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta||_{\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]||_{\alpha})+h_{2}(||\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}+||\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]||_{r+\alpha})\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}\end{split}

and given the improved estimate for
\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta^{p}|_{I^{cut}}=0
see (
6.30
), we conclude:

||\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta|_{I^{cut}}||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}(\tau^{a}/\tau^{c})\delta_{q+1}.

Pure time derivative.
From the full equation (
6.13
) we obtain:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}[\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]&=\partial_{t}\left[f\mathbb{T}F-\mathbb{H}_{1}[\Theta]-\mathbb{H}_{2}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\right]\\
&=(\partial_{t}f)\mathbb{T}F+f\mathbb{T}\partial_{t}F-\mathbb{H}_{1}[\partial_{t}\Theta]-\mathbb{H}_{2}[\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\\
&-[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H}_{1}][\Theta]-[\partial_{t},\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta],\end{split}

now, note that to get the bounds on the pure time derivatives, we can write:

\partial_{t}\Theta=\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta-z^{+}\cdot\nabla\Theta,\ \partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta=\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta-z^{+}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta

and estimate each term separately, given the bounds in (
6.39
). We first deduce:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}[b-1]\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\tau^{c}\delta_{q+1},\\
&||\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\Theta||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}[b-1]\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\\
\end{split}

and then conclude:

||\partial_{t}[\mathcal{A}^{-}\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}[b-1]\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}

where we used the bounds in Lemma
6.2
to estimate the commutator terms.

Finally, from (
6.40
) we obtain:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\left[(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta\right]&=\mathcal{A}^{-}(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta-z^{-}\cdot\nabla(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\\
&=\frac{1}{\tau^{a}}f^{\prime}\mathbb{T}F+f\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{T}F-\mathbb{H}_{1}[\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta]-\mathbb{H}_{2}[(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]\\
&-[\mathcal{A}^{+},\mathbb{H}_{1}]\Theta-[\mathcal{A}^{+}\mathbb{H}_{2}][\mathcal{A}^{+}\Theta,\mathcal{A}^{-}\Theta]-z^{-}\cdot\nabla(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\end{split}

and conclude that also in this case we have:

||\partial_{t}\left[(\mathcal{A}^{+})^{2}\Theta\right]||_{r+\alpha}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}\lambda_{q}^{[r+1-(\underline{r}-2)]^{+}[b-1]\gamma_{\ell}}\ell^{-\alpha}\delta_{q+1}.

∎

namely a map

7.
Chart Construction

In this section, we want to construct a chart
\Psi
from
Q_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})=B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\times B_{\tau^{c}}(t_{0})\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, that given
(v,B)
two divergence-free time-dependent vector fields solving on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
the Faraday-Ohm system (
1.1
), ‘straightens’ simultaneously
B,\ \partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
. We will call such a chart
\Psi

adapted
to
(v,B)
.

We now clarify what we mean by ‘straightens’ and all the properties we will require on
\Psi
.

(1)

Chart property:
we think of
\Psi
as a map:

\Psi:Q_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\to\mathbb{R}^{3}

such that the restriction to each time slice

\Psi(\cdot,t):B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\to\mathbb{R}^{3}

is a diffeomorphism onto its image, close to the identity and in particular
\mathrm{D}\Psi
is invertible.

(2)

Magnetic field straightening
: the chart sends the magnetic field to a constant vector parallel to
e_{3}
, namely

\Psi(\cdot,t)^{*}e_{3}=cB(\cdot,t)

where the pullback is meant as a map

B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\to\mathbb{R}^{3}\ \ x\mapsto\Psi(x,t)

for each fixed
t
and we write
e_{1},\ e_{2},\ e_{3}
for the coordinate vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
c_{0}/C_{0}\leq c\leq C_{0}/c_{0}
is a constant.

(3)

Transport straightening:
the chart sends the transport along
v
operator to a constant vector parallel to
e_{4}
, namely

\Psi^{*}e_{4}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla

where the pullback is meant as a map

Q_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\to\mathbb{R}^{4}\ \ (x,t)\mapsto(\Psi(x,t),t)

and we write
e_{4}
for the coordinate vector corresponding to
\mathbb{R}
.

To make the construction quantitative we assume the following bounds:

(7.1a)

\displaystyle||v||_{r},||B||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}L(r)\delta_{q}^{1/2}\text{ for }\ r\geq 1,

(7.1b)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}v||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+1}L(r+1)\delta_{q}^{1/2}\text{ for }\ r\geq 0,

(7.1c)

\displaystyle c_{0}\leq|B(x,t)|\leq C_{0},\ |v(x,t)|\leq C_{0}\ \ \forall(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},\ t_{0}+\tau^{c})

where
L:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
is a loss function in the sense of Definition
8.1
and
c_{0},\ C_{0}
are constants. The pure derivative bound is needed for the applications we have in mind, since we want to estimate second-order pure time derivatives of the chart; it wouldn’t be needed otherwise.

The assumed differential condition (
1.1
) corresponds to the commutation of
\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla,\ B\cdot\nabla
as space-time vector fields. The following Lemma states that this is a sufficient condition for achieving the above properties of the chart, and it is just a specific instance of the Frobenius Theorem in the context of magnetohydrodynamics. Since we need estimates on
\Psi
, we need to make the standard construction quantitative. We direct the reader to Appendix
A
for the notation and conventions used.

Lemma 7.1

(Chart Construction)
.

Given divergence-free vector fields
(v,B)
solving (
1.1
) on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},t_{0}+\tau^{c})
and satisfying the estimates in (
7.1
), there exist a map

\Psi:Q_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\to\mathbb{R}^{3}

for which the properties
1
,
2
,
3
hold with quantitative bounds:

\begin{split}&||\mathrm{D}\Psi-\mathrm{Id}||_{0},\ ||(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0},\ ||\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi]-1||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},\\
&||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathrm{D}\Psi||_{r},\ ||\partial_{t}^{j}(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j}L(r+j+1)\ \text{ for }j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ r\geq 0\end{split}

where the implicit constants depend on
r
, the data in (
7.1
) and the norm is taken on
Q_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
.

Moreover, let
e
be a fixed unit vector, which we think of as either a constant 1-form or 2-form. We have:

\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v}^{1})\Psi^{1*}e=0,\\
\mathcal{L}_{B}^{1}\Psi^{1*}e=0\\
\end{cases}\ \text{ and }\ \begin{cases}(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\Psi^{2*}e=0,\\
\mathcal{L}_{B}\Psi^{2*}e=0.\\
\end{cases}

In addition, for any scalar function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
,
k\in e_{3}^{\perp}
define

\varphi_{k}(x)=\varphi(x\cdot k)
, which we regard as a function
\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
. We have:

\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)\Psi^{*}\varphi_{k}=0,\\
B\cdot\nabla\Psi^{*}\varphi_{k}=0.\\
\end{cases}

In particular, if
(k,\nu,\zeta)
is an orthonormal basis with
k\in e_{3}^{\perp}
, then

\xi=\mathrm{curl}[\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k}\nu)]=\varphi^{\prime}\Psi^{2*}\zeta

is a well-defined vector field on
Q_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
satisfying:

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\xi=0,\ \mathcal{L}_{B}\xi=0,\ \mathrm{div}\ \xi=0.

Remark 25
.

The Lie derivative as a two-form and as a vector field coincide, as
v,\ B
are divergence-free.

Remark 26
.

The size of the chart domain is optimal in the sense that:

||v||_{1}\tau^{c},\ ||B||_{1}\tau^{c}\sim 1

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

The construction of
\Psi
is done in two steps. We first freeze
B
at
t_{0}
and normalize it at
x_{0}
, we write:

\bar{B}(x)=\frac{B(t_{0},x)}{|B(t_{0},x_{0})|},

this is why
c=1/|B(t_{0},x_{0})|
appears in the property
2
above. We construct a chart
\Upsilon:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
straightening
\bar{B}
, in the sense that:

\Upsilon^{*}e_{3}=\bar{B}\ \text{ everywhere in }\ \Omega,

here
\Omega
is a special
2C_{0}\tau^{c}
neighborhood of
B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
which will be constructed later, we then solve on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},t_{0}+\tau^{c})
for the inverse and Lagrangian flow of
v
, that is

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\Phi+v\cdot\nabla\Phi=0,\\
\Phi|_{t=t_{0}}=\mathrm{Id}\end{cases}\text{ and }\begin{cases}\partial_{t}X=v(X),\\
X|_{t=t_{0}}=\mathrm{Id}\end{cases}

and set

\Psi(x,t)=\Upsilon\circ\Phi_{t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

Note that the composition
\Upsilon\circ\Phi
is well defined on
B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
. Indeed, given
7.1
points in
B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
can move at most
2C_{0}\tau^{c}
from their initial position and won’t thus leave
\Omega
on which
\Upsilon
is defined.

This takes care of the straightening of the magnetic field. Indeed, solving (
1.1
) in the smooth category according to (
A.14
) is equivalent to
cB=X_{*}\bar{B}
, and we can compute:

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}\Psi[cB]&=\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)\mathrm{D}\Phi[\mathrm{D}X(X^{-1})\bar{B}(X^{-1})]\\
&=\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)\mathrm{D}\Phi\mathrm{D}(X^{-1})^{-1}\bar{B}(\Phi)\\
&=\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)\mathrm{D}\Phi(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\bar{B}(\Phi)\\
&=\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)\bar{B}(\Phi)\\
&=e_{3}.\end{split}

Here we used that
\Phi(\cdot,t)=X^{-1}(\cdot,t)
, see (
A.15
), and
\bar{B}=\Upsilon^{*}e_{3}=(\mathrm{D}\Upsilon)^{-1}e_{3}
. This is equivalent to

\Psi(\cdot,t)^{*}e_{3}=cB
.

To see the straightening of
\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
as a space-time vector field, we first note that:

(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)\Psi=\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)[(\partial_{t}+\cdot\nabla v)\Phi]=0

and then, thinking of
\Psi
as a mapping
(x,t)\mapsto(\Psi(x,t),t)
, compute:

\mathrm{D}_{x,t}\Psi[\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla]=\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{D}_{x}\Psi&\partial_{t}\Psi\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}v\\
1\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)\Psi\\
1\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
1\end{bmatrix}=e_{4}

and we conclude that
\Psi^{*}e_{4}=(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)
under the proper identifications. We are then left with the construction of
\Upsilon
and
\Omega
.

Construction of
\Omega
.
We want
\Omega
to be a flow-box for
\bar{B}
centred at
x_{0}
and containing a
2C_{0}\tau^{c}
neighborhood of
B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
. We call the plane passing through
x_{0}
and orthogonal to
\bar{B}(x_{0})
,
\pi_{0}
. Eventually applying a fixed rotation, we may assume that
\bar{B}(x_{0})=B(x_{0},t_{0})/|B(x_{0},t_{0})|=e_{3}
. We then let
Y
solve:

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}Y=\bar{B}\left(Y\right),\\
Y|_{s=0}=\mathrm{Id}\end{cases}

fix
r_{0},\ s_{0}\geq 0
, and set

\Omega=\{Y_{s}(B_{r_{0}\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\cap\pi_{0}):|s|\leq s_{0}\tau^{c}\}.

We claim that for
r_{0},\ s_{0}
sufficiently large we have:

(7.2)

B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\subset B_{2C_{0}\tau^{c}}(B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0}))\subset B_{(1+2C_{0})\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\subset\Omega

where the first two inequalities are always satisfied independently of
r_{0},\ s_{0}
. To see this, we expand
Y_{s}
as follows:

\begin{split}Y_{s}(x)&=x+s\bar{B}(x)+\int_{0}^{s}(\bar{B}\cdot\nabla\bar{B})(Y_{s^{\prime}})(s-s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=x+s\bar{B}(x_{0})+\underbrace{s\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{D}\bar{B}(sx+(1-s)x_{0})[x-x_{0}]+\int_{0}^{s}(\bar{B}\cdot\nabla\bar{B})(Y_{s^{\prime}})(s-s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}}_{r(x,s)}\\
&=x+se_{3}+r(x,s).\end{split}

Note that for
|s|\leq s_{0}\tau^{c}
and
x\in B_{r_{0}\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\cap\pi_{0}\subset B_{r_{0}\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
, given our assumptions (
7.1
) and the definition of
\tau^{c}
in (
1.21
), we have:

|r(x,s)|\leq\tau^{c}\frac{C(s_{0}^{2}+s_{0}r_{0})}{\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}}

where the constant
C
is independent of
r_{0},s_{0}
. Now fix
r_{0}=s_{0}=(1+2C_{0})+2
for any
\alpha,\ b
we can pick
a
so large that

\frac{C(s_{0}^{2}+s_{0}r_{0})}{\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}}<1\Longrightarrow|r(x,s)|<\tau^{c}

we deduce

B_{(1+2C_{0})\tau^{c}+1}(x_{0})\subset\Omega

and conclude that (
7.2
) holds for this choice of
\Omega
.

Construction of
\Upsilon
.
We now set:

(7.3)

U=\{x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\sqrt{|x_{1}|^{2}+|x_{2}|^{2}}\leq r_{0}\tau^{c},|s|\leq s_{0}\tau^{c}\}

and we put new coordinates on
\Omega
by means of the map:

\Gamma:\mathbb{R}^{3}\supset U\ni(x_{1},x_{2},s)\longrightarrow Y_{s}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0))\in\Omega\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}.

The key point here is that its differential can be made uniformly close to the identity and has the right geometric properties. First, write:

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}\Gamma&=\left[\partial_{1}\Gamma,\partial_{2}\Gamma,\partial_{s}\Gamma\right]=\left[\partial_{1}Y,\partial_{2}Y,\partial_{s}Y\right]=\left[\partial_{1}Y,\partial_{2}Y,\bar{B}(Y)\right]=\left[\partial_{1}Y,\partial_{2}Y,\bar{B}(\Gamma)\right]\end{split}

and assume for a moment we showed
\mathrm{D}\Gamma
is invertible everywhere in
U
so that by the Inverse Function Theorem
B.1
,
\Gamma
also is, then for
\Upsilon=\Gamma^{-1}
we have:

\begin{split}\Upsilon^{*}e_{3}=(\mathrm{D}\Upsilon)^{-1}[e_{3}]=\mathrm{D}(\Upsilon^{-1})(\Upsilon)[e_{3}]=\mathrm{D}\Gamma(\Gamma^{-1})[e_{3}]=\left[\partial_{1}Y(\Gamma^{-1}),\ \partial_{2}Y(\Gamma^{-1}),\ \bar{B}\right][e_{3}]=\bar{B}\end{split}

and the straightening property
2
is proven.

In what follows, we show invertibility and higher-order derivative estimates for
\Gamma,\Upsilon
, we define

\mathrm{D}\Gamma-\mathrm{Id}=[r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}]

and bound each remainder
r_{i}
. We begin with
r_{3}
:

\begin{split}\bar{B}(Y_{s}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0))&=\bar{B}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0))+\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{s^{\prime}}(\bar{B}(Y_{s^{\prime}}))\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\bar{B}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0))+\int_{0}^{s}(\bar{B}\cdot\nabla\bar{B})(Y_{s^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=\bar{B}(x_{0})+\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{D}\bar{B}(s^{\prime}(x_{1},x_{2},0)+x_{0})[(x_{1},x_{2},0)^{\top}]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{s}(\bar{B}\cdot\nabla\bar{B})(Y_{s^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=e_{3}+\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{D}\bar{B}(s^{\prime}(x_{1},x_{2},0)+x_{0})[(x_{1},x_{2},0)^{\top}]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{s}(\bar{B}\cdot\nabla\bar{B})(Y_{s^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=e_{3}+r_{3}\end{split}

and it follows that for
(x_{1},x_{2},s)\in U
, we have:

|r_{3}(x_{1},x_{2},s)|\lesssim\tau_{c}\lambda_{q}L(1)\delta_{q}^{1/2}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}

where we used
L(1)=1
. The estimates on
r_{1},r_{2}
follow immediately from those for the Lagrangian flow
Y
in terms of
\bar{B}
, see Proposition
B.5
and we deduce:

(7.4)

||\mathrm{D}\Gamma-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}

where the sup norm is though on
U
. The higher-order derivatives also follow from Proposition
B.5
and the assumption (
7.1
) upon noticing that for any
r
the derivatives in the coordinate
s
correspond to:

\partial_{s}^{r}[Y_{s}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0))]=\bar{B}\cdot\nabla^{r-1}\bar{B}(Y_{s}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0))),

and we conclude:

(7.5)

||\mathrm{D}\Gamma||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}L(r+1)\ \text{ for }\ r\geq 1

where the derivatives are taken with respect to the coordinates
(x_{1},x_{2},s)
on
U
, we won’t mention this point again in the following. The implicit constant depends only on the data (
7.1
) and
r
.

In particular, given any
\alpha,b
we can choose
a
sufficiently large to ensure that:

|\mathrm{D}\Gamma|_{op}\geq 1-|\mathrm{D}\Gamma-\mathrm{Id}|_{op}\geq 1-C\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}>1/2\ \text{ everywhere in }\ U

and an application of the Inverse Function Theorem
B.1
then gives the existence of a smooth inverse
\Upsilon=\Gamma^{-1}:\Omega\to U
and the validity of the formula:

\mathrm{D}(\Gamma^{-1})=(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}(\Gamma^{-1}).

We now establish estimates for this inverse map. Recall that for any matrix
A
with
|A|_{op}<1
we have

|(\mathrm{Id}-A)^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}|_{op}\lesssim|A|_{op}

and thus from (
7.4
) we deduce:

||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}=||(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}(\Gamma^{-1})-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\leq||(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}

where the
\sup
norm is though in
\Omega=\Gamma(U)
.

We now move to higher-order derivatives
r\geq 1
. We first claim that:

(7.6)

||(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}L(r+1)

where the implicit constant depends on
r
and the data (
7.1
). Set
A=\mathrm{D}\Gamma
, for any index
i=1,2,3
and multi index
\sigma
, with
|\sigma|=r-1
we have:

\partial_{\sigma}\partial_{i}A^{-1}=-\partial_{\sigma}(A^{-1}\partial_{i}AA^{-1}),\\

we can then estimate:

\begin{split}||\partial_{\sigma}\partial_{i}A^{-1}||_{0}&=||\partial_{\sigma}(A^{-1}\partial_{i}AA^{-1})||_{0}\leq||A^{-1}\partial_{i}AA^{-1}||_{r-1}\\
&\lesssim||A^{-1}||_{r-1}||\partial_{i}A||_{0}||A^{-1}||_{0}+||A^{-1}||_{0}^{2}||\partial_{i}A||_{r-1}\\
\end{split}

and the claim follows by induction together with
L(2)=1
and the fact that
L(r)
is non-decreasing.

This, together with composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
, gives us:

\begin{split}[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon]_{r}&=[(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}(\Upsilon)]_{r}\lesssim[(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}]_{1}||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon||_{r-1}+||(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}||_{r}||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon||^{r}_{0}\\
\end{split}

and by induction together with the estimates on
\mathrm{D}\Gamma
in (
7.5
) we conclude:

\begin{split}||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},\ \ \ \ ||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}L(r+1)\end{split}

where the implicit constants depend only on
r
and the data in (
7.1
), and
the
\sup
norm is thought on
\Omega
.

Conclusion.
We now restrict the space domain of
\Phi
to
B_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})
so that the composition
\Psi=\Upsilon\circ\Phi
is well defined for
|t-t_{0}|<\tau^{c}
and proceed with proving derivative bounds. We first rewrite:

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}\Psi-\mathrm{Id}&=\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)\mathrm{D}\Phi-\mathrm{Id}\\
&=\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)[\mathrm{D}\Phi-\mathrm{Id}]+\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}\\
&=[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}][\mathrm{D}\Phi-\mathrm{Id}]+[\mathrm{D}\Phi-\mathrm{Id}]+[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}]\\
\\
(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}&=(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}(\mathrm{D}\Upsilon)^{-1}(\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}\\
&=\mathrm{D}X(\Phi)\mathrm{D}\Gamma(\Upsilon\circ\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}\\
&=[\mathrm{D}X(\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}][\mathrm{D}\Gamma(\Upsilon\circ\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}]+[\mathrm{D}X(\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}]+[\mathrm{D}\Gamma(\Upsilon\circ\Phi)-\mathrm{Id}]\\
\\
\end{split}

here we used that
\Phi^{-1}=X
, see (
A.15
), and conclude from the estimates on
\Phi,X
coming from Propositions
B.4
,
B.5
, and the definition of
\tau^{c}
in (
1.21
), that:

\begin{split}||\mathrm{D}\Psi-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}&\leq||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\Phi-\mathrm{Id}]||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\Phi-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon-\mathrm{Id}||_{0},\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\\
||(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}&\leq||\mathrm{D}X-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\Gamma-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}X-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\Gamma-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\\
\end{split}

and from

|\det[\mathrm{Id}+A]-1|\lesssim|A|_{op}

we also deduce:

||\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi]-1||_{0},\ ||\det[(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}]-1||_{0}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}.

For
r\geq 1
by means of the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
we conclude:

\begin{split}||\mathrm{D}\Psi||_{r}&\lesssim||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)||_{r}||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\left(||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon||_{1}||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{r-1}+||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon||_{r}||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{0}^{r}\right)||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\Upsilon||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}L(r+1)\end{split}

where we used the fact that
L(2)=1
and the implicit constant depends on the data and
r
. The bounds for
(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}
follow similarly.

Lie-Transport properties.
It follows from (
A.14
) that

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v}^{1})\Psi^{1*}e=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v}^{1})\Phi^{1*}(\Upsilon^{1*}e)=0,

similarly,

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\Psi^{2*}e=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{v})\Phi^{*}(\Upsilon^{2*}e)=0,

the same computations hold for the scalar case and we deduce
(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)\Psi^{*}\varphi_{k}=0
, here the direction
k
plays no role.

From the properties in (
A.16
) we deduce:

\mathcal{L}_{B}^{1}\Psi^{1*}e=1/c\mathcal{L}_{\Psi^{*}e_{3}}\Psi^{1*}e=1/c\Psi^{1*}(\mathcal{L}_{e_{3}}e)=0,

similarly, from (
A.10
) and (
A.8
) we obtain:

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{B}\Psi^{2*}e&=[B,\Psi^{2*}e]=\mathrm{curl}[B\times\Psi^{2*}e]\\
&=1/c\mathrm{curl}[\Psi^{*}e_{3}\times\Psi^{2*}e]\\
&=1/c\mathrm{curl}[\Psi^{1*}(e_{3}\times e)]\\
&=\Psi^{2*}\mathrm{curl}[e_{3}\times e]=0\\
\end{split}

as both
e_{3}
and
e
are constant. For the scalar case, we use that
k\cdot e_{3}=0
and the property in (
A.9
) to compute:

\begin{split}B\cdot\nabla\Psi^{*}\varphi_{k}=B\cdot\mathrm{d}\Psi^{*}\varphi_{k}=B\cdot\Psi^{1*}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{k}=1/c\Psi^{*}e_{3}\cdot\Psi^{1*}\nabla\varphi_{k}=1/c\Psi^{*}(e_{3}\cdot\nabla\varphi_{k})=\varphi^{\prime}(k\cdot\Psi)e_{3}\cdot k=0\end{split}

as
d=\nabla
in our setting, see (
A.5
). Combining the above with
\mathrm{div}\mathrm{curl}=0
and (
A.12
) to commute the Lie operators with
\mathrm{curl}
, we deduce the properties for
\xi
.

Pure Time derivatives.
From the Lie transport properties for constant 1-forms above, we deduce:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{\top}=&-v\cdot\nabla\mathrm{D}\Psi^{\top}-(\mathrm{D}v)^{\top}\mathrm{D}\Psi^{\top},\\
\partial_{t}^{2}\mathrm{D}\Psi^{\top}=&-(\partial_{t}v)\cdot\nabla\mathrm{D}\Psi^{\top}-v\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}^{\top}\\
&-(\mathrm{D}\partial_{t}v)^{\top}\mathrm{D}\Psi^{\top}-(\mathrm{D}v)^{\top}\partial_{t}\mathrm{D}\Psi_{I}^{\top}.\\
\end{split}

Similar identities hold for
\mathrm{D}\Psi^{-1}
where
-\mathrm{D}v^{\top}
is substituted by
-\mathrm{D}v
. From the assumed pure-time derivative bound on
v
in (
7.1
), we conclude:

||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathrm{D}\Psi||_{r},\ ||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathrm{D}\Psi^{-1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+j}L(r+j+1)\ \text{ for }j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ r\geq 0.

∎

Remark 27

(An Interesting Cancellation)
.

We claim that:

B\cdot\nabla(\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi])=0

from which it quickly follows that:

(7.7)

\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi]=0.

Indeed, we have:

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)(\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi])&=(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Phi)\mathrm{D}\Phi])\\
&=(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon](\Phi)\det[\mathrm{D}\Phi])\\
&=(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon](\Phi))\\
&=\mathrm{D}(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon])(\Phi)[(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)\Phi]=0\end{split}

where we used that
\mathrm{div}\ v=0\leadsto\det[\mathrm{D}\Phi]=1
.

To prove the claim, we first compute:

\begin{split}cB\cdot\nabla\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi]&=cB\cdot\nabla(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon](\Phi))\\
&=\Phi^{*}\bar{B}\cdot\nabla(\Phi^{*}(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon]))\\
&=\Phi^{*}\bar{B}\cdot\Phi^{1*}\nabla(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon])\\
&=(\bar{B}\cdot\nabla\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon])(\Phi)\end{split}

where we used that
cB=X_{*}\bar{B}=\Phi^{*}\bar{B}
and the identity in (
A.9
). In particular, to prove the claim, it suffices to show:

\bar{B}\cdot\nabla\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon]=0.

Now set
f=\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon]
, since the
\bar{B}
derivative on the manifold corresponds to the
\partial_{3}=\partial_{s}
derivative in the chart, we have:

(7.8)

\begin{split}\bar{B}\cdot\nabla f&=(\partial_{s}(f\circ\Gamma))(\Upsilon)\\
&=[\partial_{s}(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon(\Gamma)]](\Upsilon)\\
&=[\partial_{s}(\det[(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}])](\Upsilon)\\
&=\left[\partial_{s}\frac{1}{\det[\mathrm{D}\Gamma]}\right](\Upsilon)\\
&=\left[-\frac{1}{2\det[\mathrm{D}\Gamma]}\text{trace}\left[(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}\partial_{s}\mathrm{D}\Gamma\right]\right](\Upsilon)\\
\end{split}

and with this at hand, we need only compute:

\partial_{s}\mathrm{D}\Gamma=\mathrm{D}\partial_{s}\Gamma=\mathrm{D}\left[\bar{B}(\Gamma)\right]=\mathrm{D}\bar{B}(\Gamma)\mathrm{D}\Gamma

we can plug this back in (
7.8
) and by means of the identity
\text{trace}[AB]=\text{trace}[BA]
for any two matrices
A,B
, deduce:

\begin{split}\bar{B}\cdot\nabla(\det[\mathrm{D}\Upsilon])&=\left[-\frac{1}{2\det[\mathrm{D}\Gamma]}\text{trace}\left[(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}\partial_{s}\mathrm{D}\Gamma\right]\right](\Upsilon)\\
&=\left[-\frac{1}{2\det[\mathrm{D}\Gamma]}\text{trace}\left[(\mathrm{D}\Gamma)^{-1}\mathrm{D}\bar{B}(\Gamma)\mathrm{D}\Gamma\right]\right](\Upsilon)\\
&=-\frac{\mathrm{div}\bar{B}}{2\det[\mathrm{D}\Gamma](\Upsilon)}\\
&=0\end{split}

and the claim follows.

7.1.
Stability Estimates

We now perturb our original vector fields:

(v,B)\leadsto(\tilde{v},\tilde{B})=(v+w,B+b),

we still assume that
(\tilde{v},\tilde{B})
are divergence-free, solve (
1.1
) and satisfy (
7.1
). In particular, we can construct a new chart
\tilde{\Psi}:Q_{\tau^{c}}(x_{0})\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
associated with this new pair by means of Lemma
7.1
. We want to estimate the difference between the two constructions. We assume that:

(7.9)

||w||_{r},||b||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r)\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}

and we also require:

L\leq\tilde{L},\ \tilde{L}(0)=\tilde{L}(1)=\tilde{L}(2)=1,\ \tilde{L}(r)\tilde{L}(r^{\prime})\leq\tilde{L}(r+r^{\prime})\ \text{ for }\ r,r^{\prime}\geq 0.

We will show the following stability result for the chart construction.

Lemma 7.2

(Chart Stability)
.

Let
r\geq 0
be an integer. Under the above assumptions, we have:

\begin{split}&||\tilde{\Psi}-\Psi||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+1)\frac{1}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{g},\\
&||(\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi})^{-1}-(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+2)\mathcal{T}_{g}\\
\end{split}

where the implicit constants depend on
r
and the data (
7.1
), (
7.9
).

Remark 28
.

The estimate (
7.9
) on the perturbation is chosen to match that of
(w^{g},b^{g})
from the Galbrun stage, but in principle, the result can be adapted to any given one.

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

We split the proof in 2 steps. Since
\Psi=\Upsilon\circ\Phi
and
\Upsilon
is constructed as the inverse of the auxiliary map
\Gamma
, in the first part, we prove stability estimates for
\Gamma
and
\Phi
. In the second part, we deal with the stability of
\Upsilon
and the composition.

First step.
Let
\bar{B},\ \bar{\tilde{B}},\ Y,\ \tilde{Y},\ \Gamma,\ \tilde{\Gamma},\ \Upsilon,\ \tilde{\Upsilon}
be the intermediate objects in the construction behind the proof of Lemma
7.1
. We begin with the following observations. By direct computation, we see that the difference
\tilde{\Phi}-\Phi
solves:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}(\tilde{\Phi}-\Phi)+v\cdot\nabla(\tilde{\Phi}-\Phi)=-w\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Phi},\\
(\tilde{\Phi}-\Phi)|_{t=t_{0}}=0\end{cases}

given any
b,\ \alpha
, we can always choose
a
sufficiently large to have
||v||_{1}2\tau^{c}\leq 1
, then apply Propositions
B.4
to deduce for
|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau^{c}
:

(7.10)

\begin{split}||\tilde{\Phi}_{t}-\Phi_{t}||_{r}&\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t}||(w\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Phi})(\cdot,s^{\prime})||_{r}+(t-s^{\prime})[v]_{r}||(w\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Phi})(\cdot,l)||_{1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+1)\frac{1}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{g}.\end{split}

Similarly, the difference:

F(x_{1},x_{2},s)=(\tilde{\Gamma}-\Gamma)(x_{1},x_{2},s)=(\tilde{Y}_{s}-Y_{s})(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0)),

solves the problem:

(7.11)

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}F+AF=G,\\
F|_{s=0}=0\end{cases}

where

\begin{split}A(x_{1},x_{2},s)&=-\int_{0}^{1}(\mathrm{D}\bar{\tilde{B}})(s^{\prime}\tilde{Y}_{s}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0))+(1-s^{\prime})Y_{s}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0)))\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\\
&=-\int_{0}^{1}(\mathrm{D}\bar{\tilde{B}})(s^{\prime}\tilde{\Gamma}(x_{1},x_{2},s)+(1-s^{\prime})\Gamma(x_{1},x_{2},s))\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},\\
G(x_{1},x_{2},s)&=(\bar{\tilde{B}}-\bar{B})(Y_{s}(x_{0}+(x_{1},x_{2},0)))\\
&=(\bar{\tilde{B}}-\bar{B})(\Gamma(x_{1},x_{2},s))\end{split}

and the solution can be explicitly written as

(7.12)

F(\cdot,s)=e^{-\int_{0}^{s}A(\cdot,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{s}e^{\int_{0}^{s^{\prime}}A(s^{\prime\prime},\cdot)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}}G(\cdot,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

We now provide bounds on
A
and
G
, which also guarantee that the representation (
7.12
) is well-defined. We first rewrite:

\begin{split}G&=(\bar{\tilde{B}}-\bar{B})(\Gamma)=\frac{(B+b)(\Gamma)}{|B+b|(x_{0},t_{0})}-\frac{B(\Gamma)}{|B|(x_{0},t_{0})}\\
&=\frac{1}{|B+b|(x_{0},t_{0})}b(\Gamma)+\left(\frac{|B|-|B+b|}{|B||B+b|}\right)(x_{0},t_{0})B(\Gamma)\end{split}

note that given (
7.1
) and (
7.9
) we have:

\frac{1}{|B+b|(x_{0},t_{0})}\lesssim\frac{1}{c_{0}-|b|}\lesssim 1\ \text{ and }\ \left(\frac{\left||B|-|B+b|\right|}{|B||B+b|}\right)(x_{0},t_{0})\lesssim\frac{|b|}{c_{0}(c_{0}-|b|)}\lesssim\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}

where the implicit constants depend on the data, and to bound the numerators, we choose
a
sufficiently large so that
c_{0}/2>\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}
. By means of the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
, the bounds for
\Gamma
in (
7.5
), and the fact that
|s|\lesssim\tau^{c}
we deduce:

||G||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r)\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}\ \text{ and }\ ||A||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+1)\lambda_{q}\delta_{q}^{1/2},\\

in particular, given
b
we can always choose
a
sufficiently large so that
||\int_{0}^{s}A\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}||_{0}<1
. From the representation formula (
7.12
), and the bounds for
A,\ G
we conclude:

(7.13)

||\tilde{\Gamma}-\Gamma||_{r}=||F||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+1)\lambda_{q}\ell^{-\alpha}\tau^{c}\tau^{a}\delta_{q+1}=\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+1)\frac{1}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{g}.

Second step.
Fix two diffeomorphisms
\varphi,\ \psi
with same domain and image. We use the following standard trick

\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1}=\varphi^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\psi^{-1}-\varphi^{-1}\circ\varphi\circ\psi^{-1}

from this deduce

\begin{split}||\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1}||_{0}&\leq[\varphi^{-1}]_{1}||\psi\circ\psi^{-1}-\varphi\circ\psi^{-1}||_{0}\\
&\leq[\varphi^{-1}]_{1}||\psi-\varphi||_{0}\\
\end{split}

and applying this with
\varphi=\tilde{\Gamma},\psi=\Gamma
from the construction of
\tilde{\Psi},\Psi
with the related estimates given by Lemma
7.1
, and (
7.13
) above we conclude:

(7.14)

||\tilde{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon||_{0}\lesssim||\tilde{\Gamma}-\Gamma||_{0}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{g}

where the implicit constants depend on the data (
7.1
) and (
7.9
). We now deal with higher-order derivatives. We need some preliminary bounds. We will repeatedly use the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
. We first compute:

\begin{split}(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\varphi^{-1})-(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\psi^{-1})=\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{D}[(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}](s\varphi^{-1}+(1-s)\psi^{-1})[\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1}]\mathrm{d}s,\end{split}

from which we deduce:

(7.15)

\begin{split}&||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\varphi^{-1})-(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\psi^{-1})||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}||\mathrm{D}[(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}](s\varphi^{-1}+(1-s)\psi^{-1})||_{0}||\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1}||_{r}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{0}^{1}||\mathrm{D}[(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}](s\varphi^{-1}+(1-s)\psi^{-1})||_{r}||\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1}||_{0}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\overset{r=0}{\lesssim}||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}||_{1}||\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1}||_{0}\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}||_{1}||\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1}||_{r}\\
&+\left[||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}||_{2}(||\varphi^{-1}||_{r}+||\psi^{-1}||_{r})+||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}||_{r+1}(||\varphi^{-1}||_{1}+||\psi^{-1}||_{1})^{r}\right]||\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1}||_{0}.\end{split}

Secondly, we compute:

(7.16)

\begin{split}||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\mathrm{D}\varphi-\mathrm{D}\psi)(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{r}&\lesssim\left(||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}||_{r}||(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{0}+||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}||_{0}||(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{r}\right)[\varphi-\psi]_{1}\\
&+||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}||_{0}||(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{0}||\varphi-\psi||_{r+1}.\end{split}

We now go back to the bound we are interested in. We will use that for any two matrices
A,B
one has
A^{-1}-B^{-1}=-A^{-1}(A-B)B^{-1}
to compute:

(7.17)

\begin{split}[\mathrm{D}(\varphi^{-1}-\psi^{-1})]_{r}&=[(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\varphi^{-1})-(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}(\psi^{-1})]_{r}\\
&=[(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\varphi^{-1})-(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\psi^{-1})]_{r}+[((\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}-(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1})(\psi^{-1})]_{r}\\
&\overset{r=0}{\leq}||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\varphi^{-1})-(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\psi^{-1})||_{0}+||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\varphi-\mathrm{D}\psi||_{0}||(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{0}\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}[(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\varphi^{-1})-(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\psi^{-1})]_{r}\\
&+||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}-(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{1}||\psi^{-1}||_{r}+||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}-(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{r}[\psi^{-1}]_{1}^{r}\\
&\lesssim[(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\varphi^{-1})-(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\psi^{-1})]_{r}\\
&+||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\mathrm{D}\varphi-\mathrm{D}\psi)(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{1}||\psi^{-1}||_{r}+||(\mathrm{D}\varphi)^{-1}(\mathrm{D}\varphi-\mathrm{D}\psi)(\mathrm{D}\psi)^{-1}||_{r}[\psi^{-1}]_{1}^{r}.\end{split}

We combine (
7.15
), (
7.16
), (
7.17
) for
r=0
, with
\varphi=\tilde{\Gamma},\psi=\Gamma
and the bounds in (
7.6
), (
7.14
) to deduce:

||\tilde{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon||_{1}\lesssim\tilde{L}(2)\mathcal{T}_{g}=\mathcal{T}_{g}

and for
r\geq 1
, we proceed by induction and obtain:

(7.18)

||\tilde{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon||_{r}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+1)\frac{1}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{g}

where the implicit constants depend on
r
and the data in (
7.1
), (
7.9
).

Conclusion.
We write

\begin{split}\tilde{\Psi}-\Psi&=\tilde{\Upsilon}\circ\tilde{\Phi}-\tilde{\Upsilon}\circ\Phi+\tilde{\Upsilon}\circ\Phi-\Upsilon\circ\Phi\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Upsilon}(s\tilde{\Phi}+(1-s)\Phi)[\tilde{\Phi}-\Phi]\mathrm{d}s+(\tilde{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon)(\Phi).\\
\end{split}

and use the composition estimates in Proposition
B.3
together with (
7.10
), (
7.18
) to bound:

\begin{split}||\tilde{\Psi}-\Psi||_{r}&\lesssim||\tilde{\Phi}-\Phi||_{0}\int_{0}^{1}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Upsilon}(s\tilde{\Phi}+(1-s)\Phi)||_{r}\mathrm{d}s+||\tilde{\Phi}-\Phi||_{r}\int_{0}^{1}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Upsilon}(s\tilde{\Phi}+(1-s)\Phi)||_{0}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+||(\tilde{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon)(\Phi)||_{r}\\
&\overset{r=0}{\lesssim}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\\
&\overset{r\geq 1}{\lesssim}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\left[||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Upsilon}||_{1}(||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Phi}||_{r-1}+||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{r-1})+||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Upsilon}||_{r}(||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Phi}||_{0}+||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{0})^{r}\right]\\
&+||\tilde{\Phi}-\Phi||_{r}+||\tilde{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon||_{1}||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{r-1}+||\tilde{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon||_{r}||\mathrm{D}\Phi||_{0}^{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+1)\frac{1}{\lambda_{q}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\end{split}

and conclude:

\begin{split}||(\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi})^{-1}-(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}||_{r}&=||(\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi})^{-1}(\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}-\mathrm{D}\Psi)(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim||(\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi})^{-1}||_{r}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}-\mathrm{D}\Psi||_{0}||(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}||_{0}+||(\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi})^{-1}||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}-\mathrm{D}\Psi||_{r}||(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}||_{0}\\
&+||(\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi})^{-1}||_{0}||\mathrm{D}\tilde{\Psi}-\mathrm{D}\Psi||_{0}||(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}\tilde{L}(r+2)\mathcal{T}_{g}.\\
\end{split}

The implicit constants in the above bounds depend on
r
and the data in (
7.1
) and (
7.9
).
∎

8.
Inductive Lemma

In this section, given a time-dependent map
F_{0}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},t_{0}+\tau^{c})\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
, which we think of as a time-dependent 1-form, we wish to study the structure and give estimates for:

F_{k}=\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\sigma_{k}}\dots\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{1}}^{1}F_{0},

for an arbitrary
k
, where
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i}
is a family of divergence-free vector fields defined as:

(8.1)

\sigma_{i}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}\left[\varsigma_{i}\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{i}\nu)\right]=\varsigma_{i}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{i}^{\prime}\zeta)+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{i}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{i}\nu)=\sigma_{i}^{o}+\sigma_{i}^{c}

with

\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{i}(\lambda_{q+1}x\cdot k)

for
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
a smooth scalar function,
(k,\nu,\zeta)
an oriented orthonormal basis e.g. the ones from Lemma
2.5
,
\Psi(\cdot,t):\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
is a diffeomorphism onto its image for every
t\in B_{\tau^{c}}(t_{0})
, e.g. the charts constructed in Lemma
7.1
and
\varsigma_{i}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},t_{0}+\tau^{c})\to\mathbb{R}
are smooth functions e.g. the slow coefficients in (
3.21
). We also define the following types of geometries for a 1-form
F_{0}
:

(8.2)

F_{0}=\underbrace{a_{1}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1}\nu)}_{\text{good}}+\underbrace{a_{2}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2}k)}_{\text{good}_{c}}+\underbrace{\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3})Y}_{\text{bad}}

where as above
\phi_{i}=\phi_{i}(\lambda_{q+1}x\cdot k)
and
Y:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{0}-\tau^{c},t_{0}+\tau^{c})\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
is another smooth time dependent 1-form. For reasons that will be clear in a moment, we refer to the first two terms as having ‘good geometry’ and the third one as having ‘bad geometry’, in the sense that we are not controlling it. We adopt the symbolic notations
\text{good},\ \text{good}_{c},\ \text{bad}
to indicate this. In the applications, we will always have
a_{2}=0
, which is why we formulate Lemma
8.1
this way. Note that this is not restrictive, as one could always collect the
\text{good}_{c}
term in the one with bad geometry. This term, however, plays a crucial role in the induction argument we are about to make.

Definition 8.1

(Admissible Loss Functions)
.

We say that a non-decreasing functions
L:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
is an
admissible loss function
if

L(0)=L(1)=L(2)=1\ \text{ and }\ \left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{r^{\prime}}L(r+r^{\prime})\leq L(r)\ \text{ for }\ r,r^{\prime}\geq 0.

We now present two examples of admissible loss functions that will appear in this work.

Example 1.
The basic example is the following:

L(r)=\lambda_{q}^{[r-\underline{r}]^{+}(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}

for some
\underline{r}\geq 2
.

Example 2.
The following will appear as the loss associated with the Alfvén transport bound after the Galbrun Stage:

(8.3)

L_{p,\mathcal{A}}(r)=1_{r\leq\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-2}+1_{\underline{r}-k_{0}^{g}-1\leq r\leq\underline{r}-1}\left(\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\right)^{\underline{r}-1-r}\bar{L}+1_{r\geq\underline{r}}\lambda_{q}^{[r-(\underline{r}-1)](b-1)\gamma_{\ell}}\bar{L},

one should think of it as showing the existence of three ranges of derivatives:

•

In the first one, we have the desired estimate.

•

In the second one, we partially lose the good estimate, but we still have some smallness because of the Lie-Taylor expansion used in the Galbrun Stage.

•

In the third, we have a full loss, and we even start to see the mollification parameter appear.

We refer to (
3.42
) and the Claim afterwards for a proof that this is an admissible loss function.

We now specify the exact assumptions on the initial data
F_{0}
and the sequence
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i}
.

Standing Assumptions 2
.

Fix
\bar{N}\geq 2
integer, let
A_{i},\ A_{i,\mathcal{A}}
with
i=1,3
and
\bar{\varsigma}_{i},\ \bar{\varsigma}_{i,\mathcal{A}}
with
i=1,\dots,k
be positive real numbers and
L,\ L^{1},\ L^{\varsigma},\ L_{\mathcal{A}},\ L_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
be non-decreasing functions with:

(8.4a)

\displaystyle L^{\varsigma}(r^{\prime})L^{1}(r)\leq L^{1}(r+r^{\prime}),\ L^{\varsigma}(r^{\prime})L(r)\leq L(r+r^{\prime})

(8.4b)

\displaystyle L^{\varsigma}(r^{\prime})L^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(r)\leq L^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(r+r^{\prime}),\ L^{\varsigma}(r^{\prime})L_{\mathcal{A}}(r)\leq L_{\mathcal{A}}(r+r^{\prime})

(8.4c)

\displaystyle L(j)=L^{1}(j)=L_{\mathcal{A}}(j)=L^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(j)=1\ \text{ for }j=0,1,2

and assume in addition that
L^{\varsigma}
is admissible as in Definition
8.3
. Let
F_{0}
be as in (
8.2
) with
a_{2}=0
and the family
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i}
be as in (
8.1
). We require the following estimates:

•

Fast Coefficients:

(8.5)

||\varphi_{i}||_{r},||\phi_{i}||_{r}\leq 1

for
r\geq 0
and without
\lambda_{q+1}
rescaling.

•

Slow Coefficients:

(8.6a)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{j}a_{1}||_{r}\leq\bar{C}\lambda_{q}^{r+j}L^{1}(r+j)A_{1}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-j,

(8.6b)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{j}Y||_{r}\leq\bar{C}\lambda_{q}^{r+j}L(r+j)A_{3}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-j,

(8.6c)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{j}\varsigma_{i}||_{r}\leq\bar{C}\lambda_{q}^{r+j}L^{\varsigma}(r+j)\ \bar{\varsigma}_{i}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-j,

(8.6d)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}a_{1}||_{r}\leq\bar{C}\lambda_{q}^{r}L_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(r)A_{1,\mathcal{A}}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-1,

(8.6e)

\displaystyle||(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}})Y||_{r}\leq\bar{C}\lambda_{q}^{r}L_{\mathcal{A}}(r)A_{3,\mathcal{A}}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-1,

(8.6f)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{A}^{\pm}\varsigma_{i}||_{r}\leq\bar{C}\lambda_{q}^{r}L^{\varsigma}(r)\ \bar{\varsigma}_{i,\mathcal{A}}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-1.

for some constant
\bar{C}
.

•

Charts:

(8.7a)

\displaystyle||\mathrm{D}\Psi-\mathrm{Id}||_{0},||(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}||_{0}\leq 1/2,

(8.7b)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{j}\mathrm{D}\Psi||_{r},||\partial_{t}^{j}(\mathrm{D}\Psi)^{-1}||_{r}\leq\bar{C}\lambda_{q}^{r+j}L^{\varsigma}(r+j)\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-j.

for
\bar{C}
as above.

•

Lie-Transport Properties:

(8.8)

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}})\Psi^{*}(\phi_{i})=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}})\Psi^{*}(\varphi_{i})=(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}})\Psi^{1*}\nu=0.

for any constant vector
\nu
and
\varphi_{i},\ \phi_{i}
as above.

Notation:
In the following, we write
F\simeq G
, for
F
and
G
1-forms to mean that
F
can be written as a finite sum of terms of the same geometry as
G
(in the sense of (
8.2
)) and obeying the same estimates.

With all the preparations done, we can state the following key result.

Lemma 8.1

(Inductive Lemma: 1-Forms)
.

Under the Standing Assumptions
2
and the notation above, we have:

F_{k}\simeq a_{1,k}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)+a_{2,k}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)+\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k})Y_{k}

where the number of terms hidden in
\simeq
grows at most exponentially in
k
.

Moreover, there exists constants
C,\ C^{\prime},\ C^{\prime\prime}
, depending only on
\bar{C},\ \bar{N}
, such that for
j=0,1,2\text{ and }0\leq r+k+j\leq\bar{N}
, we have:

•

Fast Coefficients:

||\phi_{i,k}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\ \text{ for }\ i=1,2,3.

•

Slow Coefficients:

(8.9a)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{j}a_{1,k}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+k+j}L^{1}(r+k+j)A_{1}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i},

(8.9b)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{j}a_{2,k}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\lambda_{q}}\lambda_{q}^{r+k+j}L(r+k+j-1)A_{3}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i},

(8.9c)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{j}Y_{k}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+k+j}L(r+k+j)A_{3}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}.

In addition, for possibly different
\phi_{i,k}
, we have:

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}})F_{k}\simeq a_{1,k,\mathcal{A}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)+a_{2,k,\mathcal{A}}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k})Y_{k,\mathcal{A}}

where the number of terms hidden in
\simeq
grows at most exponentially in
k
.

Moreover, there exists possibly different constants
C,\ C^{\prime},\ C^{\prime\prime}
such that for
0\leq r+k\leq\bar{N}-1
, we have:

•

Fast Coefficients:

(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}})\Psi^{*}(\phi_{i,k})=0\ \text{ and }\ ||\phi_{i,k}||_{r}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\ \text{ for }\ i=1,2,3.

•

Slow Coefficients:

(8.10a)

\displaystyle||a_{1,k,\mathcal{A}}||_{r}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+k}\Bigl[L_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(k+r)A_{1,\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}+L^{1}(k+r)A_{1}\max_{j\in\{1,\dots,k\}}\Bigl[\bar{\varsigma}_{j,\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1,\dots,k,\;i\neq j}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\Bigr]\Bigr],

(8.10b)

\displaystyle||a_{2,k,\mathcal{A}}||_{r}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\lambda_{q}}\lambda_{q}^{r+k}\begin{aligned} \Bigl[&L_{\mathcal{A}}(r+k-1)A_{3,\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\\
&\quad+L(r+k-1)A_{3}\max_{j\in\{1,\dots,k\}}\Bigl[\bar{\varsigma}_{j,\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1,\dots,k,\;i\neq j}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\Bigr]\Bigr],\end{aligned}

(8.10c)

\displaystyle||Y_{k,\mathcal{A}}||_{r}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(C)^{k}\lambda_{q}^{r+k}\Bigl[L_{\mathcal{A}}(k+r)A_{3,\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}+L(k+r)A_{3}\max_{j\in\{1,\dots,k\}}\Bigl[\bar{\varsigma}_{j,\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1,\dots,k,\;i\neq j}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\Bigr]\Bigr].

Proof of Lemma
8.1
.

We prove the Lemma by induction on
k
, the case
k=0
being contained in our Standing Assumptions
2
, with the understanding that
a_{2,0}=a_{2,0,\mathcal{A}}=0
so the bounds trivially hold. Note that for
k=0
we can just pick
C^{\prime}=\bar{C},\ C=C^{\prime\prime}=1
.

To simplify the notation, we set:

\begin{split}&A_{1,r,k,j}=\lambda_{q}^{r+k+j}L(r+k+j)A_{1}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i},\\
&A_{2,r,k,j}=\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{r+k+j-1}L(r+k+j-1)A_{3}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i},\\
&A_{3,r,k,j}=\lambda_{q}^{r+k+j}L(r+k+j)A_{3}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\\
\end{split}

and we will omit the entry
j
if
j=0
. We now assume the claimed estimates hold for
k
, where the values of the constants
C,\ C^{\prime}
will be defined along the induction step. We also assume the decomposition:

F_{k}=a_{1,k}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)+a_{2,k}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)+\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k})Y_{k}

where for definiteness, we wrote
=
instead of
\simeq
, it will be clear from the following computations that the number of terms grows at most exponentially in
k
as claimed and we then split:

F_{k+1}=\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{k+1}}F_{k}=\sigma_{k+1}\cdot\nabla F_{k}+\mathrm{D}\sigma_{k+1}^{\top}[F_{k}]=\underbrace{\mathrm{curl}[F_{k}]\times\sigma_{k+1}}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{\nabla(F_{k}\cdot\sigma_{k+1})}_{T_{2}}

Rewriting of
T_{1}
.
We begin with a sequence of preliminary computations:

\begin{split}\mathrm{curl}[F_{k}]&=\mathrm{curl}\left[a_{1,k}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)+a_{2,k}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)+\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k})Y_{k}\right]\\
&=\lambda_{q+1}a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}(\phi_{1,k}^{\prime}\zeta)+\nabla a_{1,k}\times\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)+\nabla a_{2,k}\times\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\\
&+\lambda_{q+1}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)\times Y^{k}+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k})\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\\
\end{split}

where we used

\mathrm{curl}[\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)]=\Psi^{2*}\mathrm{curl}[\phi_{2,k}k]=\lambda_{q+1}\Psi^{2*}(\phi^{\prime}_{2,k}k\times k)=0

see (
A.10
). Recall that:

\sigma_{k+1}=\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)=\sigma_{k+1}^{o}+\sigma_{k+1}^{c},

we now compute the cross product with the principal part of
\sigma_{k+1}
, namely

(8.11)

\begin{split}\mathrm{curl}[F_{k}]\times\sigma_{k+1}^{o}&=\left[\lambda_{q+1}a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}(\phi_{1,k}^{\prime}\zeta)+\nabla a_{1,k}\times\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)+\nabla a_{2,k}\times\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\right]\times\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)\\
&+\left[\lambda_{q+1}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)\times Y^{k}+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k})\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\right]\times\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)\\
&=[\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)\cdot\nabla a_{1,k}]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)+[\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)\cdot\nabla a_{2,k}]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\\
&-\lambda_{q+1}[Y_{k}\cdot(\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta))]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k})\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)\end{split}

where we used

(8.12)

(a\times b)\times c=b(a\cdot c)-a(b\cdot c)

and that according to (
A.9
) we have:

(8.13)

\begin{split}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)\cdot\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)&=\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)\cdot\Psi^{*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)=\det[\mathrm{D}\Psi]\Psi^{*}(\phi_{1,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\nu\cdot\zeta)=0,\\
\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}^{\prime}k)\cdot\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)&=\dots=0.\end{split}

We now compute the cross product with the corrector part of
\sigma_{k+1}
, namely

(8.14)

\begin{split}\mathrm{curl}[F_{k}]\times\sigma^{c}_{k+1}&=\left[\lambda_{q+1}a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}(\phi_{1,k}^{\prime}\zeta)+\nabla a_{1,k}\times\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)\right]\times\left[\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right]\\
&+\nabla a_{2,k}\times\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\times\left[\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right]\\
&+\left[\lambda_{q+1}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)\times Y^{k}+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k})\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\right]\times\left[\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right]\\
&=-(a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1})\Psi^{*}(\phi_{1,k}^{\prime}\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\Psi^{1*}\nu\cdot\left(\nabla a_{1,k}\times\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right)\right]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\\
&+(\sigma_{k+1}^{c}\cdot\nabla a_{2,k})\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)-(\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\cdot\sigma_{k+1}^{c})\nabla a_{2,k}\\
&+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k})\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\sigma_{k+1}^{c}+\left[\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)\times Y^{k}\right]\times\left[\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right]\end{split}

where we used the cancellations
8.13
above and the cross-product rule

(a\times b)\times(c\times b)=b\cdot(a\times c)b

in addition to (
8.12
).

Summing (
8.11
) and (
8.14
) we rewrite
T_{1}
as:

\begin{split}T_{1}&=\mathrm{curl}\ F_{k}\times(\sigma_{k+1}^{o}+\sigma_{k+1}^{c})\\
&=\underbrace{(\sigma_{k+1}^{o}\cdot\nabla a_{1,k})\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)}_{\text{good}}-\underbrace{(a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1})\Psi^{*}(\phi_{1,k}^{\prime}\varphi_{k+1}\nu)}_{\text{good}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\Psi^{1*}\nu\cdot\left(\nabla a_{1,k}\times\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right)\right]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\varphi_{k+1}\nu)}_{\text{good}}\\
&+\underbrace{(\sigma_{k+1}\cdot\nabla a_{2,k})\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)}_{\text{good}_{c}}-\underbrace{\lambda_{q+1}(Y_{k}\cdot\sigma_{k+1}^{o})\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)}_{\text{good}_{c}}-\underbrace{(\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\cdot\sigma_{k+1}^{c})\nabla a_{2,k}}_{\text{bad}}\\
&+\underbrace{\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k})\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\sigma_{k+1}}_{\text{bad}}+\underbrace{\left[\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)\times Y_{k}\right]\times\left[\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right]}_{\text{bad}}.\end{split}

Rewriting of
T_{2}
.
Similarly, we compute:

\begin{split}T_{2}&=\nabla\left[F_{k}\cdot\left(\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right)\right]\\
&=\nabla\underbrace{\left[a_{1,k}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)\cdot\left(\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right)\right]}_{=0}\\
&+\nabla\left[a_{2,k}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\cdot\left(\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right)\right]\\
&+\nabla\left[\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k})Y_{k}\cdot\left(\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\zeta)+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right)\right]\\
&=-\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\nabla\left[\Psi^{*}(\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})\left(a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right)\right]\\
&+\nabla\left[\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\right]\\
&=-\underbrace{\left[a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]\Psi^{1*}((\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})^{\prime}k)}_{\text{good}_{c}}-\underbrace{\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})\nabla\left[a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]}_{\text{bad}}\\
&+\underbrace{\lambda_{q+1}\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\Psi^{1*}((\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})^{\prime}k)+Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\Psi^{1*}((\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})^{\prime}k)}_{\text{good}_{c}}\\
&+\underbrace{\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})\nabla\left[\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\right]+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})\nabla\left[Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\right]}_{\text{bad}}\\
\end{split}

where we used the identity in (
A.8
) in addition to the cancellations (
8.13
) above.

We now collect the terms in
T_{1},\ T_{2}
with the same geometries and prove bounds using the inductive assumptions on
F_{k}
and the Standing Assumptions
2
on
\sigma_{k+1}
.

\text{good}_{c}
-geometry.
The new term with
\text{good}_{c}
geometry is given by:

\begin{split}\text{good}_{c}&=(\sigma_{k+1}\cdot\nabla a_{2,k})\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)-\lambda_{q+1}(Y_{k}\cdot\sigma_{k+1}^{o})\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)\\
&-\left[a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]\Psi^{1*}((\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})^{\prime}k)\\
&+\lambda_{q+1}\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\Psi^{1*}((\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})^{\prime}k)+Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\Psi^{1*}((\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})^{\prime}k)\\
&=(\sigma_{k+1}\cdot\nabla a_{2,k})\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\\
&-\left[a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]\Psi^{1*}((\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})^{\prime}k)\\
&+\lambda_{q+1}\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\Psi^{1*}((\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime\prime})k)+Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\Psi^{1*}((\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})^{\prime}k)\\
&=[\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla a_{2,k}]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}k)+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}[(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\cdot\nabla a_{2,k}]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}\varphi_{k+1}k)\\
&-\left[a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]\Psi^{1*}((\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})^{\prime}k)\\
&+\lambda_{q+1}\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\Psi^{1*}((\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime\prime})k)+Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\Psi^{1*}((\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})^{\prime}k)\\
&\simeq a_{2,k+1}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k+1}k)\end{split}

and we have can bound:

\begin{split}||a_{2,k+1}||_{r}&\lesssim||\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla a_{2,k}||_{r}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\cdot\nabla a_{2,k}||_{r}+||a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r}\\
&+\lambda_{q+1}||\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{r}+||Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)||_{r}.\end{split}

We remark that terms with bad geometry can become
\text{good}_{c}
and might gain a
\lambda_{q+1}
, and when this happens, the slow coefficients are not hit by derivatives. This, together with the fact that for
k=0
we have
a_{2}=0
, explains why the loss function is shifted for the
\text{good}_{c}
geometry. Moreover, this loss is incurred only on top of a better estimate; see the inductive assumptions on
A_{3,k,r}
. We now provide bounds on the most relevant terms and, afterwards, state the full bound.

\begin{split}||\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla a_{2,k}||_{r}&\lesssim||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||a_{2,k}||_{1}+||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{0}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{r}||a_{2,k}||_{1}\\
&+||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{0}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||a_{2,k}||_{r+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}L^{\varsigma}(r)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{2,k,1}+\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{2,k,r+1}\\
&\lesssim A_{2,k+1,r}\ ,\\
\lambda_{q+1}||\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{r}&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||Y_{k}||_{0}+\lambda_{q+1}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{0}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{r}||Y_{k}||_{0}\\
&+\lambda_{q+1}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{0}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||Y_{k}||_{r}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{r}L^{\varsigma}(r)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{3,k,0}+\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{3,k,r}\\
&\lesssim A_{2,k+1,r}\ ,\\
\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\cdot\nabla a_{2,k}||_{r}&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r+1}||\Psi^{1*}\nu||_{0}||a_{2,k}||_{1}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{1}||\Psi^{1*}\nu||_{r}||a_{2,k}||_{1}\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{1}||\Psi^{1*}\nu||_{0}||a_{2,k}||_{r+1}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q}^{r}L^{\varsigma}(r+1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{2,k,1}+\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q}^{r}L^{\varsigma}(r)L^{\varsigma}(1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{2,k,1}\\
&+\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}L^{\varsigma}(1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{2,k,r+1}\\
&\lesssim A_{2,k+1,r}\end{split}

where we used

L(r)L^{\varsigma}(r^{\prime})\leq L(r+r^{\prime})\ \text{ and }\ \frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}L^{\varsigma}(r+1)\leq L^{\varsigma}(r)

and we conclude that:

||a_{2,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim A_{2,k+1,r}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-(k+1).

Commuting space and time derivatives in the formulas above, one can also show:

||\partial_{t}^{j}a_{2,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim A_{2,k+1,r,j}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-(k+1)-j.

good-geometry.
The new term with good geometry is given by:

\begin{split}\text{good}&=(\sigma_{k+1}^{o}\cdot\nabla a_{1,k})\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\nu)-(a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1})\Psi^{*}(\phi_{1,k}^{\prime}\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\Psi^{1*}\nu\cdot\left(\nabla a_{1,k}\times\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right)\right]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\\
&=(\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla a_{1,k})\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime}\phi_{1,k}\nu)-(a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1})\Psi^{*}(\phi_{1,k}^{\prime}\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\Psi^{1*}\nu\cdot\left(\nabla a_{1,k}\times\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right)\right]\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k}\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\\
&\simeq a_{1,k+1}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{1,k+1}\nu).\end{split}

Note that no indexes 2 and 3 appear. This means that if a term had a bad or
\text{good}_{c}
geometry stays that way. In fact, good terms stay good. This is why we can differentiate the loss function and keep different bounds on the terms with good geometry. By construction, we have:

||a_{1,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim||\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla a_{1,k}||_{r}+||a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\Psi^{1*}\nu\cdot\left(\nabla a_{1,k}\times\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right)||_{r}

and we can estimate as above using the properties of the loss functions:

\begin{split}||a_{1,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r}&\lesssim||a_{1,k}||_{r}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{1}+||a_{1,k}||_{0}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{r}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{1}\\
&+||a_{1,k}||_{0}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}L^{\varsigma}(1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{1,k,r}+\lambda_{q}^{r+1}L^{\varsigma}(r)L^{\varsigma}(1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{1,k,0}\\
&+\lambda_{q}^{r+1}L^{\varsigma}(r+1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{1,k,0}\\
&\lesssim A_{1,k+1,r}\ ,\\
||\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla a_{1,k}||_{r}&\lesssim||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||a_{1,k}||_{1}+||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{0}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{r}||a_{1,k}||_{1}\\
&+||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{0}||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||a_{1,k}||_{r+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r}L^{\varsigma}(r)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{1,k,1}+\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{1,k,r+1}\\
&\lesssim A_{1,k+1,r}\ ,\\
\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\Psi^{1*}\nu\cdot\left(\nabla a_{1,k}\times\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right)||_{r}&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\Psi^{1*}\nu||_{r}||a_{1,k}||_{1}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{1}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\Psi^{1*}\nu||_{0}||a_{1,k}||_{r+1}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{1}\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\Psi^{1*}\nu||_{0}||a_{1,k}||_{1}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r+1}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q}^{r}L^{\varsigma}(r)L^{\varsigma}(1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{1,k,1}+\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}L^{\varsigma}(1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{1,k,r+1}\\
&+\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q}^{r}L^{\varsigma}(r+1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}A_{1,k,1}\\
&\lesssim A_{1,k+1,r}\end{split}

and we conclude that:

||a_{1,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim A_{1,k+1,r}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-(k+1).

Commuting space and time derivatives in the formulas above, one can also show:

||\partial_{t}^{j}a_{1,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim A_{1,k+1,r,j}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-(k+1)-j

bad-geometry:
the new term with bad geometry is given by:

\begin{split}\text{bad}&=-(\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k}k)\cdot\sigma_{k+1}^{c})\nabla a_{2,k}+\left[\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}k)\times Y_{k}\right]\times\left[\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{k+1}\nu)\right]+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k})\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\sigma_{k+1}\\
&-\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})\nabla\left[a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]\\
&+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})\nabla\left[\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\right]+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})\nabla\left[Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{2,k}\varphi_{k+1})[(\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1})\nabla a_{2,k}]+\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}\varphi_{k+1})\left[\Psi^{1*}k\times Y_{k}\right]\times\left[\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu\right]\\
&+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})[\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta]+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})\left[\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\left(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu\right)\right]\\
&-\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})\nabla\left[a_{2,k}\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]\\
&+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})\nabla\left[\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\right]+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})\nabla\left[Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\right]\\
&=\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{3,k}^{\prime}\varphi_{k+1})\left[\Psi^{1*}k\times Y_{k}\right]\times\left[\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu\right]\\
&+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})[\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta]+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})\left[\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\left(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu\right)\right]\\
&-\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\varphi_{k+1}\phi_{2,k})a_{2,k}\nabla\left[\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]\\
&+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1}^{\prime})\nabla\left[\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\right]+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k}\varphi_{k+1})\nabla\left[Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)\right]\\
&\simeq\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k+1})Y_{k+1}\end{split}

where we used the identity in (
A.8
). By construction, we have:

\begin{split}||Y_{k+1}||_{r}&\lesssim||(\Psi^{1*}k\times Y_{k})\times(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)||_{r}+||\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times\varsigma_{k+1}\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{r}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\mathrm{curl}\ Y_{k}\times(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)||_{r}\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||a_{2,k}\nabla\left[\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right]||_{r}+||\nabla\left[\varsigma_{k+1}Y_{k}\cdot\Psi^{2*}\zeta\right]||_{r}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||\nabla[Y_{k}\cdot(\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\times\Psi^{1*}\nu)]||_{r}.\end{split}

The key observation here is that the additional
1/\lambda_{q+1}
smallness compensates for the presence of a
\lambda_{q+1}
in the terms switching from
\text{good}_{g}\mapsto\text{bad}
geometry, namely the one with index
2
. This allows us to close the induction argument. We give an example computation of this fact for the term:

T=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}a_{2,k}\nabla\left[\Psi^{2*}\zeta\cdot\nabla\varsigma_{k+1}\right],

the remaining ones can be addressed using the same ideas

\begin{split}||T||_{r}&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||a_{2,k}||_{r}(||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{1}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{1}+||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{2})\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}||a_{2,k}||_{0}(||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{r+1}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{1}+||\Psi^{2*}\zeta||_{0}||\varsigma_{k+1}||_{r+2})\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}A_{2,k,r}\lambda_{q}^{2}\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}(L^{\varsigma}(1)^{2}+L^{\varsigma}(0)L^{\varsigma}(2))+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}A_{2,k,0}(\lambda_{q}^{r+2}L^{\varsigma}(r+1)L^{\varsigma}(1)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}+\lambda_{q}^{r+2}L^{\varsigma}(r+2)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1})\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{r+k-1}L(r+k-1)A_{3}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\right]\lambda_{q}^{2}L^{\varsigma}(2)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\left[\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{k-1}L(k-1)A_{3}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\right]\lambda_{q}^{r+2}L^{\varsigma}(r+2)\bar{\varsigma}_{k+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{r+k+1}L(r+k+1)A_{3}\prod_{i=1}^{k+1}\bar{\varsigma}_{i}\\
&=A_{3,k+1,r}\end{split}

where we used the properties of the loss functions as above, to handle the products. We conclude that:

||Y_{k+1}||_{r}\lesssim A_{3,k+1,r}\ \text{ for }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-(k+1).

Commuting space and time derivatives in the formulas above, one can also show:

||\partial_{t}^{j}Y_{k+1}||_{r}\lesssim A_{3,k+1,r,j}\ \text{ for }\ j=0,1,2\ \text{ and }\ 0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-(k+1)-j.

Conclusion.
Gathering everything we have:

F_{k+1}\simeq a_{1,k+1}\Psi^{*}(\phi_{1,k+1}\nu)+a_{2,k+1}\Psi^{1*}(\phi_{2,k+1}k)+\Psi^{*}(\phi_{3,k+1})Y_{k+1}

with

•

Fast Coefficients (without
\lambda_{q+1}
rescaling):
||\phi_{i,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim 1
for
r\geq 0,i=1,2,3
.

•

Slow Coefficients:

\begin{split}&||\partial_{t}^{j}a_{i,k+1}||_{r}\lesssim A_{i,k+1,r,j}\ \text{ for }\ i=1,2\\
&||\partial_{t}^{j}Y_{k+1}||_{r}\lesssim A_{3,k+1,r,j}\end{split}

for
j=0,1,2
and
0\leq r\leq\bar{N}-(k+1)-j
.

Inspection of the proof above shows that we can set

(8.15)

C=n_{1}n_{2}\bar{C},\ C^{\prime}=\bar{C},\ C^{\prime\prime}=n_{1}

where
n_{1}
is the value of the implicit constant in the interpolation inequality in Lemma
B.1
for
r=\bar{N}
,
n_{2}
the largest number of terms in the definitions of the new slow coefficients.

Alfvén transport.
The transport estimates follow from the first part of the lemma proved above. Indeed, the identity (
A.12
) gives:

\left(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{z^{\pm}}\right)\sigma_{i}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathrm{curl}\left[\mathcal{A}^{\pm}(\varsigma_{i})\Psi^{1*}(\varphi_{i}\nu)\right],

which shows that the Lie derivative of
\sigma_{i}
still has the same structure as
\sigma_{i}
and with the help of the identity (
A.17
) we obtain:

(8.16)
